# PH — top-down Minto pyramid

Built by scripts/minto_pass.py from the grouped classification. Three rules (Minto pp. 9-11): each summary states the idea of its group, members are the same kind of idea, and they are in a stated order.

## Introduction

**Situation.** Physics claims range from everyday mechanics to the quantum, the cosmic and the collective behaviour of matter.

**Complication.** Source schemes group topics by catalogue cells and mixed constants, so the same regime is scattered across many groups.

**Question.** How is everything in physics divided?

**Answer.** Physics divides by the governing constants that set the scale of a claim into classical motion and fields, quantum systems, gravitation and the cosmos, matter in equilibrium, and matter out of equilibrium.

## Governing thought

Physics divides by the governing constants that set the scale of a claim into classical motion and fields, quantum systems, gravitation and the cosmos, matter in equilibrium, and matter out of equilibrium.

## Key line

Group of regimes. Basis: kinds. Dimension: the governing constants of the claim (which of c, ħ, G and N set its scale). Order: structure. The regimes run from fewest constants to most, then the N axis of matter in and out of equilibrium.

1. Classical motion and fields is the regime where claims rest on classical laws with no quantum constant, covering mechanics, fluids, fields, optics, beams and the teaching of physics. `PH.K1`
2. Quantum systems is the regime where Planck's constant ħ sets the scale, covering particles, nuclei, atoms, molecules and quantum light. `PH.K2`
3. Gravitation and the cosmos is the regime where G, with c, sets the scale, covering gravity, planets, stars, galaxies and the Earth and space environment. `PH.K3`
4. Matter in equilibrium is the regime where particle number N and thermal energy set the scale of settled phases, covering condensed matter, surfaces, soft matter and energy materials. `PH.K4`
5. Matter out of equilibrium is the regime where driven collective behaviour of N particles sets the scale, covering plasma, transport, networks and nonlinear dynamics. `PH.K5`

## Groups

### 1. Classical motion and fields is the regime where claims rest on classical laws with no quantum constant, covering mechanics, fluids, fields, optics, beams and the teaching of physics.

- **Classical mechanics and core fluid flows describe the motion of bodies and continua through Newtonian theory and dimensionless numbers.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From solid mechanics to core flows to specialised and applied flows._
  - **Classical mechanics and elasticity are kinds of Newtonian and continuum-solid problems whose scale is set by N or by no constant.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from few-body systems to deformable solids._
    - **Classical mechanics topics are kinds of Newtonian systems whose scale is set by N or none of c, ħ, G.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_
      - **Broad physics fields outside classical mechanics proper, covering fluid-scale devices, sound and thermal transport.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by position in the PACS outline._
        - Nanofluidic devices
        - Acoustics (for more detailed headings, see Appendix to section 43)
        - Heat transfer
      - **Classical mechanics of discrete systems and its formalisms, whose scale is set by Newtonian quantities rather than c, ħ or G.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by position in the PACS outline._
        - Classical mechanics of discrete systems
        - Formalisms in classical mechanics
        - Newtonian mechanics
      - **Computational methods used in classical mechanics, including variational, perturbative and geometrical techniques.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by position in the PACS outline._
        - Computational methods in classical mechanics (see also 02.70.-c Computational techniques in mathematical methods in physics)
        - Variational and optimization methods
        - Perturbation and fractional calculus methods
        - Geometrical and tensorial methods
      - **General dynamical principles of classical mechanics relate forces, momentum and energy without any scale-setting constant.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from formalism to forces and conserved quantities._
        - Lagrangian and Hamiltonian mechanics
        - Forces and torques
        - Momentum conservation
        - Mechanical energy, work, and power
        - Energy conservation
      - **Rigid-body topics describe how extended solid bodies rotate and translate under classical mechanics.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from rotational dynamics to kinematics and gyroscopic motion._
        - Rotational dynamics
        - Dynamics and kinematics of rigid bodies
        - Translation kinematics
        - Rotational kinematics
        - Rigid body and gyroscope motion
    - **Mechanical systems are kinds ordered by the number of bodies, from few bodies to many grains to a continuum.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_
      - **Single-body and general motion topics are set by classical dynamics alone, with N equal to one or a few bodies and no scale from c, ħ or G.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Weakest dependence on N: one or a few bodies, no fundamental-constant scale._
        - Ballistics (projectiles; rockets)
        - Robotics
        - General motion
      - **Particle-system topics treat several interacting bodies, so N enters as a modest number, and only celestial mechanics brings in G.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Moderate dependence on N: few to many interacting bodies, with G relevant only for celestial mechanics._
        - Dynamics and kinematics of a particle and a system of particles
        - Few- and many-body systems
        - Celestial mechanics (see also 95.10.Ce in fundamental astronomy)
        - Collisions
        - Concepts & principles
      - **Granular-system topics concern very many grains, so the scale of the claim is set by N with no role for c, ħ or G.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Strong dependence on N: very large numbers of grains determine collective behaviour._
        - Granular systems (see also 05.65.+b Self-organized systems)
        - Static sandpiles; granular compaction
        - Avalanches
        - Granular flow: mixing, segregation and stratification
        - Pattern formation
      - **Continuum-mechanics topics take the limit of N going to infinity, so the scale is set by material parameters instead of by c, ħ or G.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Strongest dependence on N: the continuum limit where individual bodies are no longer resolved._
        - Continuum mechanics of solids (see also 83.10.Ff in rheology; 91.60.Ba Elasticity, fracture, and flow; 91.45.Ga Dynamics and mechanics of tectonics; 91.55.Ln Kinematics of crustal and mantle deformation in geophysics)
        - Computational methods in continuum mechanics (see also 02.70.-c Computational techniques; simulations, in mathematical methods in physics)
        - Variational and optimizational methods
        - Perturbation and complex analysis methods
        - Static elasticity
      - **Theoretical studies of static elasticity and granular traffic models are idealised descriptions whose scale comes from N or material constants only.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Strong to continuum dependence on N, as idealised models of many-body systems._
        - Theoretical studies
        - Granular models of complex systems; traffic flow
    - **Elasticity and structural topics are kinds of deformable-solid problems in continuum mechanics.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_
      - **Elasticity and vibration topics describe how deformable solids respond to thermal, electromagnetic and dynamic loading and carry waves.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS numbering within section 46._
        - Thermoelasticity and electromagnetic elasticity (electroelasticity, magnetoelasticity)
        - Vibrations and mechanical waves (see also 43.40.+s Structural acoustics and vibration; 62.30.+d Mechanical and elastic waves; vibrations in mechanical properties of solids)
        - Mechanical wave propagation (including diffraction, scattering, and dispersion)
        - Resonance, damping, and dynamic stability
        - Aeroelasticity and hydroelasticity
      - **Structural applications of continuum mechanics treat specific engineered forms such as beams, plates, shells, membranes, rods and strings.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS numbering within 46.70._
        - Application of continuum mechanics to structures
        - Beams, plates, and shells
        - Membranes, rods, and strings
        - Other structures
  - **Laminar, turbulent and vortex flows are kinds of core fluid-dynamics regimes together with their theory and computation.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from theory to laminar flow to turbulence to vortices._
    - **Fluid dynamics topics cover flow regimes, numerical methods, phenomena and mathematical models of fluids.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from regimes to computation._
      - **Continuum flow regimes are characterized by how fluid motion behaves in smooth, bounded, wavy, or nonlinear conditions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Laminar flows
        - Boundary layers
        - Nonlinear dynamics in fluids
        - Waves and free surface flows
      - **Numerical methods for fluid problems discretize boundaries or average turbulent equations to compute flows.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Reynolds-averaged Navier Stokes
        - Boundary element method
      - **Fluid phenomena and properties are physical behaviours and kinds of fluid that classical fluid dynamics studies.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from material properties to flow phenomena._
        - Rheological properties
        - Buoyancy
        - Convection
        - Binary fluids
      - **Computational and mathematical methods are the equations and numerical techniques used to model fluid flow.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from governing equation to numerical techniques._
        - Euler equation
        - Direct numerical simulations
        - Volume of fluid method
    - **Fluid theory and computation topics are kinds of formal and numerical approaches to fluid motion.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_
      - **General fluid dynamics theory covers the field itself and its mathematical, Hamiltonian and dynamical-systems formulations.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS numbering from the section heading downward._

        - Dynamical systems methods
      - **The governing equations of fluid motion are the conservation laws, constitutive relations and the Navier-Stokes equations.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS numbering._
        - Conservation laws and constitutive relations

      - **Computational methods in fluid dynamics are numerical discretization schemes for solving the flow equations.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS numbering._

        - Finite element methods
      - **Numerical methods for simulating fluid flow, each discretizing the problem in a different way.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered by structure_

        - Spectral methods
        - Molecular dynamics methods
        - Lattice gas
        - Multi-scale methods
    - **Laminar flow topics are kinds of orderly flows classified by regime, geometry and stability.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_
      - **Broad categories of laminar flow defined by the dominant physical regime or idealization of the fluid.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered by structure_

      - **Laminar flows defined by the geometry or confinement in which they occur, such as layers, wakes, jets, channels and films.**

      - **Instabilities that arise from material or interfacial properties of the fluid such as surface tension, viscosity, density contrast and phase change.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from bulk inviscid and viscous instabilities to interface-driven ones._

      - **Instabilities that arise from the geometry and structure of the flow itself, such as shear, boundary layers, rotation and nonlinear bifurcation.**

        - Nonlinearity, bifurcation, and symmetry breaking

    - **Turbulence topics are kinds of approaches and settings from onset to developed and wall-bounded or free flows.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_

    - Applied classical electromagnetism (for submillimeter wave, microwave, and radiowave instruments and equipment, see 07.57.-c)
    - Electrostatics; Poisson and Laplace equations, boundary-value problems
    - Magnetostatics; magnetic shielding, magnetic induction, boundary-value problems
    - Electromagnetic wave propagation; radiowave propagation (for light propagation, see 42.25.Bs; for electromagnetic waves in plasma, see 52.35.Hr; for atmospheric, ionospheric, and magnetospheric propagation, see 92.60.Ta, 94.20.Bb, and 94.30.Tz, respectively; see also 94.05.Pt Wave/wave, wave/particle interactions, in space plasma physics)
  - **Classical optics topics are kinds of light behavior and media described by rays, waves and materials rather than quantum constants.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from ray optics to wave optics to media._
    - **Geometric and general optics topics treat light as beams and rays without needing quantum constants.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general to geometric optics._
      - **General optics topics are the broad fields of beam optics and light propagation that frame the more specific beam topics.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from broad optics to geometrical optics._
        - Optics (for optical properties of gases, see 51.70.+f; for optical properties of bulk materials and thin films, see 78.20.-e; for x-ray optics, see 41.50.+h)
        - Geometrical optics
        - Beam optics (see also 07.77.Ka Charged-particle beam sources and detectors in instruments; 29.27.-a Beams in particle accelerators)
      - **Geometric optics topics treat light as rays and wave fronts, ignoring wave effects such as diffraction.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from basic ray tracing to design and aberration analysis._
        - Wave fronts and ray tracing
        - Optical system design
        - Aberrations
      - **Wave optics topics treat light as a wave whose propagation, transmission and absorption are described directly.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the general wave description to specific propagation effects._
        - Wave optics
        - Wave propagation, transmission and absorption [see also 41.20.Jb—in electromagnetism; for propagation in atmosphere, see 42.68.Ay; see also 52.40.Db Electromagnetic (nonlaser) radiation interactions with plasma and 52.38-r Laser-plasma interactions—in plasma physics]
    - **Wave-optical phenomena and imaging cover classical wave behavior of light, scaled by c and wavelength.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from wave phenomena to imaging and holography._
      - **Classical wave phenomena of light, each governed by the speed of light c and the wavelength rather than by ħ, G or N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Kinds are listed in structural order without ranking by degree._
        - Wave propagation in random media
        - Diffraction and scattering
        - Edge and boundary effects; reflection and refraction
        - Interference
        - Polarization
      - **Wave-optics phenomena and methods describe how light's wave nature, set by c and ħ, shapes the optical field.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered by structure of the PACS 42.25 and 42.30 headings_
        - Coherence
        - Birefringence
        - Fourier optics
        - Speckle and moiré patterns
        - Phase retrieval
      - **Imaging and processing topics concern forming, transferring, and interpreting images by optical or computational means.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered by structure of the PACS 42.30 headings_
        - Imaging and optical processing
        - Modulation and optical transfer functions
        - Pattern recognition
        - Computer vision; robotic vision
        - Image forming and processing
      - **Classical wave-optical imaging and hologram types whose scale is set only by c through the wavelength, with no ħ, G or N entering.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Image reconstruction; tomography
        - Holography
        - Holographic optical elements; holographic gratings
        - Computer-generated holograms
        - Volume holograms
      - **Hologram recording, readout, measurement and application topics that remain classical, so only c fixes the scale of the claim.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Hologram recording and readout methods (see also 42.70.Ln Holographic recording materials; optical storage media)
        - Holographic interferometry; other holographic techniques (for interferometers, see 07.60.Ly in instruments)
        - Diffraction efficiency, resolution, and other hologram characteristics
        - Applications
        - Quantum optics (for lasers, see 42.55.-f and 42.60.-v; see also 42.65.-k Nonlinear optics; 03.65.-w Quantum mechanics)
    - **Optics and photonics topics are grouped as kinds of light phenomena, media and nonlinear effects.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_
      - **Parametric processes are nonlinear optical effects in which light is converted or amplified through second-order wave mixing.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered as in the PACS 42.65 listing._
        - Parametric down conversion and production of entangled photons (see also 42.50.Dv Quantum state engineering and measurements; for optical parametric oscillators and amplifiers, see 42.65.Yj)
        - Optical parametric oscillators and amplifiers (see also 42.65.Lm Parametric down conversion and production of entangled photons)
      - **Nonlinear dynamics and pulse phenomena cover time-dependent, switching, soliton and guided-wave behavior of intense light.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered as in the PACS 42.65 listing._
        - Optical bistability, multistability, and switching, including local field effects (see also 42.60.Gd Q-switching; 42.79.Ta Optical computers, logic elements, interconnects, switches; neural networks)
        - Ultrafast processes; optical pulse generation and pulse compression (for ultrafast spectroscopy, see 78.47.J-; for ultrafast magnetization dynamics, see 75.78.Jp)
        - Dynamics of nonlinear optical systems; optical instabilities, optical chaos and complexity, and optical spatio-temporal dynamics
        - Optical solitons; nonlinear guided waves (for solitons in fibers, see 42.81.Dp)
        - Nonlinear waveguides
      - **Eye-related topics treat the anatomy, optics and physiology of the visual organ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered as in the PACS 42.66 listing._
        - Physiological optics (see also 87.19.lt Sensory systems: visual, auditory, tactile, taste, and olfaction)
        - Anatomy and optics of eye
        - Physiology of eye; optic-nerve structure and function (see also 87.19.lt Sensory systems: visual, auditory, tactile, taste, and olfaction)
      - **Vision topics concern how the eye and brain detect, adapt to, scale, and interpret light and color.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from detection and adaptation to scales and perception._
        - Vision: light detection, adaptation, and discrimination
        - Color vision: color detection, adaptation, and discrimination
        - Scales for light and color detection
        - Psychophysics of vision, visual perception; binocular vision
      - **Atmospheric propagation topics concern how light travels through the air and is attenuated or disturbed along the path.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general propagation to specific path effects._
        - Atmospheric and ocean optics
        - Propagation, transmission, attenuation, and radiative transfer (see also 92.60.Ta Electromagnetic wave propagation)
        - Atmospheric turbulence effects (see also 92.60.hk Convection, turbulence, and diffusion in meteorology)
    - **Atmospheric optics and optical material topics are kinds of natural and engineered media that transmit light.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_
      - **Atmospheric constituent topics concern how gases, clouds, and aerosols absorb and scatter light.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from gases to clouds to aerosols._
        - Spectral absorption by atmospheric gases (see also 92.60.Vb Radiative processes, solar radiation in meteorology)
        - Effects of clouds and water; ice crystal phenomena (see also 92.60.Jq Water in the atmosphere; 92.60.Nv Cloud physics and chemistry in meteorology)
        - Effects of aerosols (see also 92.60.Mt Particles and aerosols in meteorology; 92.20.Bk Aerosols in chemical and biological oceanography; 91.40.Dr Atmospheric effects in volcanology)
      - **Atmospheric and ocean optics covers how light propagates, scatters and forms images through natural media.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS code within 42.68._
        - Effects of air pollution (see also 92.60.Sz Air quality and air pollution in meteorology; 92.10.Xc Ocean fog in oceanography)
        - Scattering, polarization (see also 92.60.Ta Electromagnetic wave propagation and 92.60.Vb Radiative processes, solar radiation in meteorology)
        - Image transmission and formation
        - Remote sensing; LIDAR and adaptive systems
        - Ocean optics (see also 92.05.Hj Physical and chemical properties of sea water in oceanography)
      - **Glass, crystal and organic optical materials are passive solids and liquids chosen for their transmission and structural properties.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS code within 42.70._
        - Glasses, quartz
        - Liquid crystals (for structure of liquid crystals, see 61.30.-v)
        - Polymers and organics
        - Infrared transmitting materials
        - Optical materials (see also 81.05.-t Specific materials: fabrication, treatment, testing and analysis)
      - **Active and responsive optical materials change or generate light through gain, recording, nonlinearity or band structure.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS code within 42.70._
        - Light-sensitive materials
        - Laser materials
        - Holographic recording materials; optical storage media
        - Nonlinear optical crystals (see also 77.84.-s Dielectric, piezoelectric, and ferroelectric materials)
        - Other nonlinear optical materials; photorefractive and semiconductor materials
      - **Photonic bandgap materials control light propagation through periodic structure rather than bulk composition.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS code within 42.70._
        - Photonic bandgap materials (for photonic crystal lasers, see 42.55.Tv)
        - Optical elements, devices, and systems (for integrated optics, see 42.82.-m; for fiber optics, see 42.81.-i)
  - **Classical physics subfields are the kinds of branches of physics that rest on classical, non-quantum laws.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the general field to its subfields._
    - Classical physics
    - Acoustics and acoustical devices; waves
    - Classical and physical optics
    - Electrostatics and electrodynamics
  - **Wave and field phenomena in which c and ħ set the scale of light and magnetism.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from optical to magnetic phenomena._
    - Optical Phenomena
    - Magnetic Phenomena
    - Physics
    - Time
    - Motion
  - **Fluid and continuum phenomena whose scale is governed by many particles (N) and flow dynamics.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from general flow to specific equations._
    - Hydrodynamics
    - Lagrangian flow field
    - Navier-Stokes equation
    - Filtration
    - Mechanical Phenomena
- **Charged-particle beams and accelerators are relativistic machines and radiation processes whose scale is set largely by c.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From radiation and beam species to handling to machines._
  - **Beam radiation, species and handling cover how charged particles emit, are classed, and are steered as a beam.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. From emission to species to handling._
    - **Radiation topics cover emission of electromagnetic radiation by moving charges, from the general heading to synchrotron, Cherenkov and free-electron-laser sources.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the general electromagnetism heading to specific radiation types._
      - Electromagnetism; electron and ion optics
      - Radiation by moving charges
      - Synchrotron radiation (for synchrotron radiation instrumentation, see 07.85.Qe)
      - Cherenkov radiation
      - Free-electron lasers (see also 52.59.Rz Free-electron devices—in plasma physics)
    - **Beam species are distinguished by the charge and speed of the particles, with the relativistic limit set by c.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the general beam class to specific species._
      - Charged-particle beams
      - Positive-ion beams
      - Negative-ion beams
      - Electron and positron beams
      - Relativistic electron and positron beams
    - **Advanced acceleration and radiation phenomena are ways a charged particle gains energy or emits light, with scales set by c and ħ.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from radiation to acceleration schemes._
      - Transition radiation
      - Laser-driven acceleration (see also 52.38.-r Laser-plasma interactions in plasma physics)
      - Other advanced accelerator concepts
    - **Beam handling steps are the successive operations that inject, shape, transport and clean up a beam.**
      _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered along the beamline from injection to collimation._
      - Particle beam extraction, beam injection
      - Particle beam shaping, beam splitting
      - Particle beam transport
      - Particle beam collimators, monochromators
    - **Beam elements and diagnostics are the devices and measures that focus a beam, limit its aberrations, and measure its profile and intensity.**
      _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from focusing devices to measurement tools._
      - Particle beam focusing and bending magnets, wiggler magnets, and quadrupoles (see also 07.55.Db Generation of magnetic fields; magnets in instruments; for superconducting magnets, see 84.71.Ba)
      - Electrostatic lenses, septa
      - Chromatic and geometrical aberrations
      - Particle beam profile, beam intensity
      - Particle beam analyzers, beam monitors, and Faraday cups
  - **Accelerator machines and facilities are the relativistic hardware used to produce, accelerate and store beams.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. From general beam topics to machines to applications._
    - **Accelerator and synchrotron topics are relativistic machines whose scale is set largely by the speed of light c.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Grouped by accelerator subtopic, no ranking implied._
      - Synchrotrons and accelerators
      - Accelerators
      - Synchrotrons
      - Synchrotrons and accelerators not elsewhere classified
      - Advanced accelerator test facilities
    - **Beam and accelerator topics are kinds of subjects concerning the production, manipulation, and motion of particle beams.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the broad field to specific topics._
      - Accelerators & Beams
      - Beam techniques
      - Particle-beam sources
      - Beam dynamics
    - **Accelerator-related concepts cover the machines and facilities used to accelerate and store particles.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from device type to facility._
      - Accelerators & storage rings
      - Particle accelerator facilities
    - Accelerator applications
    - **Fluid-related physical systems are the kinds of specialized fluid media and accelerator hardware outside conventional flow engineering.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, with the hardware item first and the fluid medium second._
      - Accelerator subsystems
      - Magnetic fluids
- **Physics as a classical human and methodological activity covers teaching, people, mathematical methods, measurement and general phenomena.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From education to people to methods to general phenomena._
  - **Physics education covers the teaching, learning, settings and policies of the discipline.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. From general education to settings and policy._
    - **Education and general physics topics concern how physics is taught, studied and organized as a discipline.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed from general physics to teaching materials and audiences._
      - General Physics
      - Research methodology
      - K-12 students
      - Physics Education Research
      - Instructional materials development
    - **Physics education concepts cover the people, settings, and policies involved in teaching and learning physics.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from people to settings and policies._
      - Learning environment
      - Educational policy
      - Faculty
      - Lower undergraduate students
      - Assessment
    - Instructional strategies
    - Undergraduate research
    - Scientific reasoning & problem solving
  - **Students, teachers and the attitudes of the physics community describe the people who learn and teach physics.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. From students to teachers to attitudes._
    - Diversity & inclusion
    - Student preparation
    - Upper undergraduate students
    - Epistemology, attitudes, & beliefs
    - **Field-driven manipulation topics and the people who study them fall outside the instrument and flow-regime groups.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - Electrokinetic flows
      - Acoustic tweezers
      - Graduate students
  - **Methods, measurement and general phenomena are the tools and broad headings through which physics claims are made.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. From mathematical methods to measurement to broad headings._
    - **Non-physical, mathematical and general topics are kinds of subject matter whose scale involves no physical constant or only a simple one.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general and educational material to mathematics and methods._
      - **Literature and community nodes of the general section are kinds of material whose scale involves no physical constant.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - **The outer container nodes of the general PACS section, from the root down to the literature branch.**
          _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from outermost container to the literature branch._
          - GENERAL
          - Communication, education, history, and philosophy
          - Physics literature and publications
        - **Announcements and organizational activities are the kinds of community events and news in physics.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Parent heading first, then its child activity types._
          - Announcements, news, and organizational activities
          - Announcements, news, and awards
          - Conferences, lectures, and institutes
          - Physics organizational activities
        - **Book-length and collected publications are kinds of physics literature issued as volumes or proceedings.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in PACS order of the volume-type entries._
          - Publications of lectures (advanced institutes, summer schools, etc.)
          - Conference proceedings
          - Monographs and collections
          - Handbooks, dictionaries, tables, and data compilations
          - Publications in electronic media (for the topic of electronic publishing, see 01.20.+x)
        - **Teaching-oriented publications are kinds of literature written for instruction in physics.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in PACS order of the teaching-related entries._
          - Physics laboratory manuals
          - Textbooks
          - Books of general interest to physics teachers
          - Secondary schools
        - **Review and commentary pieces are kinds of literature that survey, list, or comment on other work.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in PACS order of the review-type entries._
          - Surveys and tutorial papers; resource letters
          - Bibliographies
          - Book reviews
          - Editorials
      - **Education topics are kinds of teaching subject matter that carry no scale from c, ħ, G or N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - **Textbooks and schools are educational materials and institutions defined by the level of student they serve.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered from graduate to elementary level_
          - Undergraduate schools
          - Textbooks for graduates and researchers
          - Textbooks for undergraduates
          - Textbooks for students in grades 9-12
          - Textbooks for students in grades K-8
        - **General education and research topics concern the design, study and theory of physics education as a field.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered from general field to specific research areas_
          - Education
          - Course design and evaluation
          - Research in physics education
          - Curricula and evaluation
          - Learning theory and science teaching
        - **Teaching practice and training topics concern how teachers are prepared and how instruction and testing are carried out.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered from general training to specific methods_
          - Teacher training
          - Teaching methods and strategies
          - Theory of testing and techniques
          - Preservice training
          - Inservice training
        - **Educational aids and school-level categories cover the settings and tools used for science instruction in schools.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered from school settings to teaching aids_
          - Science in school
          - Elementary school
          - Secondary school
          - Educational aids
          - Audio and visual aids
      - **Educational technology and media topics are kinds of tools used to teach and communicate physics.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - **Computer use in education covers the ways computers and software are used in teaching and laboratories.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the classification listing._
          - Computers in education
          - Laboratory computer use (see also 01.50.Pa)
          - Instructional computer use
          - Computer software and software reviews
        - **Laboratory and demonstration work covers the apparatus, experiments and courses used for hands-on physics teaching.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the classification listing._
          - Demonstration experiments and apparatus
          - Laboratory experiments and apparatus (see also 01.50.Lc)
          - Laboratory course design, organization, and evaluation
        - **Audiovisual and artistic media are the sound, video and visual materials used to communicate physics.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the classification listing._
          - Audio devices
          - Films; electronic video devices
          - Posters, cartoons, art, etc
        - **Assessment, contests and classroom content covers testing, competitions, toy physics and errors in teaching materials.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the classification listing._
          - Techniques of testing
          - Physics tournaments and contests
          - Physics of toys
          - Errors in physics classroom materials
      - **Algebraic and group-theoretic topics are abstract mathematics with no physical constant setting their scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - **Logic and algebra topics are the foundational branches of set-theoretic and algebraic mathematics.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the classification listing._
          - Logic, set theory, and algebra
          - Logic and set theory
          - Algebraic structures and number theory
        - **Rings, knots and general methods are mathematical topics applied within physics.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the classification listing._
          - Mathematical methods in physics
          - Rings and algebras
          - Knot theory
        - **Linear and matrix algebra topics deal with vector spaces, tensors and matrices as constant-free mathematical tools.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from linear algebra to multilinear algebra to matrix theory._
          - Linear algebra
          - Multilinear algebra
          - Matrix theory
        - **Group-theoretic topics cover abstract groups, classical and Lie groups, their algebras, and quantum groups as symmetry structures.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general group theory to specific types of groups._
          - Group theory (for algebraic methods in quantum mechanics, see 03.65.Fd; for symmetries in elementary particle physics, see 11.30.-j)
          - General structures of groups
          - Classical groups
          - General properties, structure, and representation of Lie groups
          - Discrete subgroups of Lie groups
        - **Further group-theoretic topics concern Lie algebras and infinite-dimensional or quantum deformations of groups.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from Lie algebras to infinite-dimensional groups to quantum groups._
          - Lie algebras of Lie groups
          - Infinite-dimensional Lie groups
          - Quantum groups
      - **Analysis and equation topics are kinds of function-theoretic mathematics whose scale no constant sets.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - **Function theory and analysis topics cover measure, potential theory, complex analysis, special functions and differential equations.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general function theory to specific equation types._
          - Function theory, analysis
          - Measure and integration
          - Potential theory
          - Several complex variables and analytic spaces
          - Special functions
        - **Differential-equation and discrete-structure topics cover ordinary and partial equations, integrable systems, and combinatorics.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from ordinary equations to partial equations to discrete graph theory._
          - Ordinary differential equations
          - Integrable systems
          - Partial differential equations
          - Combinatorics; graph theory
        - **Equation, series and expansion methods are scale-free analysis tools that no physical constant sets.**
          _basis: kinds, order: degree. Least connected to c, ħ, G and N: purely formal analysis with no constant setting the scale._
          - Delay and functional equations
          - Sequences, series, and summability
          - Approximations and expansions
          - Fourier analysis
          - Bifurcation theory (see also 47.20.Ky in fluid dynamics)
        - **Harmonic, integral and operator methods are abstract analysis whose link to ħ arises only through quantum applications.**
          _basis: kinds, order: degree. Slightly more connected: operator and transform methods are the mathematical language of ħ-scale quantum theory._
          - Abstract harmonic analysis
          - Integral equations
          - Functional analysis
          - Operator theory
          - Integral transforms
        - **Operational, variational, control and inverse methods together with geometry-and-topology as a field are general frameworks that connect to physical constants only through the problem they are applied to.**
          _basis: kinds, order: degree. Intermediate: variational and control frameworks apply across c, ħ, G scales depending on the problem._
          - Operational calculus
          - Calculus of variations
          - Control theory
          - Inverse problems
          - Geometry, differential geometry, and topology (see also section 04 Relativity and gravitation)
    - **Formal geometry, numerical methods and simulation are kinds of mathematical and computational approaches to physics problems.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from formal mathematics to numerical and simulation methods._
      - **Geometry, topology and probability topics are kinds of formal mathematics, with c and G entering only for specific geometries.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from fully constant-free to those where c and G set the scale of curvature._
        - **Topology and geometry of spaces study the shape and connectivity of manifolds and related spaces without any physical constant.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general topology to manifold structures._
          - General topology
          - Algebraic topology
          - Manifolds and cell complexes
          - Complex manifolds
          - Global differential geometry
        - **Global analysis, singularity theory and geometric mechanics apply analytic methods to manifolds and mechanical structures in a constant-free way.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from analysis to singularities to mechanics._
          - Global analysis and analysis on manifolds
          - Singularity theory (see also 05.45.-a Nonlinear dynamics and chaos)
          - Geometric mechanics (see also 45.20.Jj in formalisms in classical mechanics)
        - **Probability theory and stochastic processes describe random phenomena abstractly, independent of physical scales.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general probability to specific process types._
          - Probability theory, stochastic processes, and statistics (see also section 05 Statistical physics, thermodynamics, and nonlinear dynamical systems)
          - Probability theory
          - Stochastic processes
          - Stochastic analysis
          - Markov processes
        - **Statistical and decision-theoretic methods treat distributions, multivariate data and inference as formal tools with no intrinsic physical scale.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from decision theory to distributions to inference._
          - Decision theory and game theory
          - Distribution theory and Monte Carlo studies
          - Multivariate analysis
          - Inference methods
        - **Specific geometries are the structures nearest to relativity and gravitation, where c and G set the scale of curvature.**
          _basis: kinds, order: degree. Most connected to c and G: Riemannian and differential geometry underlie general relativity._
          - Euclidean and projective geometries
          - Convex sets and geometric inequalities
          - Noncommutative geometry
          - Classical differential geometry
          - Riemannian geometries
      - **Numerical methods are kinds of algorithms whose scale is set by the problem rather than by any constant.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - **Numerical approximation methods compute solutions algorithmically, with scale set by the problem rather than by c, ħ, G or N.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general numerical analysis to specific techniques._
          - Numerical approximation and analysis
          - Numerical simulation; solution of equations
          - Numerical linear algebra
        - **Numerical analysis algorithms approximate, differentiate, integrate and optimize functions without any physical constant setting their scale.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure_
          - Interpolation; curve fitting
          - Algorithms for functional approximation
          - Numerical differentiation and integration
          - Numerical optimization
          - Symbolic computation (computer algebra)
        - **Discretization methods turn differential and integral equations into finite problems, with the scale set by the problem and not by c, ħ, G or N.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure_
          - Ordinary and partial differential equations; boundary value problems
          - Integral and integrodifferential equations
          - Finite-difference methods
          - Finite-element and Galerkin methods
          - Spectral methods
      - **Simulation and field-theory topics are kinds of methods whose scale depends on N, ħ and c.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from simulations depending on N to field theories depending on ħ, c and G._
        - **Particle, statistical and Monte Carlo simulation methods depend on the particle number N and, for quantum Monte Carlo, on ħ.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure_
          - Collocation methods
          - Molecular dynamics and particle methods
          - Boundary-integral methods
          - General statistical methods
          - Quantum Monte Carlo methods
        - **Monte Carlo justifications and applications together with quantum, relativistic and classical field theories have scales set by ħ, c and N.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure_
          - Computational techniques; simulations (for quantum computation, see 03.67.Lx; for computational techniques extensively used in subdivisions of physics, see the appropriate section; for example, see 47.11.-j Computational methods in fluid dynamics)
          - Justifications or modifications of Monte Carlo methods
          - Applications of Monte Carlo methods (see also 02.50.Ng in probability theory, stochastic processes, and statistics, and 05.10.Ln in statistical physics)
          - Quantum mechanics, field theories, and special relativity (see also section 11 General theory of fields and particles)
          - Classical field theories
        - **Classical field theories have no quantum scale, so their scale is set by c, and by G for gravity-like fields.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the standard Maxwell theory to other special classical field theories._
          - Classical electromagnetism, Maxwell equations (for applied classical electromagnetism, see 41.20.-q)
          - Other special classical field theories
    - **Measurement and experimental practice are kinds of quantities, standards and procedures whose scale is fixed through units.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from units and standards to experimental procedures._
      - **Measurement topics are kinds of quantities and standards whose scale is fixed through c, ħ, G and N via units.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from general metrology to specific quantities scaled directly by the constants._
        - **Metrology topics concern measurement, its error theory, and the units and standards on which measurements rest.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS code sequence._
          - Metrology, measurements, and laboratory procedures (for laser applications in metrology, see 42.62.Eh)
          - Metrology
          - Measurement and error theory
          - Units and standards
        - **Constants, units and standards define the foundation that fixes the scale of any physical measurement.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from constants to units to standards._
          - Determination of fundamental constants
          - Units
          - Standards and calibration
        - **Mechanical measured quantities such as length, mass, time and velocity are scaled by c, ħ, G and N through their units.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from space to mass to time to motion._
          - Measurements common to several branches of physics and astronomy
          - Spatial dimensions (e.g., position, lengths, volume, angles, and displacements)
          - Mass and density
          - Time and frequency
          - Velocity, acceleration, and rotation
        - **Basic electromagnetic quantities are measured quantities whose scale depends on c and on other fundamental constants.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure._
          - Basic electromagnetic quantities (see also 84.37.+q Measurements in electric variables)
          - High-speed techniques (microsecond to femtosecond)
      - **Experimental and computational practice topics are kinds of procedures for carrying out and analysing experiments.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - **Laboratory procedures are practical techniques for preparing, handling, testing and safely carrying out experiments.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from preparation to testing to safety._
          - Laboratory procedures
          - Sample preparation (including design of sample holders)
          - Testing and inspecting procedures
          - Positioning and alignment; manipulating, remote handling
          - Workshop procedures (welding, machining, lubrication, bearings, etc.)
        - **Computing hardware and fluid turbulence are further topics treated with instruments and computation in experimental physics.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure._
          - Laboratory safety procedures (for national and international laboratory facilities, see 01.52.+r)
          - Turbulence
          - Instruments, apparatus, and components common to several branches of physics and astronomy (see also each subdiscipline for specialized instrumentation and techniques)
          - Computers in experimental physics (for computers in education, see 01.50.H-, and 01.50.Lc; for quantum computation architectures, see 03.67.Lx; for optical computers, see 42.79.Ta; for computational and simulation techniques, see 02.70.-c in mathematical methods)
          - Computer systems: hardware, operating systems, computer languages, and utilities
        - **Computational methods that process, analyse, model and visualize data once it has been gathered.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 07.05 listing._
          - Data analysis: algorithms and implementation; data management (for data analysis in nuclear physics, see 29.85.-c)
          - Neural networks, fuzzy logic, artificial intelligence
          - Image processing (see also 42.30.Va in optics; 87.57.-s Medical imaging in biological and medical physics; 95.75.Tv Digitization techniques in astronomy)
          - Data presentation and visualization: algorithms and implementation
          - Computer modeling and simulation
    - **Instrument and apparatus categories are kinds of hardware used to acquire, sense, generate and analyse physical quantities.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general apparatus to specialised hardware._
      - **General measurement and mechanical or thermal apparatus covers methods and devices that acquire, sense, and handle physical quantities.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general acquisition through mechanical to thermal equipment._
        - **Methods and hardware for planning measurements, acquiring signals, interfacing with computers, and controlling systems.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 07.05 and 07.07 listing._
          - Control systems
          - Design of experiments
          - Data acquisition: hardware and software
          - Computer interfaces (for nuclear physics applications, see 29.50.+v)
          - Servo and control equipment; robots
        - **Equipment that senses physical quantities, converts them to signals, or displays and records them.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 07.07 listing._
          - General equipment
          - Sensors (chemical, optical, electrical, movement, gas, etc.); remote sensing
          - Display and recording equipment, oscilloscopes, TV cameras, etc
          - Transducers
          - Hygrometers; hygrometry
        - **Mechanical instruments and devices that measure or manage force, strain, mass and vibration, including micromechanical systems.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 07.10 listing._
          - Mechanical instruments and equipment
          - Micromechanical devices and systems [for micro- and nano-electromechanical systems (MEMS/NEMS), see 85.85.+j in electronic and magnetic devices; see also 81.07.Oj Nanoelectromechanical systems (NEMS) in nanoscale materials and structures; see also 87.80.Ek Mechanical and micromechanical techniques; 87.85.Ox Biomedical instrumentation and transducers including micro-electro-mechanical systems in biological and medical physics]
          - Vibration isolation
          - Balance systems, tensile machines, etc
          - Instruments for strain, force, and torque
        - **Thermal measuring and heating apparatus covers instruments that sense, hold or generate temperature and heat.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS section._
          - Thermal instruments and apparatus
          - Thermometers
          - Calorimeters (for calorimeters as radiation detectors, see 29.40.Vj)
          - Furnaces; heaters
          - High-temperature instrumentation; pyrometers
        - **Low-temperature and heat-transfer machinery covers equipment that cools or moves heat to do work.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS section._
          - Cryogenics; refrigerators, low-temperature detectors, and other low-temperature equipment
          - Heat engines; heat pumps; heat pipes
      - **Vacuum apparatus covers the equipment that produces, measures, contains and verifies a vacuum.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from production to containment._
        - **Vacuum production and measurement apparatus covers the general category plus gas removal, pumping and pressure gauging.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS section._
          - Vacuum apparatus
          - Degasification, residual gas
          - Vacuum pumps
          - Vacuum gauges
        - **Vacuum containment and verification equipment covers the testing of seals and the chambers that hold a vacuum.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS section._
          - Vacuum testing methods; leak detectors
          - Vacuum chambers, auxiliary apparatus, and materials
      - **Electrical and magnetic instruments cover components, signal electronics and field-measuring devices.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from electrical components to magnetic instruments._
        - **Electrical instrument components cover the general category plus circuits, noise control and charge measurement.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS section._
          - Electrical and electronic instruments and components
          - Circuits and circuit components (see also 84.30.-r Electronic circuits and 84.32.-y Passive circuit components)
          - Electrical noise and shielding equipment
          - Electrometers
          - Magnetic instruments and components
        - **Signal handling and field generation equipment covers electronics that process signals and magnets that produce fields.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS section._
          - Signal processing electronics (see also 84.40.Ua in radiowave and microwave technology; 87.85.Ng Biological signal processing in biomedical engineering)
          - Generation of magnetic fields; magnets (for superconducting magnets, see 84.71.Ba; for beam focusing magnets, see 41.85.Lc in beam optics)
        - **Magnetic instruments measure magnetic fields, moments and magnetization, or shield against magnetic fields.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS listing: field, moment, shielding._
          - Magnetometers for magnetic field measurements
          - Magnetometers for susceptibility, magnetic moment, and magnetization measurements
          - Magnetic shielding in instruments
      - **Radiation and optical instruments are devices that generate, detect or analyze waves from radio frequencies to ultraviolet light.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from long-wavelength to short-wavelength instruments._
        - **Infrared and radio-frequency instruments generate, detect or analyze radiation from the infrared through the microwave and radiowave bands.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS listing: general, sources, detectors, spectrometers._
          - Infrared, submillimeter wave, microwave and radiowave instruments and equipment (for infrared and radio telescopes, see 95.55.Cs, 95.55.Fw, and 95.55.Jz in astronomy; for biophysical spectroscopic applications, see 87.64.-t)
          - Infrared, submillimeter wave, microwave, and radiowave sources (see also 42.72.Ai Infrared sources in optics)
          - Bolometers; infrared, submillimeter wave, microwave, and radiowave receivers and detectors (see also 85.60.Gz Photodetectors in electronic and magnetic devices, and 95.55.Rg Photoconductors and bolometers in astronomy)
          - Submillimeter wave, microwave and radiowave spectrometers; magnetic resonance spectrometers, auxiliary equipment, and techniques
          - Infrared spectrometers, auxiliary equipment, and techniques
        - **Optical measurement instruments quantify light intensity, polarization, refractive properties or interference.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS listing._
          - Optical instruments and equipment (see also 87.64.M- Optical microscopy in biological and medical physics; for optical sources, see 42.72.-g; see also 42.79.-e Optical elements, devices and systems; for optoelectronic devices, see 85.60.-q; for optical telescopes, see 95.55.Cs; for photometric, polarimetric, and spectroscopic equipment in astronomy, see 95.55.Qf)
          - Photometers, radiometers, and colorimeters
          - Polarimeters and ellipsometers
          - Refractometers and reflectometers
          - Interferometers
        - **Optical imaging and spectral instruments magnify, resolve or guide visible and ultraviolet light.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS listing._
          - Conventional optical microscopes (for near-field scanning optical microscopes, see 07.79.Fc; for x-ray microscopes, see 07.85.Tt)
          - Visible and ultraviolet spectrometers
          - Fiber-optic instruments (see also 42.81.-i Fiber optics)
      - **Particle, probe and X-ray hardware covers sources, detectors, scanning tips and high-energy photon analysis.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from particle hardware to X-ray analysis._
        - **Particle sources, detectors and scanning probes work with atoms, molecules, charged particles or a scanned physical tip.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS listing._
          - Atomic, molecular, and charged-particle sources and detectors
          - Atomic and molecular beam sources and detectors (see also 37.20.+j Atomic and molecular beam sources and techniques, in atomic and molecular physics)
          - Charged-particle beam sources and detectors (see also 29.40.-n Radiation detectors in nuclear physics)
          - Scanning probe microscopes and components (see also 68.37.-d Microscopy of surfaces, interfaces, and thin films)
        - **Scanning probe microscopes image surfaces by sensing a local tunneling, optical, or force interaction with a sharp tip.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure_
          - Scanning tunneling microscopes
          - Near-field scanning optical microscopes
          - Atomic force microscopes
          - Magnetic force microscopes
          - Friction force microscopes
        - **X-ray and gamma-ray hardware generates, steers, and detects high-energy photons, whose scale is set by ħ and c.**
          _basis: parts, order: structure_
          - X- and γ-ray instruments (for x- and γ-ray telescopes, see 95.55.Ka in astronomy; for x-ray beams and x-ray optics, see 41.50.+h)
          - X- and γ-ray sources, mirrors, gratings, and detectors
          - Synchrotron radiation instrumentation
        - **X-ray analysis instruments use diffraction, spectroscopy, or imaging to probe the structure of matter.**
          _basis: kinds, order: structure_
          - Diffractometers
          - X-ray and γ-ray spectrometers
          - X-ray microscopes
    - **Descriptions and calculation methods are kinds of mathematical approaches used to model fluid and field behavior.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from equations to flow-field descriptions._
      - Stokes equations
      - Electromagnetic field calculations
      - Lagrangian particle tracking
      - Eulerian flow field
  - **Transport, wave and fundamental phenomena are broad physical processes in which matter or waves are taken up, spread or shifted.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. From transport to fundamental phenomena to general headings._
    - **Transport and wave phenomena involve matter or waves being taken up, spread, or shifted in frequency.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed from uptake processes to spreading and frequency shift._
      - Absorption
      - Adsorption
      - Diffusion
      - Doppler Effect
      - Vortex flows
    - **Fundamental and natural phenomena cover broad physical processes from particle interactions to astronomical and biological systems.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed from broad category to specific phenomena._
      - Physical Phenomena
      - Astronomical Phenomena
      - Biophysical Phenomena
      - Elementary Particle Interactions
      - Cryogenics & vacuum technology
    - **General category concepts are broad headings that classify physics work by systems, professional topics, techniques, and technology.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from broad headings to specific areas._
      - K-12 teachers
      - Physical Systems
      - Professional Topics
      - Techniques
      - Technology
    - **Collective and large-scale phenomena in which G and N set the scale of bulk behavior.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from bulk to statistical and geological._
      - Geological Phenomena
      - Phase Separation
      - Phase Transition
      - Thermodynamics
      - Learning theory

### 2. Quantum systems is the regime where Planck's constant ħ sets the scale, covering particles, nuclei, atoms, molecules and quantum light.

- **Particles and fields are processes and entities whose scale is set by ħ and c with couplings and masses.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From frameworks to processes to species._
  - **General frameworks and further topics are broad headings in physics whose scale rests on ħ and c, with G and N entering for particular topics.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from foundational frameworks to applications and wider topic collections._
    - **Foundational particle and field headings and renormalization-type methods are kinds of general framework whose scale rests on ħ and c.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the broad foundational headings to the more technical methods that add N or temperature as parameters._
      - **Perturbative and semiclassical gauge methods are scaled by ħ and a small coupling, expanding around a classical or symmetry-broken vacuum.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the approach._
        - Gauge field theories
        - General properties of perturbation theory
        - Spontaneous breaking of gauge symmetries
        - Classical and semiclassical techniques
      - **Nonperturbative gauge methods are scaled by a large coupling or a large number of colors N rather than by a small coupling.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the approach._
        - Lattice gauge theory (see also 12.38.Gc Lattice QCD calculations)
        - Strong-coupling expansions
        - Expansions for large numbers of components (e.g., 1/ N c expansions)
        - Other nonperturbative techniques
      - **Topological gauge theories have scale set by a quantized level or topological mass rather than a continuous coupling.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the approach._
        - Topologically massive gauge theories (see also 04.60.Rt Topologically massive gravity)
        - Chern-Simons gauge theory
      - **Symmetry-breaking and chiral topics are kinds of dynamics in which a symmetry is hidden or only approximately conserved.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from breaking mechanisms to chiral symmetry and the partially conserved axial current._
        - Spontaneous and radiative symmetry breaking
        - Chiral symmetries
        - Partially conserved axial-vector currents
      - **Calculational methods extract QCD predictions either perturbatively in the small coupling or nonperturbatively on the lattice and by other means.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from perturbative expansions to resummation to nonperturbative techniques._
        - Perturbative calculations
        - Summation of perturbation theory
        - Lattice QCD calculations (see also 11.15.Ha Lattice gauge theory)
        - Other nonperturbative calculations
    - **Quantum information and nuclear topics are kinds of application whose scale is set by ħ and c, with nucleon number N entering for nuclear matter.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from ħ-dominated quantum information topics to nuclear topics that add the many-body number N._
      - **Quantum information and precision topics whose scale is set mainly by ħ, with c entering through measurement and communication limits.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in structure order, from foundations to applied technology and measurement._
        - Quantum correlations, foundations & formalism
        - Quantum communication, protocols & technology
        - Precision measurements
        - Noncommutative field theory
        - Nonlinear or nonlocal theories and models (see also 11.27.+d Extended classical solutions; cosmic strings, domain walls, texture)
      - **Nuclear and strong-interaction topics whose scale is set by ħ and c together with the many-body number N of nucleons.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in structure order, from the broad field to specific models and synthesis._
        - Nuclear Physics
        - Optical, coupled-channel & distorted wave models
        - Strong interaction
        - Synthesis of new superheavy elements
        - Bound and unstable states; Bethe-Salpeter equations
      - **Unified field theories and models set their scale by c, ħ and G, since they try to combine the interactions, possibly with gravity, and relate their couplings.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general class of unified theories to strong-electroweak unification and coupling relations._
        - Specific theories and interaction models; particle systematics
        - Unified field theories and models (see also 04.50.-h Higher-dimensional gravity and other theories of gravity—in general relativity and gravitation, 11.25.Mj Compactification and four-dimensional models)
        - Unified theories and models of strong and electroweak interactions
        - Unification of couplings; mass relations
      - **Electroweak flavor and mixing claims set their scale by c and ħ through quark and lepton masses and CKM mixing parameters.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general electroweak theory to flavor parameters and their determination._
        - Electroweak interactions
        - Quark and lepton masses and mixing (see also 14.60.Pq Neutrino mass and mixing)
        - Determination of Cabibbo-Kobayashi & Maskawa (CKM) matrix elements
      - **Electroweak process predictions and quantum electrodynamics calculations set their scale by c and ħ through the fine-structure coupling in specific processes and tests.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from electroweak applications and corrections to QED calculations and tests._
        - Applications of electroweak models to specific processes
        - Electroweak radiative corrections (see also 13.40.Ks Electromagnetic corrections to strong- and weak-interaction processes)
        - Quantum electrodynamics
        - Experimental tests (for optical tests in quantum electrodynamics, see 42.50.Xa)
    - **Further topics in physics are grouped by which governing constants among c, ħ, G and N set the scale of each claim, from the strong interaction and string theory to symmetries and many-body methods.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **The general framework of QCD sets the fundamental scale of the strong interaction through the coupling, confinement, and experimental checks of the theory.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the overarching theory to its general properties and experimental tests._
        - Quantum chromodynamics (for quarks, gluons, and QCD in nuclear reactions, see 24.85.+p)
        - General properties of QCD (dynamics, confinement, etc.)
        - Experimental tests
      - **Core string theory topics are set by the string scale, combining ħ, c and the string tension, along with the string coupling.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the approach._
        - Strings and branes (for cosmic strings, see 98.80.Cq in cosmology; see also 11.27.+d Extended classical solutions; cosmic strings, domain walls, texture)
        - Properties of perturbation theory
        - Conformal field theory, algebraic structures
        - Noncritical string theory
        - Nonperturbative techniques; string field theory
      - **String applications and dualities connect the string scale to gravity and gauge theory through G, N and compactification scales.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the approach._
        - Compactification and four-dimensional models
        - Gauge/string duality
        - D branes
        - String and brane phenomenology
        - M theory
      - **Spacetime and discrete symmetries are kinds of symmetry whose scale is set by the speed of light c through relativistic invariance and discrete transformations.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general framework to specific spacetime and discrete symmetries._
        - Symmetry and conservation laws (see also 02.20.-a Group theory)
        - Lorentz and Poincaré invariance
        - Charge conjugation, parity, time reversal, and other discrete symmetries
        - Global symmetries (e.g., baryon number, lepton number)
      - **Approximation and many-body scattering methods set their scale by c and ħ together with the particle number N, because they handle several channels or several scatterers.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from approximation schemes to multi-channel and many-body treatments._
        - Approximations (eikonal approximation, variational principles, etc.)
        - Multichannel scattering
        - Many-body scattering and Faddeev equation
        - Multiple scattering
    - **This group collects further topics of quantum field and particle theory, each classified by which of the constants c, ħ, G and N sets the scale of its claims.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **S-matrix topics are kinds of analytic constraint on scattering amplitudes, such as analyticity, exact solutions and dispersion relations.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general S-matrix theory to analytic properties, exact S matrices and dispersion relations._
        - S-matrix theory; analytic structure of amplitudes
        - Analytic properties of S matrix
        - Exact S matrices
        - Dispersion relations
      - **Collective and exotic states of strongly interacting matter are described by thermal ensembles, deconfined plasma, and multi-quark or gluonic bound states.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from deconfined plasma to exotic hadron states to statistical and other models._
        - Quark-gluon plasma (see also 25.75.Nq Quark deconfinement, quark-gluon plasma production and phase transitions in relativistic heavy ion collisions; see also 21.65.Qr Quark matter)
        - Glueball and nonstandard multi-quark/gluon states
        - Statistical models
        - Other models for strong interactions
      - **Current and sum-rule topics are kinds of formal tools that express conserved quantities and their algebraic and spectral relations.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general theory of currents to current algebras, sum rules and Regge methods._
        - Currents and their properties
        - General theory of currents
        - Formal properties of current algebras (see also 12.39.Fe Chiral Lagrangians)
        - Sum rules
        - Regge formalism (see also 12.40.Nn in strong interactions)
      - **Flavor, higher and supersymmetries are kinds of internal or extended symmetry whose quantum scale is set by ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from ordinary flavor symmetry to extended and dynamical symmetries._
        - Flavor symmetries
        - Other internal and higher symmetries
        - Nonlinear and dynamical symmetries (spectrum-generating symmetries)
        - Supersymmetry (see also 12.60.Jv Supersymmetric models)
      - **Relativistic scattering formalism and kinematics set their scale by c and ħ alone, since they describe general S-matrix structure without any specific coupling.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general scattering framework to its kinematic and angular-momentum decomposition._
        - Relativistic scattering theory
        - Kinematical properties (helicity and invariant amplitudes, kinematic singularities, etc.)
        - Partial-wave analysis
        - Neutral currents
        - Specific calculations
  - **Particle and field claims are kinds of process or model whose scale is set by ħ and c together with couplings, masses and collision energy.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general particle claims to models, decays, electroweak claims and collisions._
    - **Claims about particles and fields whose scale is set by the governing constants ħ and c, with N or temperature entering only as additional parameters.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **Broad foundational headings for particles and fields, whose scale depends on ħ and c through relativistic quantum theory.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in structure order, from the most general heading to more specific ones._
        - THE PHYSICS OF ELEMENTARY PARTICLES AND FIELDS (for experimental methods and instrumentation for elementary-particle physics, see section 29)
        - General theory of fields and particles (see also 03.65.-w Quantum mechanics and 03.70.+k Theory of quantized fields)
        - Field theory (for gauge field theories, see 11.15.-q)
        - Axiomatic approach
        - Lagrangian and Hamiltonian approach
      - **Technical field-theory methods for handling scale dependence, in which ħ and c set the scale and N or temperature enter as parameters.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in structure order, from renormalization methods to special regimes and settings._
        - Renormalization
        - Renormalization group evolution of parameters
        - Asymptotic problems and properties
        - Field theories in dimensions other than four (see also 04.50.-h Higher-dimensional gravity and other theories of gravity; 04.60.Kz Lower dimensional models; minisuperspace models in general relativity and gravitation)
        - Finite-temperature field theory
    - **Theoretical and model frameworks describe hadrons and new physics through effective or phenomenological constructions whose scale comes from ħ, c, couplings or binding.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from hadron-level models to effective theories to physics beyond the standard model._
      - **Phenomenological quark models describe hadrons as bound states of constituent quarks, with differing treatments of confinement and relativistic effects.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the general class of quark models to specific bag, nonrelativistic, relativistic, and potential variants._
        - Phenomenological quark models
        - Bag model
        - Nonrelativistic quark model
        - Relativistic quark model
        - Potential models
      - **Effective-theory approaches reduce QCD to simpler low-energy or heavy-quark descriptions by exploiting symmetries and separating scales.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from chiral and soliton descriptions to heavy-quark theory and factorization._
        - Skyrmions
        - Chiral Lagrangians
        - Heavy quark effective theory
        - Factorization
      - **Phenomenological hadron models describe strong-interaction behavior and hadron properties using effective frameworks scaled by ħ, c and the strong coupling.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: from scattering models to mass models._
        - Regge theory, duality, absorptive/optical models (see also 11.55.Jy Regge formalism)
        - Vector-meson dominance
        - Hadron mass models and calculations
