      - **Models beyond the standard model extend the known gauge, Higgs or matter content, setting their scale by new physics above the electroweak constants.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: from the general category to specific extensions._
        - Models beyond the standard model (for unified field theories, see 12.10.-g)
        - Extensions of electroweak gauge sector
        - Extensions of electroweak Higgs sector
        - Supersymmetric models (see also 04.65.+e Supergravity)
        - Technicolor models
      - **Composite models and atomic and molecular collisions treat systems built from constituents, with the scale set by binding of those parts.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: from the substructure model to the collision category._
        - Composite models
        - Atomic & molecular collisions
    - **Decay claims cover weak, electromagnetic and strong transitions of mesons, leptons and bosons, with rates scaled by c, ħ and the relevant couplings.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by the decaying species, from mesons through hadrons to leptons and bosons._
      - **Leptonic, semileptonic and radiative meson decays are weak or electromagnetic transitions of specific meson families, with scales set by c, ħ and the weak or fine-structure couplings.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: from the general category to specific meson families._
        - Specific reactions and phenomenology
        - Leptonic, semileptonic, and radiative decays of mesons
        - Decays of π mesons
        - Decays of K mesons
        - Decays of charmed mesons
      - **Quarkonium, bottom-meson, other-meson and hadronic pion decays are meson decays whose rates are governed by the strong interaction and heavy-quark masses.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: from quarkonia to hadronic decays._
        - Decays of J /ψ, Υ, and other quarkonia
        - Decays of bottom mesons
        - Decays of other mesons
        - Hadronic decays of mesons
        - Decays of π mesons
      - **Light mesons and baryons are hadrons whose weak and strong decay modes are classified together by decaying hadron.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from meson decays to baryon decays and their sub-modes._
        - Decays of K mesons
        - Decays of other mesons
        - Decays of baryons
        - Leptonic, semileptonic, and radiative decays
        - Hadronic decays
      - **Heavy-flavor mesons are mesons containing charm or bottom quarks, including quarkonia, whose decays are classified together.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, by increasing heavy quark mass._
        - Decays of charmed mesons
        - Decays of J /ψ, Υ, and other quarkonia
        - Decays of bottom mesons
      - **Lepton decays are the decays of the charged leptons and heavy neutrinos through the weak interaction.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the general category to specific leptons._
        - Decays of leptons
        - Decays of muons
        - Decays of taus
        - Decays of heavy neutrinos
    - **Electroweak and electromagnetic claims concern the W and Z bosons and electromagnetic properties, whose scale is set by c, ħ and the weak or fine-structure couplings.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the weak bosons to electromagnetic properties and interactions._
      - **Intermediate boson decays are the decays of the W and Z bosons that carry the weak force.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the general category to W and Z._
        - Decays of intermediate bosons
        - Decays of W bosons
        - Decays of Z bosons
      - **Electromagnetic processes and properties cover masses, moments, form factors and decays governed by the electromagnetic interaction.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the general category to specific properties._
        - Electromagnetic processes and properties
        - Electromagnetic mass differences
        - Electric and magnetic moments
        - Electromagnetic form factors
        - Electromagnetic decays
      - **Photon and charged-lepton interactions with hadrons, including electromagnetic corrections, are set in scale by c and ħ through the electromagnetic coupling.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered from the general category to specific scattering and production types_
        - Electromagnetic corrections to strong- and weak-interaction processes
        - Photon and charged-lepton interactions with hadrons (for neutrino interactions, see 13.15.+g)
        - Elastic and Compton scattering
        - Total and inclusive cross sections (including deep-inelastic processes)
        - Meson production
      - **Inclusive production measurements are classified by which particles are detected or bounded in a collision, including cosmic-ray interactions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from identified hadrons to nonhadronic particles to limits to cosmic rays._
        - Inclusive production with identified hadrons
        - Inclusive production with identified leptons, photons, or other nonhadronic particles
        - Limits on production of particles
        - Cosmic-ray interactions (see also 96.50.S- Cosmic rays in interplanetary physics)
      - **Jets in large-Q2 scattering are described by the stages of their formation, from the general phenomenon through production to fragmentation into hadrons.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general jets to production to fragmentation._
        - Jets in large-Q 2 scattering
        - Production
        - Fragmentation into hadrons
    - **Collision and production claims describe reactions of leptons and hadrons whose scale is set by collision energy, interaction range and particle masses with c and ħ.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from lepton collisions to high-energy and hadron reactions to jets._
      - **Baryon production and lepton-lepton collisions producing hadrons and leptons are scaled by c and ħ through the center-of-mass energy of the colliding leptons.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered from the general lepton-lepton category to specific e-e+ final states_
        - Baryon production
        - Lepton-lepton interactions
        - Hadron production in e − e + interactions
        - Lepton production in e − e + interactions
        - Processes in other lepton-lepton interactions
      - **High-energy electroweak, Higgs, new-physics and super-high-energy hadronic processes are scaled by c and ħ at the highest available energies.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered from e-e+ precision and heavy-boson processes to the highest-energy hadron reactions_
        - Gauge and Higgs boson production in e − e + interactions
        - Production of non-standard model particles in e − e + interactions
        - Precision measurements in e − e + interactions
        - Hadron-induced high- and super-high-energy interactions (energy > 10 GeV) (for low energies, see 13.75.-n)
      - **Low- and intermediate-energy nucleon and hyperon reactions are scaled by c and ħ through the strong-interaction range and the nucleon mass.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered from the general category to nucleon, hyperon and pion interactions_
        - Hadron-induced low- and intermediate-energy reactions and scattering (energy ≤ 10 GeV) (for higher energies, see 13.85.-t)
        - Nucleon-nucleon interactions (including antinucleons, deuterons, etc.) (for N - N interactions in nuclei, see 21.30.-x)
        - Hyperon-nucleon interactions
        - Pion-baryon interactions
      - **Kaon-baryon and meson-meson interactions are scaled by c and ħ through the meson masses and strong-interaction range.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. ordered from meson-baryon to meson-meson scattering_
        - Kaon-baryon interactions
        - Meson-meson interactions
      - **Hadron collision processes are classified by the scattering outcome, with cross sections whose scale is set by ħ and c.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from elastic to inelastic to total cross section._
        - Elastic scattering
        - Inelastic scattering: two-particle final states
        - Inelastic scattering: many-particle final states
        - Total cross sections
  - **Particle species are kinds of entity in the Standard Model, namely hadrons, fundamental fermions and gauge or scalar bosons.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from composite hadrons to fundamental fermions and then bosons._
    - **Particle catalogue entries classify baryons, mesons and other specific hadron categories by their flavor content and type.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from baryons to other categories to mesons._
      - **Baryons are classified by their flavor content and type, from nucleons to resonances, hyperons and charmed baryons.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the general class to nucleons, resonances, hyperons and charmed baryons._
        - Baryons (including antiparticles) (for decays of baryons, see 13.30.-a)
        - Protons and neutrons
        - Baryon resonances ( S = C = B =0)
        - Hyperons
        - Charmed baryons (| C |>0, B =0)
      - **Other specific particle categories include the general properties heading, bottom and exotic baryons, and mesons.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the general heading to bottom baryons, exotic baryons and mesons._
        - Properties of specific particles
        - Bottom baryons (| B |>0)
        - Exotic baryons
        - Mesons (for leptonic decays of mesons, see 13.20.-v; for hadronic decays of mesons, see 13.25.-k)
      - **Mesons are quark-antiquark hadrons that differ by the flavour content of their constituent quarks.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from light flavour-free mesons through strange, charmed and bottom to exotic states._
        - Light mesons ( S = C = B =0)
        - Strange mesons (| S |>0, C = B =0)
        - Charmed mesons (| C |>0, B =0)
        - Bottom mesons (| B |>0)
        - Exotic mesons
    - **Fundamental fermions and their heavy bound states are the leptons and quarks of the Standard Model, distinguished by flavour, generation and mass.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from leptons to quarks to the overview of heavy bound states._
      - **Charged leptons are the electrically charged point-like fermions that do not feel the strong force, distinguished by generation and mass.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the electron through the muon and tau to heavier hypothetical leptons._
        - Electrons (including positrons)
        - Muons
        - Taus
        - Other charged heavy leptons
      - **Neutrino topics cover the neutral, weakly interacting leptons, including their ordinary, massive and non-standard forms.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from ordinary neutrinos to mass and mixing to non-standard neutrinos._
        - Ordinary neutrinos
        - Neutrino mass and mixing (see also 12.15.Ff Quark and lepton masses and mixing)
        - Non-standard-model neutrinos, right-handed neutrinos, etc
      - **Quark flavours are the distinct types of quark, each characterized by its own flavour and mass.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from light quarks through charm, bottom and top to further quarks._
        - Light quarks
        - Charmed quarks
        - Bottom quarks
        - Top quarks
        - Other quarks (e.g., 4th generations)
      - **Overview headings and heavy bound states link the general lepton and quark categories to quark-antiquark systems of heavy flavours.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general lepton and quark headings to the heavy quarkonium bound states._
        - Leptons (for decays of leptons, see 13.35.-r)
        - Quarks
        - Heavy quarkonia
    - **Standard Model gauge and scalar bosons are classed by how c, ħ, N and the Higgs vacuum scale set the size of their claims.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from massless bosons scaled by c and ħ alone, to massive bosons needing a Higgs mass, to the scalar sector scaled by the vacuum expectation value._
      - **Massless gauge bosons of the unbroken forces have scales set by c and ħ alone, with the number of colours N entering for gluons.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the setting constants_
        - Gauge bosons
        - Photons
        - Gluons
        - Gravitons (see also 04.60.-m Quantum gravity)
      - **Massive electroweak gauge bosons have scales set by ħ and c together with the Higgs-given mass.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the setting constants_
        - W bosons
        - Z bosons
        - Other gauge bosons
        - Other particles (including hypothetical)
        - Axions and other Nambu-Goldstone bosons (Majorons, familons, etc.)
      - **Higgs and scalar-sector particles have scales set by the vacuum expectation value and the breaking of symmetry.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the setting constants_
        - Standard-model Higgs bosons
        - Supersymmetric Higgs bosons
        - Other neutral Higgs bosons
        - Other charged Higgs bosons
      - **Hypothetical new-physics particles have scales set by N-dependent new couplings or by extra structure beyond the standard model.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the setting constants_
        - Magnetic monopoles
        - Supersymmetric partners of known particles (see also 12.60.Jv Supersymmetric models)
        - Neutralinos and charginos
        - R -hadrons
        - Kaluza-Klein excitations (see also 04.50.Cd Kaluza-Klein theories)
      - **Technicolor and leptoquarks are hypothesized particles beyond the Standard Model whose claims are scaled by the fundamental constants c, ħ, G and N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Technicolor
        - Leptoquarks
  - **Particle and nuclear phenomena whose scale is fixed by ħ and c.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from particles to nuclear processes._
    - Elementary Particles
    - Nuclear Energy
    - Radioactivity
    - Scattering, Radiation
    - Radiation
- **Nuclear systems are structures, reactions and radiation technologies scaled by nucleon number and ħ.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From structure to reactions to technology._
  - **Nuclear structure and model topics are scaled by nucleon number N and ħ, describing static properties, few-body systems and bulk or collective behaviour.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general nuclear structure to N-scaled topics and the models they use._
    - **Nuclear structure and models cover nuclear headings, static properties, forces, few-body systems, models and bulk matter, with scales set by nucleon number N and ħ.**
      _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from broad headings through static properties and forces to models and bulk matter._
      - **Broad nuclear-domain headings whose claims are scaled by the nucleon number N and the nuclear interaction scale, with ħ and c entering only as background constants.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the broadest heading (nuclear physics as a whole) to the narrower ones (structure, then properties of nuclei)._
        - NUCLEAR PHYSICS
        - Nuclear structure (for nucleon structure, see 14.20.Dh Properties of protons and neutrons; 13.40.-f for electromagnetic processes and properties; 13.60.Hb for deep-inelastic structure functions)
        - Properties of nuclei; nuclear energy levels (for properties of specific nuclei listed by mass ranges, see section 27)
        - Nuclear structure & decays
      - **Bulk static properties of a nucleus whose magnitudes are set by the nucleon number N together with ħ and c through the nucleon size and mass scales.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the most N-dependent bulk quantity (binding energy and mass) to the distribution-based quantities._
        - Binding energies and masses
        - Charge distribution
        - Nucleon distributions and halo features
        - Level density
        - Coulomb energies, analogue states
      - **Quantum-number and moment properties of nuclear states whose scale is fixed by ħ, with c entering through the electromagnetic moments and N through the number of nucleons.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the purely ħ-quantized property (spin and parity) to the properties that also need c and N._
        - Spin, parity, and isobaric spin
        - Electromagnetic moments
        - Spectroscopic factors and asymptotic normalization coefficients
        - Lifetimes, widths
      - **Nuclear force topics describe the interaction between nucleons and hadrons, with scale set by ħ and c through meson exchange and effective interactions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from vacuum forces to effective hadronic interactions._
        - Nuclear forces in vacuum
        - Forces in hadronic systems and effective interactions
        - Symmetry energy
        - Radioactive decay and in-beam spectroscopy
      - **Microscopic and algebraic model methods build nuclear structure from many-body quantum mechanics, where ħ and the nucleon number N set the scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the general heading to the ab initio, mean-field and stochastic computational methods._
        - Nuclear structure models and methods
        - Ab initio methods
        - Nuclear Density Functional Theory and extensions (includes Hartree-Fock and random-phase approximations)
        - Monte Carlo models
    - **Nuclear-physics topics whose scale is fixed by the number of constituents N, together with ħ for quantum level spacing, running from few-body systems to bulk matter and collective structure models.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **Collective and symmetry-based nuclear models describe structure through shells, clusters, collective modes and group-theoretical symmetries rather than full microscopic calculation.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the shell model to collective, group-theoretical and cluster descriptions._
        - Shell model
        - Collective models
        - Models based on group theory
        - Cluster models
      - **Theoretical models and level descriptions of the nucleus whose scale depends on N collectively, with ħ setting the quantum level spacing.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the single-particle description (smallest N dependence) to the collective and few-body treatments._
        - Single-particle levels and strength functions
        - Collective levels
        - Collective models
        - Hyperspherical method
        - Nuclear forces (see also 13.75.Cs Nucleon-nucleon interactions)
      - **Few-body topics treat systems of two or three nucleons, where the number N of bodies is small and fixes the scale of the problem.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the general few-body class to two-nucleon and three-nucleon systems._
        - Few-body systems
        - Two-nucleon system
        - Three-nucleon forces
      - **Bulk matter and decay topics concern large-N nuclear and quark matter and radioactive decay, where thermodynamic limits and the weak interaction set the scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from nuclear matter in general to its asymmetric, symmetry-energy, meson, equation-of-state and quark forms, then decay._
        - Nuclear matter (for exotic atoms and molecules, see 36.10.-k)
        - Asymmetric matter, neutron matter
        - Mesons in nuclear matter
        - Equations of state of nuclear matter (see also 26.60.Kp Equations of state of neutron-star matter)
        - Quark matter (see also 12.38.Mh Quark-gluon plasma in quantum chromodynamics; 25.75.Nq Quark deconfinement, quark-gluon plasma production and phase transitions in relativistic heavy-ion collisions)
    - **Models and processes whose scale is set by ħ and c treat nuclear collisions through wave equations, diffraction and relativistic dynamics.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from wave-based models to relativistic collisions._
      - **Quantum many-body and wave-based reaction models are scaled by ħ and c, treating nuclear scattering through wave equations and relativistic dynamics.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from general reaction models to specific wave-based formulations._
        - Nuclear reaction models and methods
        - Coupled-channel and distorted-wave models
        - Optical and diffraction models
        - Relativistic models
      - **Inelastic neutron scattering and inelastic proton scattering are nuclear reactions in which an incident nucleon excites a target nucleus, so the claim's scale is set by the constants ħ and c together with the nucleon number N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Inelastic neutron scattering
        - Inelastic proton scattering
      - **Scattering, excitation and resonance processes have a scale set by ħ through the de Broglie wavelength and the diffraction of the colliding nuclei.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from total cross sections through elastic scattering and Coulomb excitation to resonances._
        - Interaction and reaction cross sections
        - Elastic and quasielastic scattering
        - Coulomb excitation
        - Resonances
      - **Relativistic heavy-ion collisions have a scale set by the speed of light c because the collision energies approach relativistic values.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by how strongly relativistic each entry is, from the general low-energy heading to the hard-scattering regime._
        - Low and intermediate energy heavy-ion reactions
        - Relativistic heavy-ion collisions (collisions induced by light ions studied to calibrate relativistic heavy-ion collisions should be classified under both 25.75.-q and sections 13 or 25 appropriate to the light ions)
        - Global features in relativistic heavy ion collisions
        - Hard scattering in relativistic heavy ion collisions
      - **Heavy-ion collision observables are the particles, photons and correlations measured to characterize relativistic nuclear collisions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in PACS numbering order._
        - Photon, lepton, and heavy quark production in relativistic heavy ion collisions
        - Particle and resonance production
        - Particle correlations and fluctuations
        - Collective flow
        - Quark deconfinement, quark-gluon plasma production, and phase transitions (see also 12.38.Mh Quark-gluon plasma in quantum chromodynamics; 21.65.Qr Quark matter in nuclear matter)
    - **Models and processes whose scale is set by the number N of particles describe ensemble behaviour, averaging and multi-fragment splitting.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from bulk models to specific many-body reaction types._
      - **Statistical and bulk-flow reaction models are scaled by the particle number N, which governs ensemble behaviour through sampling, hydrodynamics and thermal averages.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from numerical sampling to continuum and thermal descriptions._
        - Monte Carlo simulations (including hadron and parton cascades and string breaking models)
        - Hydrodynamic models
        - Thermal and statistical models
      - **Statistical reaction theories describe nuclear reactions through averaged behaviour, fluctuations and chaos among many overlapping levels.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 24.60 subtree: general heading, then compound-nucleus, multistep direct, fluctuation and chaos entries._
        - Statistical theory and fluctuations
        - Statistical compound-nucleus reactions
        - Statistical multistep direct reactions
        - Fluctuation phenomena
        - Chaos in nuclear systems
      - **Fragmentation and breakup processes have a scale set by the number N of nucleons that the colliding nuclei split into.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from simple breakup to strongly damped collisions and multifragment emission._
        - Breakup and momentum distributions
        - Projectile and target fragmentation
        - Multifragment emission and correlations
        - Strongly damped collisions
      - **Resonance reactions are nuclear reactions dominated by collective or discrete resonant excitations of the nucleus.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 24.30 subtree: general heading, then giant resonances, then other resonances._
        - Resonance reactions
        - Giant resonances
        - Other resonances
  - **Nuclear reactions and transitions are processes in which nuclei interact, decay or are probed, classed by the projectile and mechanism involved.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from transition processes to quantum reactions, probe reactions and applied reaction classes._
    - **Nuclear transition, decay and resonance processes are scaled by coupling strengths together with c and ħ rather than by nucleon number.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from phenomena independent of c to electromagnetic scaling by c and ħ to weak-coupling processes._
      - **Atomic and resonance phenomena whose scale is set by ħ and the interaction strength of the colliding or driven particles, independent of c, G and nuclear N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the scattering resonance to the driven coherent optical process._
        - Feshbach resonance
        - Coherent Raman in a low pressure gas
      - **Electromagnetic transition measurements are scaled by the fine-structure coupling built from c and ħ, which fixes photon emission rates and angular patterns.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from measurement methods to the quantities they extract._
        - Electromagnetic transitions
        - Angular distribution and correlation measurements
        - Multipole mixing ratios
        - Multipole matrix elements
        - Optics & lasers
      - **Gamma-ray energy and electron-emission processes are scaled by c and ħ through photon energies and the conversion of nuclear excitation into atomic electrons or pairs.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from gamma emission to the processes that replace it._
        - γ transitions and level energies
        - Internal conversion and extranuclear effects (including Auger electrons and internal bremsstrahlung)
        - Internal pair production
      - **Beta decay and capture processes are scaled by the weak coupling together with c and ħ, which set lepton emission rates tied to nuclear structure.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from general weak processes to nuclear-structure links._
        - β decay; double β decay; electron and muon capture
        - Weak-interaction and lepton (including neutrino) aspects (see also 14.60.Pq Neutrino mass and mixing)
        - Relation with nuclear matrix elements and nuclear structure
        - Many-body theory
        - Nuclear reactions: general
    - **Reactions whose scale is set by ħ through quantum tunnelling and the overlap of nuclear wave functions as nuclei join, exchange nucleons or scatter.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from fusion to transfer and exchange to scattering._
      - **Fusion and capture processes have a scale set by ħ through quantum tunnelling of the Coulomb barrier and the joining of nuclei.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from light-ion fusion and capture to heavy-ion fusion and the compound nucleus._
        - Fusion reactions
        - Radiative capture
        - Fusion and fusion-fission reactions
        - Compound nucleus
      - **Transfer and charge-exchange reactions have a scale set by ħ through the quantum overlap of nuclear wave functions when nucleons or charge pass between nuclei.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, with light-ion reactions before heavy-ion reactions and transfer before charge exchange._
        - Transfer reactions
        - Charge-exchange reactions
        - Transfer reactions
        - Charge-exchange reactions
      - **Transfer reactions move one or more nucleons between projectile and target nuclei.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by increasing projectile mass: nucleon-induced, then light-ion, then heavy-ion transfer._
        - Transfer reactions
        - Transfer reactions
        - Transfer reactions
        - Radiative capture
        - Resonance reactions
      - **Charge-exchange reactions change the charge state of the colliding nucleons while the mass number stays fixed.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by increasing projectile mass: nucleon-induced, then light-ion, then heavy-ion charge exchange._
        - Charge-exchange reactions
        - Charge-exchange reactions
        - Charge-exchange reactions
      - **Scattering processes leave projectile and target intact, changing only their direction or excitation.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked least to most disruptive: elastic scattering first, then inelastic scattering._
        - Elastic proton scattering
        - Elastic neutron scattering
        - Elastic and inelastic scattering
        - Elastic and inelastic scattering
        - Elastic scattering
    - **Reactions in which a photon, electron, other lepton or meson serves as the projectile probing the nucleus.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from photon and lepton probes to meson probes._
      - **Photonuclear reactions are nuclear reactions in which a real photon is absorbed, scattered or used to produce particles.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 25.20 subtree: general heading, then absorption and scattering, then photoproduction._
        - Photonuclear reactions
        - Photon absorption and scattering
        - Photoproduction reactions
      - **Electron scattering reactions probe nuclear structure by scattering electrons elastically or inelastically from the nucleus.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 25.30 subtree: general heading, then elastic, then inelastic to specific states, then inelastic to continuum._
        - Lepton-induced reactions
        - Elastic electron scattering
        - Inelastic electron scattering to specific states
        - Inelastic electron scattering to continuum
      - **Other lepton-induced reactions use positrons, muons, neutrinos or electroproduction to probe nuclei through leptonic interactions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 25.30 subtree: positron, muon, neutrino, then electroproduction entries._
        - Nuclear reactions: specific reactions
        - Positron-induced reactions
        - Muon-induced reactions (including the EMC effect)
        - Neutrino-induced reactions
        - Electroproduction reactions
      - **Pion-induced reactions are nuclear processes in which an incident pion scatters elastically, inelastically, by charge exchange, or is absorbed.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in PACS numbering order._
        - Pion elastic scattering
        - Pion inelastic scattering
        - Pion charge-exchange reactions
        - Pion-induced reactions
        - Pion inclusive scattering and absorption
      - **Strange-particle and general meson-induced reactions are nuclear processes initiated by kaons, hyperons or mesons in general.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in PACS numbering order._
        - Meson- and hyperon-induced reactions
        - Kaon-induced reactions
        - Hyperon-induced reactions
    - **Fission, stellar nuclear processes and broad high-energy categories are applied or general classes of nuclear reaction.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from fission to astrophysics to general categories._
      - **Fission reactions are nuclear splitting processes distinguished by whether they occur spontaneously or are induced by neutrons, charged particles or photons.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in PACS numbering order._
        - Fission reactions
        - Spontaneous fission
        - Neutron-induced fission
        - Charged-particle-induced fission
        - Photofission
      - **Nuclear astrophysics covers nuclear processes in stars, including nucleosynthesis during hydrostatic stellar burning.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in PACS numbering order._
        - Nuclear astrophysics (see also 95.30.-k Fundamental aspects of astrophysics in astronomy)
        - Hydrostatic stellar nucleosynthesis (see also 97.10.Cv Stellar structure, interiors, evolution, nucleosynthesis, ages in astronomy)
      - **Quiescent stellar burning stages convert lighter nuclei into heavier ones in sequence over a star's lifetime.**
        _basis: cause_effect, order: time. Ordered from hydrogen burning to helium burning to s-process, late evolution and quasistatic processes._
        - Stellar hydrogen burning
        - Stellar helium burning
        - s-process
        - Nucleosynthesis in late stellar evolution
        - Quasistatistical processes
      - **High-energy and general-category entries cover meson production, spallation and broad classes of nucleon or unstable-nucleus induced reactions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the broad general categories to the most violent reactions above meson production thresholds._
        - Nucleon-induced reactions (see also 28.20.-v Neutron physics)
        - Reactions induced by unstable nuclei
        - ( p , π) reactions
        - Spallation reactions
        - Other reactions above meson production thresholds (energies > 400 MeV)
  - **Nuclear and radiation-technology topics concern nuclei, neutrons and engineered machines, instruments and beams whose scales are set by nuclear processes and particle energies.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from natural nuclear processes to reactors, machines, beams and detection._
    - **Astrophysical nuclear topics and neutron interaction physics are the areas where nuclei and neutrons are studied as natural or fundamental processes.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from astrophysical settings to neutron interactions._
      - **Explosive nucleosynthesis environments produce nuclei rapidly under extreme temperature, density and neutron or neutrino flux.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by environment and process type._
        - Nucleosynthesis in novae, supernovae, and other explosive environments
        - Explosive burning in accreting binary systems (novae, x-ray bursts)
        - Explosive burning in supernovae shock fronts
        - r-process
        - Weak interaction and neutrino induced processes, galactic radioactivity
      - **Nuclear matter aspects of neutron stars are the regions and equation of state whose scale is set by gravity and nuclear interactions.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from the star's interior core to its crust to the matter equation of state._
        - Nuclear matter aspects of neutron stars
        - Neutron star core
        - Neutron star crust
        - Equations of state of neutron-star matter
      - **Entries on specific nuclei and a truncated conformal space computational method are treated as structure-and-method topics rather than astrophysical processes.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered with nuclear properties first and the computational method second._
        - Properties of specific nuclei listed by mass ranges (an additional heading must be chosen with these entries, where the given mass number limits are, to some degree, arbitrary)
        - Truncated conformal space approach
      - **Neutron physics for nuclear engineering covers how neutrons interact with matter through scattering and absorption.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from the general field to scattering to absorption._
        - Nuclear engineering and nuclear power studies
        - Neutron physics (see also 25.40.-h Nucleon-induced reactions and 25.85.Ec Neutron-induced fission)
        - Neutron scattering
        - Neutron absorption
    - **Reactor technology topics cover the neutron physics, design, operation, types and waste handling of fission plants.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from neutron physics through design and operation to reactor types and waste._
      - **Neutron-physics topics concern how neutrons interact with matter, a scale set by ħ and nuclear cross sections and by the nucleon number N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from neutron slowing and cross sections to the radiation emitted and imaged._
        - Neutron transport: diffusion and moderation
        - Thermal neutron cross sections
        - Neutron capture γ-rays
        - Neutron imaging; neutron tomography
      - **Reactor design and modelling covers the theory and simulation of the reactor as a whole, with the core scale set by neutron transport and the nuclear constants behind it.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. General overview of fission reactors followed by the theory and design of the reactor._
        - Fission reactors (see also 89.30.Gg nuclear fission power in energy resources)
        - Theory, design, and computerized simulation
        - Moderators
        - Reactor coolants, reactor cooling, and heat recovery
        - Structural and shielding materials
      - **Operation, safety and fuel-cycle topics cover control, instrumentation and protection of the plant, and the handling of fuel and radioactive waste.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from control and monitoring to fuel handling and waste disposal._
        - Reactor control systems
        - Protection systems, safety, radiation monitoring, accidents, and dismantling
        - Fuel elements, preparation, reloading, and reprocessing
        - Fuel cycles
      - **Reactor types are the kinds of fission reactor classified by their purpose, such as research, breeding, power production or propulsion.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the general class of reactor types to specific applications._
        - Fission reactor types
        - Research reactors
        - Fast and breeder reactors
        - Power and production reactors
        - Propulsion reactors
      - **This group collects the further nuclear-technology topics of radioactive waste disposal and instrumentation, where the claim's scale is set by the governing constants c, ħ, G and N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Radioactive wastes, waste disposal
        - Instrumentation
    - **Fusion plant hardware and accelerator machines are the engineered devices that confine plasma or raise particle energy.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from fusion reactor design to accelerator machines and their sources._
      - **Fusion reactor design and operation topics concern the plasma physics and fuel handling that determine whether a reactor can reach ignition.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 28.52 listing._
        - Fusion reactors (see also 52.55.-s Magnetic confinement and equilibrium, 52.57.-z Laser inertial confinement, and 52.58.-c Other confinement methods in physics of plasmas; 89.30.Jj Nuclear fusion power in energy resources)
        - Theory, design, and computerized simulation
        - Fueling, heating and ignition
      - **Fusion reactor hardware and safety topics concern the physical materials, components and safeguards that make up a reactor plant.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 28.52 listing._
        - Materials
        - Components and instrumentation
        - Safety (see also 87.55.N- Radiation monitoring, control, and safety in biological and medical physics)
        - Auxiliary generators
      - **Cyclic accelerators are machines that bend charged particles repeatedly around a closed path to raise their energy.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 29.20 listing._
        - Cyclic accelerators and storage rings
        - Storage rings and colliders
        - Betatrons
        - Cyclotrons
        - Synchrotrons
      - **Straight-path accelerators raise particle energy along a single line using static or oscillating electric fields.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 29.20 listing._
        - Accelerators (for accelerators used in medical applications, see 87.56.bd)
        - Electrostatic accelerators
        - Linear accelerators
      - **Particle sources supply the electrons, neutrons and polarized ions that experiments and accelerators use as beams or probes.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS 29.25 listing._
        - Experimental methods and instrumentation for elementary-particle and nuclear physics
        - Particle sources and targets (see also 52.59.-f Intense particle beams and radiation sources in physics of plasmas; 87.56.bg Radioactive sources in medical physics)
        - Electron sources
        - Neutron sources
        - Ion sources: polarized
    - **Beam supply and spectroscopic measurement topics cover producing, transporting and analysing particles by energy.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from sources and beam physics to spectroscopy._
      - **Sources and targets supply the ions, polarized nuclei and radioactive nuclei whose particle numbers N set the scale of the experiment.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from ion sources to targets to radioactive sources._
        - Ion sources: positive and negative
        - Polarized and other targets
        - Sources of radioactive nuclei
        - Nuclear orientation devices
      - **Accelerator beam physics covers the injection, transport, dynamics, characterization and polarization of beams whose speed approaches c.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the general beams heading through injection, dynamics, transport and characteristics to polarized beams._
        - Beams in particle accelerators (for low energy charged-particle beams, see 41.75.-i and 41.85.-p)
        - Beam injection and extraction
        - Beam dynamics; collective effects and instabilities
        - Beam handling; beam transport
      - **Beam characteristics and electron and charged-particle spectrometers measure particle momentum and energy using fields, at scales set by c and ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from beam characteristics and polarized beams to spectrometers._
        - Beam characteristics
        - Polarized beams
        - Spectrometers and spectroscopic techniques (for energy loss and stopping power, see 34.50.Bw in atomic physics, and 61.85.+p in condensed matter physics)
        - Charged-particle spectrometers: electric and magnetic
      - **Spectroscopic techniques identify particles and photons by energy, with electron, charged-particle, neutron, and X- and γ-ray spectroscopy each probing quantum scales set by ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from electron through charged-particle and neutron to X- and γ-ray spectroscopy._
        - Electron spectroscopy
        - Charged-particle spectroscopy
        - Neutron spectroscopy
        - X- and γ-ray spectroscopy
    - **Detection and data topics cover the instruments and methods that register radiation and process the resulting signals.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from facilities and detectors to data handling._
      - **Radioactive-beam facilities and radiation detectors produce, reaccelerate and register exotic nuclei and radiation, with counting statistics governed by N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from radioactive beams in general through fast and reaccelerated beams to detectors._
        - Radioactive beams
        - Fast radioactive beam techniques
        - Reaccelerated radioactive beams
        - Radiation detectors (for mass spectrometers, see 07.75.+h; see also 95.55.Vj Neutrino, muon, pion, and other particle detectors; cosmic ray detectors in astronomy)
      - **Gas-filled and tracking detectors measure charged particles by the ionization they leave in a medium, with the signal scale set by the number N of ion pairs.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from simple counting to position-resolved devices._
        - Gas-filled counters: ionization chambers, proportional, and avalanche counters
        - Tracking and position-sensitive detectors
      - **Light-producing detectors convert particle energy into photons, with the scale set by the speed of light c in the medium or by quantum emission processes involving ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from threshold-velocity (c-dependent) to emission-based devices._
        - Cherenkov detectors
        - Scintillation detectors
        - Nuclear emulsions
        - Calorimeters
        - Solid-state detectors
      - **Data-handling methods process detector output, and their scale is set by the number N of recorded events rather than by physical constants.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the general heading to specific acquisition and analysis tasks._
        - Computer data analysis
        - Data acquisition and sorting
        - Data analysis
- **Atoms and molecules are quantum systems scaled by ħ with particle number, relativistic corrections or the light field.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From particle number to relativistic to bare ħ to light-driven._
  - **Claims whose scale is set by ħ together with a particle number N cover many-electron systems, large chains, clusters, cold gases and controlled quantum systems.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from few-body electron methods to large aggregates and engineered quantum ensembles._
    - **Many-electron methods and topics are claims whose scale is set by ħ together with the number of electrons N.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general topics to specific computational methods._
      - **Electronic-structure calculation methods approximate many-electron quantum systems, where the scale is set by ħ and the electron number N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general techniques to specific correlation methods._
        - Calculations and mathematical techniques in atomic and molecular physics (see also 02.70.-c Computational techniques, in mathematical methods in physics)
        - Ab initio calculations
        - Approximate calculations
        - Density-functional theory
        - Electron correlation calculations for atoms, ions and molecules
      - **Density-functional formalisms express the many-electron problem through the electron density, with the scale set by ħ and the electron number N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from formal foundations to specific functionals and time-dependent extensions, by increasing reliance on N-electron structure._
        - Hohenberg-Kohn theorem and formal mathematical properties, completeness theorems
        - Time-dependent density functional theory
        - Exchange-correlation functionals (in current density functional theory)
        - Spin-density functionals
        - Variational particle-number approach
      - **Correlated wavefunction methods solve the few- or many-electron Schrödinger problem, with the scale set by ħ and the number of electrons N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from high-precision few-body calculations to approximate treatments of larger systems, by how strongly N sets the scale._
        - High-precision calculations for few-electron (or few-body) atomic systems
        - Electronic structure and bonding characteristics
        - Coupled-cluster theory
        - Electron correlation calculations for atoms and ions: ground state
      - **Correlation calculations for excited atomic states and for molecules treat the many-electron problem at a scale set by ħ and the electron number N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: atoms and ions, then diatomic, then polyatomic systems._
        - Electron correlation calculations for atoms and ions: excited states
        - Electron correlation calculations for diatomic molecules
        - Electron correlation calculations for polyatomic molecules
        - Quasiparticle methods
        - Perturbation theory
      - **Numerical and variational schemes solve the Schrodinger equation computationally, at a scale set by ħ and the electron number N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from grid and coordinate methods to self-consistent and simulation methods._
        - Finite-difference schemes
        - Hyperspherical methods
        - Self-consistent-field methods
        - Variational techniques
        - Molecular dynamics and other numerical methods (for simulation techniques for biomolecules, see 87.15.ak, ap)
    - **Macromolecule properties divide into chain-structure features scaled by large N and spectral features scaled by ħ.**
      _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered by structure from chain geometry to spectra._
      - **The chain-structure properties of macromolecules concern mass, sequence, conformation and bond geometry, where large N sets the scale.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general macromolecules to detailed chain properties._
        - Macromolecules and polymer molecules
        - Molecular weights, dispersity
        - Conformation (statistics and dynamics)
        - Constitution (chains and sequences)
        - Configuration (bonds, dimensions)
      - **The spectral properties of macromolecules are the electronic and vibrational structure that depends on ħ.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered by structure from electronic to vibrational and rotational spectra._
        - Electronic structure and spectra
        - Vibrational and rotational structure, infrared and Raman spectra
      - **Scattering processes and quantum computation are quantum phenomena whose scale is set by ħ and phase coherence.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from specific scattering types to quantum computation._
        - Spin dependence of cross sections; polarized beam experiments
        - Coherence and correlation
        - Laser-modified scattering
        - Positron scattering
        - Quantum algorithms & computation
      - **Electron and positron scattering covers collisions of light charged projectiles with atoms and molecules, from elastic scattering to dissociation and recombination.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: general electron and positron scattering, elastic, excitation and ionization, dissociation, then recombination and attachment._
        - Atomic excitation and ionization
        - Molecular excitation and ionization
        - Dissociation and dissociative attachment
        - Recombination, attachment, and positronium formation
    - **Clusters are finite aggregates of N atoms, and their properties divide into equilibrium features and processes of change.**
      _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the definition of clusters, to equilibrium properties, to change processes._
      - **Atomic and molecular clusters are finite aggregates of N atoms whose electronic, magnetic and phase behavior depends on N.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general cluster concept to specific properties._
        - Atomic and molecular clusters (see also 61.46.-w Nanoscale materials in condensed matter)
        - Electronic and magnetic properties of clusters
        - Phase transitions in clusters
      - **Equilibrium properties of clusters are the structural, optical, collective and charge-related features set by electromagnetic constants and the number of atoms N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Grouped by kind of static cluster property_
        - Plasma and collective effects in clusters
        - Spectroscopy and geometrical structure of clusters
        - Optical properties of clusters
        - Charged clusters
      - **Cluster change processes are the reactive, fragmenting and diffusive behaviors in which clusters evolve over time, scaled by ħ and N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Grouped by kind of cluster dynamical process_
        - Reactivity of clusters
        - Stability and fragmentation of clusters
        - Diffusion and dynamics of clusters
      - **Cooling methods are the techniques that reduce the kinetic energy of atoms, molecules and ions, with scales set by ħ and the speed of light c through photon momentum.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Grouped by kind of cooling technique and its umbrella headings_
        - Mechanical control of atoms, molecules, and ions (see also 82.37.Gk STM and AFM manipulations of a single molecule in physical chemistry and chemical physics; for atom manipulation in nanofabrication and processing, see 81.16.Ta; see also 03.75.-b Matter waves)
        - Atom, molecule, and ion cooling methods (see also 87.80.Cc Optical trapping in biophysical techniques)
        - Atom cooling methods
        - Slowing and cooling of molecules
        - Ion cooling
      - **Trapping and light-force methods are the ways of confining or pushing atoms, molecules and ions with fields and light, scaled by ħ and c.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Grouped by kind of confinement or light-force technique_
        - Atom traps and guides
        - Atoms in optical lattices
        - Trapping of molecules
        - Ion trapping
        - Mechanical effects of light on atoms, molecules, and ions
    - **Concepts whose scale is set by ħ together with the number N of particles or controlled quantum systems.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked above ħ alone: the particle number N adds a second scale-setting quantity._
      - **Atomic and molecular fields have their scale set chiefly by ħ together with the particle number N of the bound electrons and nuclei.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the broad atomic field to its specific atomic, molecular and residual sub-fields, with ħ and N dominant throughout._
        - Atomic, molecular and optical physics
        - Atomic and molecular physics
        - Atomic, molecular and optical physics not elsewhere classified
        - Correlation function measurements
        - Velocimetry
      - **Collective and many-body topics are scaled by ħ together with a large particle number N, whose cooperative behavior changes the effective scale of the claim.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from collective atomic effects to the nuclear mass ranges they extend to._
        - Collective effects in atomic physics
        - Nuclear mass ranges
        - Relativistic heavy-ion collisions
        - Total cross sections
      - **Many-body and nuclear concepts have scales set by ħ together with the particle number N of the system.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure._
        - Shell model
        - Nuclear engineering
        - Radioactive beams
        - Complex media
      - **Approximation and statistical methods have a scale set by ħ and by the large particle number N rather than by any single interaction.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Scale set by ħ together with the number of degrees of freedom N._
        - Adiabatic approximation
        - Random matrix theory
        - Thermal & statistical models
        - Super-resolution techniques
      - **Many-body properties and apparatus whose scale depends on particle number N and system size, with ħ and c entering only through the underlying quantum and relativistic physics.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: from nuclear and atomic many-body descriptions to the instruments built to probe them._
        - Nuclear density functional theory
        - Atomic Properties
        - Radiation detectors
        - Particle accelerators
    - **Claims in this group are physics fields or frameworks whose characteristic scale is fixed by a particular combination of the constants c, ħ and G and the particle number N.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **Cold-atom and degenerate-gas fields have a scale set by ħ and a very large particle number N acting as a coherent matter wave.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the general quantum-physics field to the specific gas and matter-wave systems, ħ and N dominant._
        - Quantum physics
        - Degenerate quantum gases and atom optics
        - Cold atoms & matter waves
      - **Quantum information and technology topics are scaled by ħ together with the number N of controlled quantum systems that carry and process information.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the general field to its specific information and technology subfields._
        - Quantum information, computation and communication
        - Quantum technologies
        - Quantum Information, Science & Technology
      - **Fundamental theoretical frameworks whose scale is set chiefly by the universal constants c and ħ, with the weak coupling G_F entering in the electroweak case.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: general quantum field theory first, then its electroweak specialization._
        - Quantum field theory
        - Electroweak interaction
      - **Nuclear and plasma physics has a scale set by ħ and c with large nucleon or particle counts N in bound or ionized matter.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the broad nuclear and plasma field to nuclear physics and the residual category._
        - Nuclear and plasma physics
        - Nuclear physics
        - Nuclear and plasma physics not elsewhere classified
  - **Claims whose scale is set by ħ and the speed of light c cover relativistic corrections, spectral properties and the coupling of light with matter.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from relativistic corrections to spectra and photon coupling._
    - **Relativistic and QED corrections are claims whose scale is set by the speed of light c together with ħ, usually through the fine-structure constant.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general relativistic treatments to specific QED and weak-interaction corrections._
      - **Relativistic treatments bring the speed of light c into the claim, so that its scale is set by c together with ħ through fine-structure corrections.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by how strongly c sets the scale, from fine-structure corrections to full relativistic many-body calculations._
        - Relativistic corrections, spin-orbit effects, fine structure; hyperfine structure
        - Relativistic configuration interaction (CI) and many-body perturbation calculations
      - **Corrections to electronic structure from relativity, QED, hyperfine and isotope effects have scales set by c, through the fine-structure constant, together with ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general heading to relativistic and QED specifics._
        - Corrections to electronic structure (see also 03.30.+p Special relativity; for exotic atoms and molecules, see 36.10.-k; for applications of density-functional theory, see 31.15.es)
        - Hyperfine interactions and isotope effects (see also 32.10.Fn Fine and hyperfine structure)
        - Relativistic and quantum electrodynamic (QED) effects in atoms, molecules, and ions
        - Relativistic corrections to atomic structure and properties
      - **Atomic-structure corrections whose scale is set by the speed of light c together with ħ, covering relativistic and quantum-electrodynamic effects such as negative-energy states, self-energy, and vacuum polarization on level energies.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - QED calculations of level energies, transition frequencies, fine structure intervals (radiative corrections, self-energy, vacuum polarization, etc.)
        - Relativistic corrections due to negative-energy states or processes
      - **Parity- and CP-violating weak-interaction effects in atoms have scales set by the weak coupling together with c and ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - QED corrections to parity nonconserving transition amplitudes and CP violations
        - QED corrections to long-range and weak interactions
        - QED corrections to electric dipole moments and other atomic properties
        - Electron electric dipole moment
      - **Radiative and bound-state QED corrections in exotic and highly charged systems have scales set by c and ħ through the fine-structure constant.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - QED corrections (Lamb shift) in muonic hydrogen and deuterium (see also 36.10.Ee Muonium, muonic atoms and molecules)
        - Corrections to bound-electron g factor
        - Decay rates of hydrogen-antihydrogen quasimolecules (for exotic atoms and molecules, see 36.10.-k)
        - Nonrelativistic limits of Dirac-Fock calculations
        - Higher-order effective Hamiltonians
    - **Spectral and static-property claims are those whose scale is set by ħ and c through level energies and transition frequencies.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from static properties to spectral regions and resonance spectra._
      - **Static atomic properties describe how an atom's energy levels respond to fields and binding, with scales set by ħ, c and the fine-structure constant.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Electric and magnetic moments, polarizabilities
        - Magnetic bound states, magnetic trapping of Rydberg states
        - Fine and hyperfine structure (see also 31.30.Gs Hyperfine interactions and isotope effects)
        - Ionization potentials, electron affinities
      - **Spectral regions classify atomic spectra by the photon energy range, whose scale is set by ħ and c through the transition frequency.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Atomic spectra (see also 78.47.J- Ultrafast spectroscopy (<1 psec) in condensed matter and 82.53.Kp Coherent spectroscopy of atoms and molecules in physical chemistry and chemical physics)
        - Radio-frequency, microwave, and infrared spectra
        - Visible and ultraviolet spectra
        - X-ray spectra
      - **Resonance and magnetic spectra concern transitions between closely spaced levels such as those driven by radio-frequency fields.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Magnetic resonance spectra
        - Intensities and shapes of atomic spectral lines (see also 31.15.-p Calculations and mathematical techniques)
        - Oscillator strengths, lifetimes, transition moments
        - Absolute and relative intensities
        - Line shapes, widths, and shifts
      - **Statistical, semi-empirical and tight-binding approaches approximate many electrons with simplified models whose scale is set by ħ and the particle number N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the most empirical models to the more fundamental statistical ones, by how much N rather than detailed ħ dynamics sets the scale._
        - Alternative approaches
        - Strongly correlated electron systems: generalized tight-binding method
        - Statistical model calculations (including Thomas-Fermi and Thomas-Fermi-Dirac models)
        - Semi-empirical and empirical calculations (differential overlap, Hückel, PPP methods, etc.)
      - **Atomic and molecular structure topics are governed by quantum mechanics, with the scale set by ħ and the number N of electrons.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from broad headings to specific computational methods._
        - ATOMIC AND MOLECULAR PHYSICS
        - Electronic structure of atoms and molecules: theory
        - Atomic & molecular structure
        - Sum rules
        - Confinement-induced resonance
    - **Concepts whose scale is set by ħ and c through the coupling of light with matter and the propagation of photons.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked above ħ with N: the speed of light c enters through radiation coupling._
      - **Laser, photonic and spectroscopic fields have a scale set by ħ and c through light-matter interaction and photon propagation.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from lasers and nonlinear optics through photonics to the terahertz band, each set by ħ and c._
        - Lasers and quantum electronics
        - Nonlinear optics and spectroscopy
        - Photonics, optoelectronics and optical communications
        - Terahertz physics
      - **Light-matter interaction topics are scaled by ħ and c, where photon energy and field coupling determine how atoms respond to radiation.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the general optics field to specific atomic radiation phenomena and approximations._
        - Quantum optics and quantum optomechanics
        - Resonance fluorescence
        - Rotating wave approximation
        - Rare-earth doped crystals
      - **Atom-light interaction concepts have scales set by ħ together with c through radiative couplings and transition matrix elements.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure._
        - Multipole matrix elements
        - Dipole approximation
        - Strong-field approximation
        - Atomic, Molecular & Optical
      - **Fundamental-physics topics are the phenomenological, nuclear-astrophysical and hypothetical-particle subjects whose scales are set by c, ħ and G.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Grouped by kind of fundamental-physics subject_
        - Phenomenology
        - Nuclear astrophysics
        - Hypothetical particles
      - **Speculative high-energy theory concepts have a scale set by c, ħ and G together, the Planck scale where gravity matters.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Highest degree: scale set by c, ħ and G jointly._
        - Naturalness
        - Strings & branes
        - Hypothetical particle physics models
        - Nuclear data analysis & compilation
    - **Concepts whose scale is set by ħ and c because the particles, fields or nucleons involved are relativistic.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked above light-matter: relativistic quantum scales from c and ħ, sometimes with N or G_F._
      - **Particle, field-theory and measurement-technique topics are characterized by relativistic quantum scales set by c and ħ, with G entering at the string-theory extreme.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the broad particle field and particle physics to the Higgs, field theory, and measurement techniques._
        - Particle and high energy physics
        - Particle physics
        - Particle and high energy physics not elsewhere classified
        - Field theory and string theory
        - Higgs bosons
      - **Relativistic particle-physics topics are scaled by ħ and c together, since high energies and fundamental particles require relativistic quantum description.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from fundamental particles and their mediators to beams, targets and collision observables._
        - Gauge bosons
        - Quarks
        - Particle properties
        - Particle beams
        - Particle sources & targets
      - **Relativistic particle and field concepts have scales set jointly by the speed of light c and Planck's constant ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure._
        - Particle phenomena
        - Particles & Fields
        - Leptons
        - Models based on symmetries
      - **Nuclear structure and reaction concepts have a scale set by ħ and c through the strong interaction among a finite number N of nucleons.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Scale set by ħ, c and the nucleon number N._
        - Nuclear many-body theory
        - Nuclear reactions
        - Cluster models
        - Nuclear tests of fundamental interactions
      - **Experimental and data-handling concepts for high-energy particles have a scale set by ħ and c, since the particles are relativistic.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Scale set by ħ and c, with relativistic kinematics dominant._
        - Particle detectors
        - Particle data analysis
        - Hadrons
        - Particle astrophysics
  - **Claims whose scale rests on ħ alone, with system size, symmetry or other constants entering only as corrections, cover formalisms and observables of structure.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from formal structure to the general ħ-only case._
    - **Formalisms and observables of structure are claims whose scale rests on ħ with system size or symmetry structure, rather than on c.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from formal frameworks to measurable properties and surfaces._
      - **Property and application claims report measurable quantities whose scale is set mainly by ħ, with N controlling the size of the system.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from single-atom spectroscopic properties to molecular and condensed applications, by increasing dependence on N._
        - Excitation energies and lifetimes; oscillator strengths
        - Polarizabilities and other atomic and molecular properties
        - Molecule transport characteristics; molecular dynamics; electronic structure of polymers
        - Corrections for core-spin polarization, surface effects, etc
        - Applications of density-functional theory (e.g., to electronic structure and stability; defect formation; dielectric properties, susceptibilities; viscoelastic coefficients; Rydberg transition frequencies)
      - **Semiclassical, path-integral, group-theoretical and valence bond formalisms are built on ħ and on symmetry or classical-limit structure.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from classical-limit and path formalisms to symmetry and bonding formalisms._
        - Semiclassical methods
        - Group-theoretical methods (see also 02.20.-a Group theory in mathematical methods in physics)
        - Path-integral methods
        - Valence bond calculations
      - **Potential energy surfaces and their couplings describe nuclear motion with a scale set by ħ and the particle number N of the molecule.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Potential energy surfaces (for potential energy surfaces for chemical reactions, see 82.20.Kh; for collisions, see 34.20.-b)
        - Potential energy surfaces for ground electronic states
        - Potential energy surfaces for excited electronic states
        - Surface crossings, non-adiabatic couplings
      - **Environmental effects, dynamics, and general atomic properties are scaled by ħ and the number N of interacting particles in the medium or sample.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Effects of atomic and molecular interactions on electronic structure (see also section 34 Atomic and molecular collision processes and interactions)
        - Environmental and solvent effects
        - Time-dependent phenomena: excitation and relaxation processes, and reaction rates (for chemical kinetics aspects, see 82.20.Rp)
        - Atomic properties and interactions with photons (for quantum chaos, see 05.45.Mt; for standards of calibration, see 06.20.fb; for relativistic and quantum electrodynamic effects, see 31.30.J-)
        - Atomic masses, mass spectra, abundances, and isotopes (for mass spectroscopy, see 07.75.+h in instruments, and 82.80.Ms, Nj, Rt in physical chemistry and chemical physics)
      - **Molecular solids and the properties of atoms are further subjects whose claims are set in scale by the governing constants c, ħ, G and N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Molecular solids
        - Properties of atoms (for astrophysical applications, see 95.30.Ky)
    - **Concepts whose scale is fixed by ħ alone, with c, N or classical frameworks entering only as small corrections or approximation parameters.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked lowest: only ħ, the quantum of action, sets the scale._
      - **Foundational and general quantum-theory topics have their scale set by ħ alone, defining the quantum of action and the structure of quantum theory.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the foundations of the theory to its general formal tools and classification._
        - Foundations of quantum mechanics
        - Quantum physics not elsewhere classified
        - Quantum orbits theory
        - Symmetries
      - **Quantum-device and measurement concepts have scales set mainly by ħ, which fixes the quantum limits of control and readout.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure._
        - Qubit architectures
        - Weak measurements
        - Micromechanical & nanomechanical oscillators
        - Structured light
      - **Atomic-scale quantum treatments set their scale mainly by ħ, with c entering only weakly through small relativistic corrections.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Lowest degree: scale fixed essentially by ħ alone._
        - Atomic & molecular processes in external fields
        - Atomic systems
        - Closed-orbit theory
        - Two-level models
      - **Methodological and effective-theory concepts have scales set by the classical or mathematical framework, with ħ and N entering only through approximation parameters.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure._
        - Nonperturbative methods
        - Mathematical physics methods
        - Hydrodynamic models
        - Spectrometers & spectroscopic techniques
  - **Claims about photon-driven and molecular processes have scales set by ħ, c and the light field, covering atomic absorption, molecular spectra and collisions.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from atomic light-driven processes to molecular properties and spectroscopy._
    - **Light-driven atomic processes are claims about how atoms absorb photons or rearrange electrons, with scales set by ħ, c and the light field.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from simple photon absorption to decay, control and nonlinear processes._
      - **Photon-driven excitation and ionization processes are the ways an atom absorbs light to reach excited, ionized or detached states, with scales set by ħ and c.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Photoionization and excitation
        - Inner-shell excitation and ionization
        - Rydberg states
        - Photoionization of atoms and ions (for fluorescence yield, see 32.50.+d)
        - Photodetachment of atomic negative ions
      - **Auger decay and coherent control are processes in which electron rearrangement or controlled light-atom coupling steers atomic transitions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Auger effect (including Coster-Krönig transitions) (for Auger effect in condensed matter physics, see 79.20.Fv)
        - Coherent control of atomic interactions with photons
      - **Nonlinear and resonant photon-atom processes have scales set by ħ and the light intensity, since energy is absorbed in quanta of ħω.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, following the PACS 32.80 sequence._
        - Multiphoton ionization and excitation to highly excited states
        - Other multiphoton processes
        - Level crossing and optical pumping
        - Autoionization
    - **Molecular properties and spectra are claims about molecules and their interaction with radiation, whose scales are fixed by ħ and c, with N entering for ensemble measurements.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the overall framing, to static properties, to response constants, to ensemble measurements, and then to the spectral topic lists._
      - **The top-level headings frame molecular properties and spectra as a whole, covering the scope in which ħ and c govern molecular interactions with photons.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the section heading to the property and spectra headings._
        - Molecular properties and interactions with photons
        - Properties of molecules (see also section 31, Electronic structure of atoms and molecules: theory; for molecules of interest in astrophysics, see 95.30.Ky; for structure and properties of biomolecules, see 87.15.-v)
        - Molecular spectra (see also 78.47.J- Ultrafast spectroscopy (<1 psec) in condensed matter and 82.53.Kp Coherent spectroscopy of atoms and molecules; for chemical analytical methods using spectroscopy, see 82.80.Dx, Gk, Ha in physical chemistry; 87.64.-t Spectroscopic and microscopic techniques in biological physics; for spectra of macromolecules and polymer molecules, see 36.20.Kd)
      - **Geometric and energetic molecular properties have scales fixed by ħ through quantum binding and structure, without any explicit role for c.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, following the PACS 33.15 sequence._
        - General molecular conformation and symmetry; stereochemistry
        - Interatomic distances and angles
        - Bond strengths, dissociation energies
        - Barrier heights (internal rotation, inversion, rotational isomerism, conformational dynamics)
      - **Electromagnetic response and spectroscopic constants of molecules involve ħ together with c through couplings, moments and fine structure.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, following the PACS 33.15 sequence._
        - Electric and magnetic moments (and derivatives), polarizability, and magnetic susceptibility
        - Rotation, vibration, and vibration-rotation constants
        - Fine and hyperfine structure
        - Ionization potentials, electron affinities, molecular core binding energy
