      - **Ensemble, dynamical and spectral measurements bring in N, the number of particles, with timing and photon scattering and absorption scales set by ħ and c.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, following the PACS 33.15 and 33.20 sequence._
        - Mass spectra
        - Correlation times in molecular dynamics
        - Radio-frequency and microwave spectra
        - Infrared spectra
        - Raman and Rayleigh spectra (including optical scattering)
      - **Line-property topics describe the strengths, widths and shapes of molecular spectral lines and bands.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in structure order, from the general field to specific properties._
        - Intensities and shapes of molecular spectral lines and bands
        - Oscillator and band strengths, lifetimes, transition moments, and Franck-Condon factors
        - Absolute and relative line and band intensities
        - Line and band widths, shapes, and shifts
    - **Photon-driven processes, collisions and exotic atoms are quantum interaction phenomena whose scales are set by ħ, field strength and particle masses.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from multiphoton processes, to potentials, to heavy-particle collisions, to light-particle scattering and exotic atoms._
      - **Photon-driven processes are those in which absorbing several photons excites or ionizes an atom or molecule, so that the field strength and ħ set the scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: ionization to highly excited states, then other multiphoton processes._
        - Multiphoton ionization and excitation to highly excited states (e.g., Rydberg states)
        - Other multiphoton processes
        - Elastic scattering
        - Electron and positron scattering
      - **Interaction potentials and forces describe the energy between atoms or molecules as a function of their separation, which sets the scale of all collisions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: general collision field, general potentials, then interatomic and atom-molecule potentials._
        - Atomic and molecular collision processes and interactions (for atomic, molecular, and ionic collisions in plasma, see 52.20.Hv; for atoms and molecules of astrophysical interest, see 95.30.Dr, Ft; see also 98.38.Bn and 98.58.Bz in interstellar media in astronomy; 87.15.K- Molecular interactions, membrane-protein interactions in biological physics)
        - Interatomic and intermolecular potentials and forces, potential energy surfaces for collisions (see also 82.20.Kh Potential energy surfaces for reactions; for potential energy surfaces in electronic structure calculations, see 31.50.-x)
        - Interatomic potentials and forces
        - Intermolecular and atom-molecule potentials and forces
      - **Elastic and energy-transfer scattering covers heavy-particle collisions in which the projectile's energy is redistributed without necessarily changing the species involved.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: general atom and molecule scattering, stopping power, elastic and ultracold, then rotational and vibrational transfer._
        - Scattering of atoms and molecules
        - Energy loss and stopping power
        - Elastic; ultracold collisions
        - Rotational and vibrational energy transfer
      - **Heavy-particle collisions that change the electronic state or chemical identity of the target include excitation, ionization, chemical reactions and laser-modified scattering.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: atomic excitation, molecular excitation, chemical reactions, laser-modified processes._
        - Electronic excitation and ionization of atoms (including beam-foil excitation and ionization)
        - Electronic excitation and ionization of molecules
        - Chemical reactions
        - Laser-modified scattering and reactions
      - **Exotic atoms replace ordinary constituents with unusual particles, so their scale is set by ħ and the particle masses.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general category to specific exotic atom types._
        - Exotic atoms and molecules; macromolecules; clusters
        - Exotic atoms and molecules (containing mesons, antiprotons and other unusual particles)
        - Positronium (see also 82.30.Gg Positronium chemistry)
        - Muonium, muonic atoms and molecules [see also 31.30.jr QED corrections (Lamb shift) in muonic hydrogen and deuterium]
        - Mesonic, hyperonic and antiprotonic atoms and molecules
    - **Molecular spectroscopy topics are grouped by how light and molecules interact, covering the spectral band observed, the molecular motions interpreted, the emission and decay of excited states, and photon-driven changes in molecules.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **Photon-driven process topics cover the ways light interacting with molecules changes their state, ionizes them or breaks them apart.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in structure order, from field-driven effects to ionization and dissociation._
        - Spectra induced by strong-field or attosecond laser irradiation
        - Photon interactions with molecules (see also 42.50.-p Quantum optics)
        - Level crossing and optical pumping
        - Autoionization, photoionization, and photodetachment
        - Diffuse spectra; predissociation, photodissociation
      - **Emission and decay topics cover how excited molecules give off light or lose energy without radiating.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in structure order, from the general field to its specific subtopics._
        - Fluorescence and phosphorescence; radiationless transitions, quenching (intersystem crossing, internal conversion) (for energy transfer, see also section 34; for biophysical applications, see 87.64.kv)
        - Fluorescence and phosphorescence spectra
        - Radiationless transitions, quenching
      - **Spectral-region topics classify molecular spectra by the wavelength band of radiation in which they are observed.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in structure order, from visible to X-ray wavelengths._
        - Visible spectra
        - Ultraviolet spectra
        - Vacuum ultraviolet spectra
        - X-ray spectra
      - **Motion-analysis topics interpret molecular spectra in terms of rotation, vibration and their couplings with electronic motion.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed in structure order, from pure rotation and vibration to coupled motions._
        - Rotational analysis
        - Vibrational analysis
        - Vibration-rotation analysis
        - Vibronic, rovibronic, and rotation-electron-spin interactions
- **Quantum and nonlinear optics and laser devices are light phenomena and sources whose scale is set by ħ or intense fields.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From quantum problems to optics to devices._
  - **Quantum topics are kinds of problems and phenomena whose scale is set by ħ alone or together with other quantities.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from quantum-mechanical problems to quantum-information phenomena._
    - **Quantum-mechanical topics are kinds of problems whose scale is set by ħ, alone or together with other quantities.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from ħ alone to ħ combined with system parameters, c, or N._
      - **General quantum-mechanical frameworks and formalisms are scaled by ħ alone, independent of any particular system.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the broadest heading to the most specific mathematical method._
        - Quantum mechanics [see also 03.67.-a Quantum information; 05.30.-d Quantum statistical mechanics; 31.30.J- Relativistic and quantum electrodynamics (QED) effects in atoms, molecules, and ions in atomic physics]
        - Quantum systems with finite Hilbert space
        - Formalism
        - Functional analytical methods
        - Algebraic methods (see also 02.20.-a Group theory)
      - **Wave-equation and scattering problems in quantum mechanics are scaled by ħ together with system parameters, and by c when relativistic.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from bound states to scattering to relativistic wave equations._
        - Solutions of wave equations: bound states
        - Scattering theory
        - Relativistic wave equations
      - **Single-system quantum mechanics topics whose scale is set by ħ, covering the semiclassical limit, foundations, phases, state reconstruction and tunneling.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_
        - Semiclassical theories and applications
        - Foundations of quantum mechanics; measurement theory (for optical tests of quantum theory, see 42.50.Xa)
        - Phases: geometric; dynamic or topological
        - State reconstruction, quantum tomography
        - Tunneling, traversal time, quantum Zeno dynamics
      - **Multi-system topics where ħ and the number of systems N together set the scale, covering entanglement, decoherence and quantum information.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_
        - Entanglement and quantum nonlocality (e.g. EPR paradox, Bell's inequalities, GHZ states, etc.) (for entanglement production and manipulation, see 03.67.Bg; for entanglement measures, witnesses etc., see 03.67.Mn; for entanglement in Bose-Einstein condensates, see 03.75.Gg)
        - Decoherence; open systems; quantum statistical methods (see also 03.67.Pp in quantum information; for decoherence in Bose-Einstein condensates, see 03.75.Gg)
        - Quantum information (see also 42.50.Dv Quantum state engineering and measurements; 42.50.Ex Optical implementations of quantum information processing and transfer in quantum optics)
        - Quantum algorithms, protocols, and simulations
        - Entanglement production and manipulation (for entanglement in Bose-Einstein condensates, see 03.75.Gg)
    - **Quantum-information and matter-wave topics are kinds of quantum phenomena of atoms, photons and condensates.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **Quantum information topics cover the communication, computation, entanglement and error-protection tasks that use quantum states.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS code sequence._
        - Quantum cryptography and communication security
        - Quantum communication
        - Quantum computation architectures and implementations
        - Entanglement measures, witnesses, and other characterizations (see also 03.65.Ud Entanglement and quantum nonlocality; 42.50.Dv Quantum state engineering and measurements in quantum optics)
        - Quantum error correction and other methods for protection against decoherence (see also 03.65.Yz Decoherence; open systems; quantum statistical methods; for decoherence in Bose-Einstein condensates, see 03.75.Gg)
      - **Matter-wave optics topics treat atoms and neutrons as waves that can be steered and made to interfere.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS code sequence._
        - Matter waves (for atom interferometry, see 37.25.+k; see also 67.85.-d ultracold gases, trapped gases in quantum fluids and solids)
        - Atom and neutron optics
        - Atom and neutron interferometry
      - **Condensate property topics describe the static, dynamic, topological, spinor and entanglement behaviour of Bose-Einstein condensates.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS code sequence._
        - Entanglement and decoherence in Bose-Einstein condensates
        - Static properties of condensates; thermodynamical, statistical, and structural properties
        - Dynamic properties of condensates; collective and hydrodynamic excitations, superfluid flow
        - Tunneling, Josephson effect, Bose-Einstein condensates in periodic potentials, solitons, vortices, and topological excitations (see also 74.50.+r Tunneling phenomena; Josephson effects in superconductivity)
        - Multicomponent condensates; spinor condensates
      - **Other degenerate quantum gas topics include miscellaneous condensation phenomena, atom lasers and degenerate Fermi gases.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS code sequence._
        - Other Bose-Einstein condensation phenomena
        - Atom lasers
        - Degenerate Fermi gases
  - **Quantum and nonlinear optics are kinds of light phenomena where ħ, N or intense fields set the scale.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from quantum optics to nonlinear effects._
    - **Quantum optics topics are those where ħ, with the number N of atoms or photons, sets the scale of the claim.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from light statistics to interaction to effects._
      - **Quantum optics topics about the statistics, states and fluctuations of light, whose scale is set by ħ through the photon quantum.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Photon statistics and coherence theory
        - Quantum state engineering and measurements (see also 03.65.Ud Entanglement and quantum nonlocality, e.g., EPR paradox, Bells inequalities, GHZ states, etc.)
        - Optical implementations of quantum information processing and transfer
        - Quantum fluctuations, quantum noise, and quantum jumps
        - Quantum description of interaction of light and matter; related experiments
      - **Quantum optics topics of light-matter interaction and cooperative behavior, whose scale is set by ħ together with the number N of atoms or photons involved.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Effects of atomic coherence on propagation, absorption, and amplification of light; electromagnetically induced transparency and absorption
        - Strong-field excitation of optical transitions in quantum systems; multiphoton processes; dynamic Stark shift (for multiphoton ionization and excitation of atoms and molecules, see 32.80.Rm, and 33.80.Rv, respectively)
        - Optical transient phenomena: quantum beats, photon echo, free-induction decay, dephasings and revivals, optical nutation, and self-induced transparency
        - Quantum optical phenomena in absorbing, amplifying, dispersive and conducting media; cooperative phenomena in quantum optical systems
        - Cavity quantum electrodynamics; micromasers
      - **Quantum optical effects are phenomena where the quantum of action ħ sets the scale of light's interference, momentum and measurement.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the most purely quantum (ħ-set) effect to the more mechanical one._
        - Optical tests of quantum theory
        - Nonclassical interferometry, subwavelength lithography
        - Optical angular momentum and its quantum aspects (see also 42.25.Ja Polarization)
        - Mechanical effects of light on material media, microstructures and particles (see also 87.80.Cc Optical trapping in biology and medicine)
    - **Nonlinear optics topics describe how intense light drives material response, scattering and beam conversion.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from material response to propagation._
      - **Nonlinear scattering and susceptibility topics describe how intense light drives material responses and scattering.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered as in the PACS 42.65 listing._
        - Nonlinear optics
        - Optical susceptibility, hyperpolarizability [see also 33.15.Kr Electric and magnetic moments (and derivatives), polarizability, and magnetic susceptibility]
        - Stimulated Raman scattering; CARS (for Raman lasers, see 42.55.Ye)
        - Stimulated Brillouin and Rayleigh scattering
      - **Nonlinear propagation and conversion topics describe how intense beams change shape, phase and frequency in a medium.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered as in the PACS 42.65 listing._
        - Phase conjugation; photorefractive and Kerr effects
        - Beam trapping, self-focusing and defocusing; self-phase modulation
        - Frequency conversion; harmonic generation, including higher-order harmonic generation (see also 42.79.Nv Optical frequency converters)
  - **Lasers and optical devices are kinds of light sources, systems and components classified by type, operation and guiding structure.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from laser types to theory to devices and fiber systems._
    - **Laser types are classified by gain medium, cavity structure or special pumping and scattering mechanism.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from gain medium to cavity to mechanism._
      - **Gain-medium lasers are classified by the material in which stimulated emission occurs, such as gas, dye, chemical, semiconductor or solid.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Chemical lasers (for chemiluminescence, see 78.60.Ps)
        - Gas lasers including excimer and metal-vapor lasers
        - Dye lasers
        - Semiconductor lasers; laser diodes
        - Doped-insulator lasers and other solid state lasers
      - **Structured-cavity lasers are defined by the resonator or scattering geometry that confines light, from microcavities to random media.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Microcavity and microdisk lasers
        - Photonic crystal lasers and coherent effects
        - Fiber lasers
        - Random lasers
      - **Special-mechanism lasers rely on a particular pumping or scattering process, such as Raman gain, diode pumping or short-wavelength emission.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - X- and γ-ray lasers
        - Raman lasers (see also 42.65.Dr Stimulated Raman scattering; CARS)
        - Diode-pumped lasers
    - **Laser theory, operation, dynamics and applications cover how lasers are designed, run and used.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from theory to operation, dynamics and application._
      - **General laser theory and design topics cover the overall principles and engineering of laser systems rather than a specific type.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Lasers
        - General laser theory
        - Laser optical systems: design and operation
        - Design of specific laser systems
      - **Laser operation topics cover how the cavity, gain and output of a laser are built and controlled in time.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered as in the PACS 42.60 listing._
        - Resonators, cavities, amplifiers, arrays, and rings
        - Modulation, tuning, and mode locking
        - Q-switching
        - Beam characteristics: profile, intensity, and power; spatial pattern formation
        - Efficiency, stability, gain, and other operational parameters
      - **Laser dynamics and regime topics cover steady, unstable and relaxing operating behavior of lasers.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered as in the PACS 42.60 listing._
        - Dynamical laser instabilities; noisy laser behavior
        - Continuous operation
        - Relaxation oscillations and long pulse operation
      - **Laser application topics are the fields in which laser light is put to practical or measurement use.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered as in the PACS 42.62 listing._
        - Laser applications
        - Biological and medical applications (see also 87.50.W-, 87.63.L-, and 87.80.Cc in biological and medical physics)
        - Industrial applications
        - Metrological applications; optical frequency synthesizers for precision spectroscopy (see also 06.20.-f Metrology in metrology, measurements, and laboratory procedures)
        - Laser spectroscopy
    - **Optical sources and devices are kinds of components that emit, shape, process or sense light.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_
      - **Optical sources are devices that emit infrared, visible and ultraviolet light.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by PACS code within 42.72._
        - Optical sources and standards (for lasers, see 42.55.-f)
        - Infrared sources (see also 07.57.Hm Infrared, submillimeter wave, microwave, and radiowave sources)
        - Visible and ultraviolet sources
      - **Passive optical elements shape, filter, split or steer light by geometry and material properties alone.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the passive element._
        - Apertures, collimators
        - Lenses, prisms and mirrors
        - Filters, zone plates, and polarizers
        - Gratings (for holographic gratings, see 42.40.Eq)
        - Reflectors, beam splitters, and deflectors
      - **Active or functional optical devices collect, guide, process, modulate or display light for a system purpose.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the device system._
        - Solar collectors and concentrators (see also 88.40.F- Solar concentrators in solar energy)
        - Optical waveguides and couplers (for fiber waveguides and waveguides in integrated optics, see 42.81.Qb and 42.82.Et, respectively)
        - Optical processors, correlators, and modulators
        - Acousto-optical devices (see also 43.38.Zp—in Acoustics Appendix)
        - Display devices, liquid-crystal devices (see also 85.60.Pg Display systems)
      - **Imaging and sensing devices capture or measure light fields, with speed c setting timing and range scales.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Grouped by function as devices that image or sense light._
        - Scanners, image intensifiers, and image converters (see also 85.60.-q Optoelectronic devices)
        - Schlieren devices
        - Imaging detectors and sensors (see also 85.60.Gz Photodetectors)
        - Range finders, remote sensing devices; laser Doppler velocimeters, SAR, and LIDAR (see also 42.68.Wt Remote sensing; LIDAR and adaptive systems)
        - Gradient-index (GRIN) devices (for fiber GRIN devices, see 42.81.Ht)
      - **Devices that convert, carry, process, store or coat with light, where photon energy ħω and c set the scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the most direct dependence on photon energy (frequency conversion) to the least (coatings)._
        - Optical frequency converters
        - Optical communication systems, multiplexers, and demultiplexers (for fiber networks, see 42.81.Uv)
        - Optical computers, logic elements, interconnects, switches; neural networks
        - Optical storage systems, optical disks (see also 42.40.Ht Hologram recording and readout methods)
        - Optical coatings
    - **Fiber and integrated optics topics cover guided-light components, systems and their characterization.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. structure_
      - **Fiber fundamentals cover how optical fibers are made, measured, and how light propagates and is polarized in them.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from the general heading through fabrication, measurement, propagation and polarization._
        - Fiber optics
        - Fabrication, cladding, and splicing
        - Fiber testing and measurement of fiber parameters
        - Propagation, scattering, and losses; solitons
        - Birefringence, polarization
      - **Fiber devices and systems are the functional components and networks built from optical fibers.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from individual devices to couplers and networks._
        - Gradient-index (GRIN) fiber devices
        - Sensors, gyros
        - Fiber waveguides, couplers, and arrays
        - Fiber networks (see also 42.79.Sz Optical communication systems, multiplexers, and demultiplexers)
        - Other fiber-optical devices (for fiber lasers, see 42.55.Wd)
      - **Integrated optics covers the design, fabrication, interconnection and waveguiding of optical circuits on a chip.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from the general heading through design, fabrication, interconnects and waveguides._
        - Integrated optics
        - Design and performance testing of integrated-optical systems
        - Fabrication techniques; lithography, pattern transfer (see also 85.40.-e Microelectronics: LSI, VLSI, ULSI; integrated circuit fabrication technology)
        - Interconnects, including holographic interconnects (see also 42.79.Ta Optical computers, logic elements, interconnects, switches; neural networks)
        - Waveguides, couplers, and arrays (for fiber waveguides, see 42.81.Qb)
      - **Hybrid and other integrated systems together with optical testing and property characterization form the supporting and miscellaneous topics.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from integrated hybrids to testing and general properties._
        - Hybrid systems
        - Other integrated-optical elements and systems
        - Optical testing techniques
        - Phase shifting interferometry (for interferometers, see 07.60.Ly in instruments)
        - Properties

### 3. Gravitation and the cosmos is the regime where G, with c, sets the scale, covering gravity, planets, stars, galaxies and the Earth and space environment.

- **Gravity and relativity claims have scales set by c and G, from classical gravity to quantum gravity.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From classical to quantum gravity._
  - **Claims in classical gravity and relativity have their scale set by c and G, with masses, N or cosmic extent, and no role for ħ.**
    _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from c and G alone, to matter-dependent scales, to whole-universe classical scales._
    - **Systems whose observable scale depends on c and G together with the masses or the number N of the bodies involved are set by matter content.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by the constants involved: c and G plus the masses or number N of the bodies._
      - **Numerical studies of specific relativistic systems, such as black holes, binaries and critical collapse, have scales set by c and G together with the masses N of the bodies involved.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from singular behavior to binary systems._
        - Numerical studies of critical behavior, singularities, and cosmic censorship
        - Numerical studies of black holes and black-hole binaries
        - Numerical studies of other relativistic binaries (see also 97.80.-d Binary and multiple stars in astronomy)
      - **Gravitational waves and astronomical mass distributions are observable phenomena whose amplitudes and frequencies scale with G, c and the masses of the sources.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from generation to propagation to astrophysical use._
        - Gravitational waves (see also 04.80.Nn Gravitational wave detectors and experiments)
        - Wave generation and sources
        - Wave propagation and interactions
        - Gravitational-wave astrophysics (see also 95.85.Sz Gravitational radiation, magnetic fields, and other observations in astronomy)
        - Astronomical masses & mass distributions
      - **Many-body stellar and galactic systems have their scale set by the number N of gravitating bodies they contain.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the smallest systems to the largest._
        - Globular clusters
        - Galaxy clusters
        - Galactic astronomy
        - Stellar astronomy and planetary systems
        - Composition of astronomical objects
    - **Classical extensions, experimental tests and cosmological theories apply or modify G and c over the scale of the whole universe while remaining classical.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by the constants involved: c and G, with G modified or applied at experimental and universal scales._
      - **Theories that extend or modify classical gravity change the effective form or scale of G, for example through extra dimensions, while remaining classical.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Second degree: still classical, but G or the number of dimensions is altered._
        - Higher-dimensional gravity and other theories of gravity (see also 11.25.Mj Compactification and four-dimensional models, 11.25.Uv D branes)
        - Kaluza-Klein theories
        - Higher-dimensional black holes, black strings, and related objects
        - Modified theories of gravity
      - **Experimental tests and applications of gravity and relativity have their scale set by the speed of light c together with Newton's constant G.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the test category to the instrument and applied cases._
        - Experimental tests of gravitational theories
        - Gravitational wave detectors and experiments (see also 95.55.Ym Gravitational radiation detectors; mass spectrometers; and other instrumentation and techniques)
        - Global Positioning System
        - General relativity and gravitational waves
      - **Broad theories of gravitation and cosmology set their scale with G and c at the level of the whole universe.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the broadest field to the specific subfields._
        - Gravitation, Cosmology & Astrophysics
        - Gravitation
        - Cosmology
        - Cosmology and extragalactic astronomy
    - **Geomagnetism fields study the Earth's magnetic field and its rock record with no scale set by c, ħ, G or N.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Kinds are distinguished by how the field is studied: overall origin, classification by records and changes, or rock magnetization._
      - **Geomagnetic topics concern the origin, structure, variation and recorded history of the Earth's magnetic field and of magnetism in rocks.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from field generation to spatial and temporal variations and records._
        - Geomagnetism and paleomagnetism; geoelectricity (see also 91.50.Iv Marine magnetics and electromagnetics)
        - Origins and models of the magnetic field; dynamo theories
        - Interactions between exterior sources and interior properties
        - Rock and mineral magnetism (see also 91.60.Pn Magnetic and electrical properties—in Physical properties of rocks and minerals)
        - Magnetic field reversals: process and timescale
      - **Geomagnetism subfields are classed by how the Earth's magnetic field is studied across past records, time changes and spatial patterns, with no scale set by c, ħ, G or N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Archeomagnetism
        - Time variations in geomagnetism
        - Spatial variations in geomagnetism
      - **Rock-magnetic record studies use the magnetization preserved in rocks and sediments to date and interpret Earth's past magnetic field.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from remanence acquisition to its dating and later alteration._
        - Paleomagnetism
        - Magnetostratigraphy
        - Remagnetization
        - Geomagnetic excursion
    - **Seafloor and marine geology covers the coastal, intra-plate, sediment and sampling topics concerning the ocean floor and its margins.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from broad approaches to margins, seafloor features, sediment systems and sampling methods._
      - **Earth evolution, tomographic imaging and marine geology are broad approaches to studying the history and structure of the Earth and its ocean floor.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from evolution and imaging to marine geology._
        - Evolution of the Earth
        - Tomography of plate tectonics (see also 91.30.Jk—in Seismology)
        - Marine geology and geophysics
      - **Nearshore and margin processes are those that shape coasts, beaches, shelves and slopes where land meets the sea.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the shoreline seaward across the margin._
        - Beach and coastal processes
        - Littoral processes
        - Continental shelf and slope processes
        - Submarine landslides
      - **Geophysical and sampling methods are the measurements and records used to probe the structure, field properties and history of the seafloor.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from potential-field and thermal measurements to drilling and fossil records._
        - Marine magnetics and electromagnetics
        - Gravity and isostasy
        - Heat flow (benthic)
        - Ocean drilling (see also 93.85.Tf Oil prospecting, pipelines, and conduits)
        - Micropaleontology
    - **Seafloor and marine geology covers the coastal, intra-plate, sediment and sampling topics concerning the ocean floor and its margins.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **Intra-plate seafloor features are volcanic and structural landforms of the ocean floor that lie away from active plate boundaries.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from volcanic features to broad plateaus and general seafloor morphology._
        - Oceanic hotspots and intra-plate volcanism (see also 91.40.Ta—in Volcanology; 91.67.fh—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Oceanic plateaus and fracture zone processes
        - Seafloor morphology, geology, and geophysics (see also 92.10.Oc Benthic boundary layers, ocean bottom processes—in oceanography)
        - Bathymetry, seafloor topology
      - **Sediment and fluid systems are processes that move, store or circulate sediments, gases and water within the seabed.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from surface sediment transport to subsurface fluids and gas._
        - Marine sediments, turbidity currents—processes and transport (see also 91.65.Ti—in petrology; 91.67.Ty—in Geochemistry; 92.10.Wa and 92.20.Vn—in oceanography; 92.40.Gc—in hydrology; 91.80.Wx—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Gas and hydrate systems (see also 92.20.Uv—in oceanography)
        - Marine hydrogeology
  - **Claims in quantum gravity and cosmic fields have their scale set by the Planck scale and quantum effects through ħ together with c and G.**
    _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the pure Planck-scale case to the broader astrophysical case also involving c._
    - **Quantum gravity and its phenomenology have their scale set by the Planck scale, built from c, ħ and G, so that ħ enters the claim.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by the constants involved: c, ħ and G together through the Planck scale._
      - **Experimental and phenomenological studies connect gravity to measurable scales, including the Planck-scale effects built from c, ħ and G.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Fourth degree: ħ enters through observable consequences, with N for many-particle or many-field effects._
        - Phenomenology of quantum gravity
        - Experimental studies of gravity
        - Loop quantum gravity, quantum geometry, spin foams
        - Quantum aspects of black holes, evaporation, thermodynamics
      - **Quantum gravity programs quantize the gravitational field itself, with the Planck scale built from c, ħ and G setting the claim.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Highest degree: full quantum gravity requiring c, ħ and G together._
        - Quantum gravity (see also 11.25.-w Strings and branes; 11.15.Wx Topologically massive gauge theories, and 11.15.Yc Chern-Simons gauge theory)
        - Gravitational aspects of string theory
        - Covariant and sum-over-histories quantization
        - Lattice and discrete methods
        - Topologically massive gravity (see also 11.15.Wx Topologically massive gauge theories, and 11.15.Yc Chern-Simons gauge theory)
      - **Approaches to quantum gravity that are distinguished by which governing constants among c, ħ, G and N set the scale of their claims, here the canonical quantization of gravity and its reduced lower-dimensional and minisuperspace models.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Lower dimensional models; minisuperspace models
        - Canonical quantization
    - **Astrophysical and cosmic fields have their scale set by the speed of light c, the Planck scale, and quantum effects through ħ.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Kinds are distinguished by the scale-setting constants c and ħ, from compact objects to solar-terrestrial plasma._
      - **Compact and high-energy astrophysical objects have their scale set by G, c and quantum effects through ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the compact object to the high-energy processes around it._
        - Astronomical black holes
        - High energy astrophysics and galactic cosmic rays
      - **Astronomy and astroparticle fields study distant cosmic objects and particles, where the speed of light c and the Planck scale set the relevant scales.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general astronomy to particle and radiation observation._
        - Astronomical sciences not elsewhere classified
        - Astroparticle physics and particle cosmology
        - Cosmic rays & astroparticles
        - Distances, redshifts, & velocities
        - Electromagnetic radiation astronomy
      - **Solar-terrestrial fields study the Sun's influence on near-Earth space, where electromagnetic fields and plasma physics with the speed of light c set the scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from solar activity outward to Earth's upper atmosphere and magnetosphere._
        - Heliophysics and space weather
        - Mesospheric, thermospheric, ionospheric and magnetospheric physics
        - Solar system energetic particles
        - Astrophysical electromagnetic fields
    - **Gravity-scaled Earth and planetary fields have their scale set mainly by the gravitational constant G through the shape, gravity field and motion of bodies.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Kinds are distinguished by which aspect of gravity they treat: orbits, shape, potential, or motion._
      - **Orbital and planetary fields concern the motion of bodies in the solar system and the solid Earth, governed mainly by Newtonian gravity G.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from orbital dynamics to planets and the solid Earth._
        - Astrodynamics and space situational awareness
        - Solar system planetary science (excl. planetary geology)
        - Solid Earth physics
      - **Geodesy and gravity fields measure and model the shape and gravity of the Earth, with the gravitational constant G setting the scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general theory to mapping and satellite applications._
        - Geodesy and gravity (see also 91.50.Kx Gravity and isostasy—in Marine geology and geophysics; 91.45.gh—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Mathematical geodesy; general theory
        - Cartography
        - Space and satellite geodesy; applications of global positioning systems
      - **Geometric and positional topics describe the shape, surface and coordinate framework of the Earth through observation and reference definitions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from surface observation to reference frames._
        - Topography; geometric observations
        - Photogrammetry
        - Reference systems
        - Ocean/Earth/atmosphere/ hydrosphere/cryosphere interactions; mass balance
        - Global change from geodesy
      - **Gravity-field topics concern the gravitational potential of the Earth, its anomalies, harmonics and the techniques that measure them.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from measurement techniques to anomalies to potential theory._
        - Geodetic techniques; gravimetric measurements and instruments
        - Gravity anomalies; time variable gravity
        - Harmonics of the gravity potential field; geopotential theory and determination
      - **Dynamical motion topics concern how the solid Earth and orbiting bodies move, deform and respond to gravitational forces.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from crustal deformation to rotation, tides and orbits._
        - Crustal movements and deformation
        - Rotational variations; polar wobble (see also 92.10.Iv Ocean influence of Earth's rotation)
        - Earth tides
        - Satellite orbits
    - **Seismological claims about earthquakes and elastic waves have their scale set by the mechanics of elastic media rather than by the fundamental constants c, ħ, G or N.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general seismology and wave propagation to tectonic settings, events and layered-Earth studies._
      - **Seismology studies the sources, propagation, and modeling of elastic waves and earthquakes in Earth.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general theory to sources, wave types, and seismicity._
        - Seismology
        - Theory and modeling, computational seismology
        - Seismic sources (mechanisms, magnitude, moment frequency spectrum)
        - Surface waves and free oscillations
      - **Seismicity and body wave propagation are further seismological topics whose claims are set by the mechanics of elastic media and not by the fundamental constants c, ħ, G or N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Seismicity (see also 91.45.gd—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Body wave propagation
      - **Plate-boundary settings are tectonic zones where lithospheric plates converge, diverge, or slide past each other.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by type of plate boundary: convergent, divergent, transform._
        - Subduction zones (see also 91.40.Rs—in Volcanology; 91.45.Hc—in Tectonophysics; 91.50.Wy—in Marine geology and geophysics; 91.67.fc—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Mid-ocean ridges (see also 91.40.St—in Volcanology; 91.50.Rt—in Marine geology and geophysics; 91.67.ff—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Transform faults
        - Tomography in seismology (see also 91.35.Pn Tomography of the Earth's interior)
        - Volcano seismology
      - **Seismic events and their records are the ground-shaking phenomena, whether natural or man-made, and the traces they leave in time.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from strong shaking to specific event sources and past events._
        - Strong motions and shock waves
        - Tsunamis (see also 92.10.hl—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Earthquakes
        - Nuclear explosion seismology
        - Paleoseismology
      - **Seismology of Earth's layers uses seismic waves to study the structure of particular regions of the Earth, from the crust down to the core.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the surface crust downward to the deep interior._
        - Oceanic crust seismology
        - Continental crust seismology
        - Lithosphere seismology (see also 91.45.gf—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Core and mantle seismology
    - **Volcanic claims concern molten rock and its eruption, with scale set by the physics and chemistry of magma rather than by the fundamental constants.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the magma body itself to the dynamics of its eruption._
      - **Magma-body topics cover the physical, chemical and thermodynamic behaviour of molten rock and its chemical modeling.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from chemical modeling to magma bodies, thermodynamics and phase equilibria._
        - Geochemical modeling
        - Physics and chemistry of magma bodies
        - Thermodynamics in volcanology
        - Reactions and phase equilibria (see also 91.67.De—in Geochemistry)
      - **Eruption-dynamics topics cover how magma moves, erupts and flows as lava and what ash and atmospheric effects it leaves.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from magma migration through eruption and lava to ash deposits and atmospheric effects._
        - Magma migration
        - Eruption mechanisms
        - Lava rheology and morphology
        - Tephrochronology; ash deposits
        - Atmospheric effects (see also 92.60.Mt Particles and aerosols—in Meteorology)
  - **Further claims in this cell are sorted by which governing constants among c, ħ, G and N set their scale, covering solid-Earth settings, rock magnetism and classical relativity.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure_
    - **Volcanic and tectonic settings are the plate-scale environments, boundaries and volcanic materials where lithosphere is created, deformed or erupted.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general topics to specific products, plate-scale regions and boundary settings._
      - **Tectonic-setting topics cover volcanic and hydrothermal processes tied to specific plate-tectonic environments.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general volcanology heading and hydrothermal systems to subduction, ridge and intra-plate settings._
        - Volcanology (see also 91.30.Tb Volcano seismology)
        - Hydrothermal systems (see also 91.67.Jk—in Geochemistry; 92.05.Lf—in oceanography)
        - Subduction zone processes (see also 91.30.Ga—in Seismology; 91.45.Hc—in Tectonophysics; 91.50.Wy—in Marine geology; 91.67.fc—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Mid-oceanic ridge processes (see also 91.30.Hc—in Seismology; 91.50.Rt—in Marine geology; 91.67.ff—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Intra-plate processes (see also 91.50.Tb—in Marine geology; 91.67.fh—in Geophysics Appendix)
      - **Volcanic products and structures are the physical materials and landforms that volcanic eruptions leave behind.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from deposits and gases to calderas._
        - Volcanoclastic deposits
        - Volcanic gases
        - Calderas
        - Remote sensing of volcanoes (see also 93.85.Pq)
        - Volcano monitoring; volcanic hazards and risks
      - **Plate-scale tectonic settings are the large-scale regions and boundaries where the lithosphere deforms and volcanism is concentrated.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from continents and plates to subduction and volcanic arcs._
        - Continental tectonics
        - Plate tectonics
        - Subduction and obduction zone processes (see also 91.30.Ga—in Seismology; 91.40.Rs—in Volcanology)
        - Volcanic arcs
      - **Plate-boundary settings are tectonic environments where oceanic lithosphere is created, consumed or extended at plate margins.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from ridge creation through subduction to back-arc extension and ophiolite emplacement._
        - Mid-ocean ridge processes (see also 91.30.Hc—in Seismology; 91.40.St—in Volcanology; 91.67.ff—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Subduction zone processes
        - Back-arc basin processes
        - Ophiolites
    - **Magnetic mineral and fabric studies, together with field description and anomaly studies, address Earth's magnetism and its record in rocks.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the magnetic mineral record in rocks to the mapped geomagnetic field._
      - **Magnetic mineral and fabric studies treat the magnetic minerals and directional properties of materials as indicators of their origin and environment.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from mineral origin to environmental and anisotropy applications._
        - Biogenic magnetic minerals
        - Environmental magnetism
        - Magnetic fabrics and anisotropy
        - Geoelectricity, electromagnetic induction, and telluric currents
        - Geomagnetic induction
      - **Field description and anomaly studies map and model Earth's magnetic field, its reference models, harmonic components, and local anomalies including seafloor stripes, and link them to core processes.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from global reference fields to anomalies and their core source._
        - Magnetic anomalies; modeling and interpretations
        - Reference fields: regional; global
        - Core processes
        - Harmonics and anomalies
        - Attributed to seafloor spreading
    - **Classical relativity theory, its spacetime structure, solution methods and self-gravitating systems, has its scale set by c and G alone, with no role for ħ or N.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by the constants involved: only c and G enter, with no matter-number or quantum scale._
      - **Framework-level topics of general relativity are scaled by c and G alone and define the general theory itself, with no matter-number or quantum scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the whole field to its formalism._
        - General relativity and gravitation (for astrophysical aspects, see 95.30.Sf Relativity and gravitation; for relativistic aspects of cosmology, see 98.80.Jk; for special relativity, see 03.30.+p)
        - Classical general relativity (see also 02.40.-k Geometry, differential geometry, and topology)
        - Fundamental problems and general formalism
        - Canonical formalism, Lagrangians, and variational principles
      - **Questions about the structure of spacetime, its causal and asymptotic properties and its singularities, are set by the geometric scale GM/c^2 and mark where classical theory breaks down, where ħ would enter.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from causal structure to singularities._
        - Singularities and cosmic censorship
        - Spacetime topology, causal structure, spinor structure
        - Asymptotic structure
      - **Methods for solving the field equations exactly, as an initial value problem, or numerically are set by c and G, with the scale fixed by the mass of the chosen system.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from analytic to numerical solution methods._
        - Initial value problem, existence and uniqueness of solutions
        - Exact solutions
        - Approximation methods; equations of motion
        - Numerical relativity
        - Post-Newtonian approximation; perturbation theory; related approximations
      - **Classical self-gravitating systems and black holes have their scale fixed by c and G alone, with no role for ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Lowest degree: purely classical, scale set by c and G only._
        - Self-gravitating systems; continuous media and classical fields in curved spacetime
        - Relativistic stars: structure, stability, and oscillations (see also 97.60.-s Late stages of stellar evolution)
        - Einstein-Maxwell spacetimes, spacetimes with fluids, radiation or classical fields
        - Physics of black holes (see also 97.60.Lf—in astronomy)
        - Classical black holes
  - **Claims about space and Earth science are sorted by whether their scale is set by Newtonian gravity through G or by system-specific material and environmental quantities rather than a fundamental constant.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure_
    - **General disciplinary and umbrella headings frame space and geophysical science through system-specific quantities rather than one fundamental constant.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Kinds are distinguished by being broad frames rather than specific phenomena with a fundamental scale._
      - **The astronomy discipline and its local subfields have their scale set by system-specific quantities rather than one fundamental constant.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the discipline as a whole to its subfields._
        - Astronomical sciences
        - Astrobiology
        - Astronomical instrumentation
        - Planetary science (excl. solar system and planetary geology)
        - Solar physics
      - **Broad umbrella headings and instrumentation categories frame space and geophysical science as a whole rather than a specific physical phenomenon.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from umbrella headings to residual and instrument categories._
        - Space sciences
        - Space sciences not elsewhere classified
        - Space instrumentation
        - GEOPHYSICS, ASTRONOMY, AND ASTROPHYSICS (for more detailed headings, see the Geophysics Appendix)
    - **Gravity-governed interior dynamics are the deep-Earth processes and deformation mechanics whose scale is set by Newtonian gravity through G.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from deep interior processes to the gravity claim and crustal deformation mechanics._
      - **Deep Earth processes are the interior dynamics, heat flow and forces that drive tectonics and mantle-sourced volcanism.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from interiors and convection to stresses and evolution._
        - Tectonophysics
        - Convection currents and mantle plumes
        - Dynamics and mechanics of tectonics
        - Hot spots, large igneous provinces, and flood basalt volcanism
        - Heat generation and transport
      - **Claims about the deep Earth and planetary interiors, such as core processes, interior structure and tectonic stresses, are governed mainly by Newtonian gravity through G, and are largely unaffected by c, ħ or N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Core processes
        - Planetary interiors (see also 96.12.Pc—in Planetology of solid surface planets; 96.15.Nd—in Planetology of fluid planets)
        - Stresses in tectonophysics
      - **Mechanics and dynamics of deformation concern the gravity-driven forces, motions and fluid effects that make crustal rock deform and fail.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from theory to faulting, kinematics and fluids._
        - Mechanics, theory and modeling
        - Dynamics and mechanics of faulting (see also 91.32.Jk Friction of fault zones, rheology of)
        - Kinematics of crustal and mantle deformation
        - Role of fluids
      - **Observation tools and remote-sensing methods are used to measure and image geological structures without any fundamental-constant scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally as instrument platforms followed by remote sensing._
        - Submergence instruments, ROV, AUV, Submersibles, and ocean observatories
        - Remote sensing in structural geology
    - **Claims about Earth's interior describe its deformation, layered structure, composition and heat flow, with scale set by material properties rather than by the fundamental constants.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from rheology and the mantle to interior structure and interior state._
      - **Rheology and interior properties describe how Earth materials deform and flow and what the interior is like physically.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general rheology through specific layers and faults to overall interior properties._
        - Rheology of the Earth
        - Crust and lithosphere
        - Friction of fault zones
        - Earth's interior structure and properties
      - **Mantle and General aspects form one kind of claim, a geophysical claim whose scale is set by the governing constants c, ħ, G and N rather than by any one of them alone.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Mantle
        - General aspects
      - **Interior-structure topics describe how the Earth's layers are built and imaged, from the crust and upper mantle down to the deep interior.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from models of the whole interior to crust, deep layers and tomographic imaging._
        - Models of interior structure
        - Structure of the crust and upper mantle
        - Structure of the Earth's interior below the upper mantle
        - Tomography of the Earth's interior (see also 91.30.Jk Tomography in seismology)
      - **Interior-state topics address what the Earth's interior is made of and how heat flows through it.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from composition and state to heat flow._
        - Composition and state of the Earth's interior (see also 91.67.gb—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Heat flow; geothermy (see also 91.50.Ln Heat flow (benthic)—in Marine geology and geophysics; see also 88.10.-g Geothermal energy in renewable energy resources and applications)
- **Earth science studies the solid earth, oceans, atmosphere and geophysics as bodies shaped by gravity.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From solid earth outward to the atmosphere._
  - **Solid-earth topics are kinds of study of rock bodies and materials, from crustal structure and material properties to petrology and geochemistry.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from crustal structure to material properties to petrology to geochemistry._
    - **Structural and emplacement topics describe how rock bodies deform, rise into place and are organized within the crust.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from deformation features to emplacement settings._
      - **Deformation structures are the folds, faults, shear zones, melanges and fabrics that record rock deformation at different scales.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from large folds and faults to small-scale fabrics._
        - Folds and folding
        - Fractures and faults (see also 91.50.Uv Oceanic plateaus and fracture zone processes)
        - High strain deformation zones
        - Melanges
        - Mesoscopic fabrics
      - **Emplacement processes and regional settings describe how rock bodies rise into place and how the crust is organized around them.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the general field and regional setting to rising bodies._
        - Structural geology
        - Continental neotectonics
        - Diapir and diapirism
        - Local crustal structure; regional crustal structure
        - Pluton emplacement
    - **Rock and Earth-material property topics describe how materials respond mechanically, thermally, to waves and to transport of fluids and charge.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from mechanical behavior to thermodynamic state to waves to transport._
      - **Rock physical properties describe the elastic, plastic and microstructural behavior of rock materials, set by atomic-scale and material constants.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the general field to bulk behavior and then crystal-scale microstructure._
        - Physical properties of rocks and minerals (for rheological properties of geological materials, see 83.80.Nb)
        - Elasticity, fracture, and flow
        - Plasticity, diffusion, and creep
        - Crystal structure and defects, microstructure
      - **Thermodynamic and phase-state properties describe how Earth materials respond to pressure and temperature and change state.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure following the PACS listing from equation of state to thermal behavior._
        - Equations of state
        - High-pressure behavior
        - Phase changes
        - Thermal properties
      - **Wave-related properties describe how acoustic and light waves propagate through and are weakened by Earth materials.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure following the PACS listing._
        - Acoustic properties
        - Optical properties
        - Wave attenuation
      - **Transport-related properties describe how fluids, charge and other quantities move through porous Earth materials.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure following the PACS listing._
        - Permeability and porosity
        - Magnetic and electrical properties (see also 91.25.F- Rock and mineral magnetism)
        - Transport properties
        - Fluid flow
    - **Petrology and mineralogy subjects are kinds of rock and mineral study organized by composition, formation setting and extreme formation conditions.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from composition to rock type to extreme conditions._
      - **Rock-forming subjects treat the composition, chemistry and isotopic makeup of minerals and magmatic rocks.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure following the PACS listing._
        - Mineralogy and petrology
        - Mineral and crystal chemistry
        - Isotopic composition (see also 91.67.Qr Radiogenic isotope geochemistry; 91.67.Rx Stable isotope geochemistry)
        - Layered magma chambers
      - **Rock-type subjects cover igneous and metamorphic rocks by where they form and the pressure-temperature histories they record.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure following the PACS listing._
        - Igneous petrology
        - Extrusive structures and rocks
        - Intrusive structures and rocks
        - Metamorphic petrology
        - Pressure-temperature-time paths
      - **Petrology and mineralogy topics are kinds of rock and mineral study defined by the extreme conditions, deposits or parent bodies in which the rocks formed.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from metamorphic conditions to deposits, meteorites and sediments._
        - Ultra-high pressure metamorphism
        - Ultra-high temperature metamorphism
        - Mineral occurrences and deposits
        - Meteorite mineralogy and petrology
        - Sedimentary petrology (see also 91.50.Jc—in marine geology; 91.67.Ty—in Geochemistry; 92.10.Wa and 92.20.Vn—in oceanography; 92.40.Gc—in hydrology; 91.80.Wx—in Geophysics Appendix)
    - **Geochemistry topics are kinds of study of earth chemistry, from general modeling to elemental cycles, isotopes and low-temperature surface chemistry.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from general approaches to specialized subfields._
      - **General geochemistry topics are kinds of foundational approach for describing earth chemistry, covering modeling, reactions, processes and composition.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the general field to its methods and descriptive aspects._
        - Geochemistry (see also 92.20.Cm Chemistry of the ocean; 92.40.Bc Chemistry of fresh water; 92.60.Ls Ion chemistry of the atmosphere; 91.62.Kt, 91.80.Kc, and 92.20.C- in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Geochemical modeling
        - Reactions and phase equilibria (see also 91.40.Qa—in Volcanology)
        - Geochemical processes
        - Chemical composition
      - **Environmental and elemental geochemistry topics are kinds of study that follow elements and fluids through hydrothermal systems, global cycles and inclusions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from fluid systems to cycles, elements and inclusions._
        - Geochemistry of hydrothermal systems (see also 91.40.Ge—in Volcanology; 92.05.Lf—in oceanography)
        - Geochemical cycles (see also 92.20.Sg Biogeochemical cycles—in oceanography; 92.60.hn—in meteorology; 92.30.Gh—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Major and trace element geochemistry (see also 92.20.Wx Trace elements—in chemical and biological oceanography)
        - Fluid and melt inclusion geochemistry
      - **Isotope and geologic time topics are kinds of study that use isotopic signatures and age information to trace earth history.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from radiogenic isotopes to stable isotopes to geologic time._
        - Radiogenic isotope geochemistry (see also 91.65.Dt Isotopic composition—in Mineralogy and petrology; 92.20.Td Radioactivity and radioisotopes—in oceanography)
        - Stable isotope geochemistry (see also 91.65.Dt Isotopic composition—in Mineralogy and petrology)
        - Information related to geologic time
      - **Sedimentary, biogenic and low-temperature geochemistry topics are kinds of study of chemistry in surface and near-surface earth environments.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from sediments to organic matter to low-temperature settings._
        - Sedimentary geochemistry (see also 91.50.Jc—in marine geology; 91.65.Ti—in Mineralogy and petrology; 92.10.Wa and 92.20.Vn—in oceanography; 92.40.Gc—in hydrology; 91.80.Wx—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Organic and biogenic geochemistry
        - Low-temperature geochemistry
  - **Oceanography topics are the parts of the field, from framing background through physical motion and chemistry to boundaries and sediments.**
    _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from framing background to motion to contents to boundaries._
    - **Framing and background topics set the context of oceanography through its methods, its history, its temporal variation and the properties of seawater.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the broadest framing to the more specific background topics._
      - **Geologic eras are the major spans of Earth history, divided by the age of the rock record.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the most recent era (Cenozoic) back to the oldest (Precambrian)._
        - Cenozoic
        - Mesozoic
        - Paleozoic
        - Precambrian
      - **The general framing of oceanography covers the field as a whole and the modeling and laboratory methods used to study it.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the broad field heading and its aspects to the specific modeling methods._
        - Hydrospheric and atmospheric geophysics
        - General aspects of oceanography
        - Analytical modeling and laboratory experiments
        - Physical oceanography
      - **Ocean climate and cycles describe how the ocean varies over time, from seasonal rhythms to long-term shifts.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from inter-annual climate variability to long-term variability and short-period cycles._
        - Climate and inter-annual variability (see also 92.60.Ry Climatology, climate change and variability—in meteorology; 92.70.Gt Climate dynamics—in Global change)
        - Long term variability; Heinrich events
        - Diurnal, seasonal and annual cycles
      - **Seawater and its resources concern the ocean's physical and chemical properties and the energy and hydrothermal systems drawn from or found in it.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from seawater properties to energy extraction and hydrothermal systems._
        - Physical and chemical properties of seawater (salinity, density, temperature)
        - Ocean energy extraction
        - Hydrothermal systems (see also 91.40.Ge—in Volcanology; 91.67.Jk—in Geochemistry)
        - Coriolis effects
        - Circulation and currents
    - **Physical motion and interface topics cover how water moves and mixes at scales from basin-wide currents to small-scale turbulence and the air-sea surface.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from surface interface processes through dynamics to large-scale circulation and small-scale mixing._
      - **Ocean dynamics are the motions of the sea, including circulation, waves, and the effects of Earth's rotation.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from large-scale circulation through depth zones to waves and rotation effects._
        - Deep ocean processes
        - Upper ocean and mixed layer processes
        - Ocean waves and oscillations
        - Ocean influence of Earth's rotation
      - **Air-sea and surface interface processes cover the exchange of energy and the surface phenomena that arise where the ocean meets the atmosphere and ice.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the sea surface exchange outward to ice and fog at the interface._
        - Sea-air energy exchange processes (see also 92.60.Cc—in meteorology)
        - Sea ice (mechanics and air/sea/ice exchange processes)
        - Ocean fog
        - Ocean currents; Eastern boundary currents, Western boundary currents
        - Thermohaline convection
      - **Small-scale mixing and measurement topics concern turbulence, fine structure, and the sound and hydrographic properties that characterise the water column.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from turbulent mixing to fine structure and then to the acoustic and hydrographic ways of observing the water._
        - Turbulence, diffusion, and mixing processes in oceanography
        - Fine structure and microstructure in oceanography
        - Underwater sound (see also 43.30.+m in acoustics; 43.30.-k in Acoustics Appendix)
        - Hydrography (for ocean parameter estimation by acoustical methods, see 43.30.Pc—in Acoustics Appendix)
      - **Large-scale circulation and its features cover the global currents, deep water formation, and vertical and frontal motions that move water across ocean basins.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the general circulation to its deep, convective, and current components and then to fronts and vertical motions._
        - General circulation
        - Deep water formation and circulation
        - Fronts and jets
        - Upwelling and convergences (see also 92.30.Vn—in Geophysics Appendix)
    - **Chemical and biological topics cover what the sea contains, including its dissolved substances, its mesoscale variability, and its living systems and oxygen conditions.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general chemical-biological coverage to composition and then to life and oxygen._
      - **Mesoscale variability and chemical-biological oceanography cover eddies, climate-scale oscillations, and the chemistry and life of the sea.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from mesoscale eddies to the ocean-atmosphere oscillation of El Nino and then to chemical and biological oceanography._
        - Eddies and mesoscale processes
        - El Nino Southern Oscillation (see also 92.30.La—in Paleoceanography)
        - Chemical and biological oceanography
      - **Marine chemical composition topics cover the dissolved substances, gases, trace elements, isotopes and airborne particles that make up ocean chemistry.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from general ocean chemistry to its specific constituents._
        - Chemistry of the ocean
        - Gases in chemical oceanography (see also 91.50.Hc Gas and hydrate systems—in marine geology)
        - Trace elements (see also 91.67.Pq Major and trace element geochemistry)
        - Radioactivity and radioisotopes (see also 91.65.Dt Isotopic composition—in Mineralogy and petrology; 91.67.Qr Radiogenic isotope geochemistry)
        - Aerosols (see also 92.60.Mt—in meteorology; 91.67.gp and 92.30.Ef—in Geophysics Appendix)
      - **Marine life and oxygen-condition topics cover the biological activity, element cycles and oxygen-limited habitats of the ocean.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from oxygen-limited habitats to biology and element cycles._
        - Anoxic environments (see also 91.62.+g Biogeosciences; 91.62.De—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Hypoxic environment (see also 91.62.De—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Biology of the ocean (see also 91.62.+g Biogeosciences; 92.40.vu Cryobiology—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Biogeochemical cycles (see also 91.67.Nc—in Geochemistry; 92.60.hn—in meteorology; 92.30.Gh—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Carbon cycling (see also 91.62.La—in Geophysics Appendix)
    - **Boundary and sediment topics cover the interaction of the ocean with the sea floor, coasts and the particulate material deposited there.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from boundary processes to sediment deposition and transport._
      - **Boundary and bottom region processes describe the ocean's interaction with the sea floor, coasts, and sediments, including dense flows that spill over topography.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the sea floor itself to coastal margins, sediment movement, and overflows._
        - Benthic boundary layers, ocean bottom processes (see also 91.50.Ey Sea floor, morphology, geology, and geophysics—in marine geology)
        - Coastal, estuarine, and near shore processes (see also 91.50.Cw Beach and coastal processes—in marine geology)
        - Sediment transport (see also 91.50.Jc—in marine geology; 91.65.Ti—in Mineralogy and petrology; 91.67.Ty—in Geochemistry; 92.20.Vn—in chemical oceanography; 92.40.Gc—in Hydrology; 91.80.Wx—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Overflows
      - **Sediment and seafloor topics cover the deposition, transport and modeling of particulate material at the bottom of oceans and in water bodies.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the seafloor to sedimentation and its transport and modeling._
        - Benthic processes, sea-bottom processes (see also 91.50.Ey—in marine geology; 92.10.Oc—in oceanography; 92.40.Gc—in hydrology)
        - Sedimentation (see also 91.50.Jc—in marine geology; 91.65.Ti—in petrology; 91.67.Ty—in Geochemistry; 92.10.Wa—in oceanography; 92.40.Gc—in hydrology; 91.80.Wx—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Erosion and sedimentation; sediment transport (see also 91.50.Jc—in marine geology; 91.65.Ti—in Mineralogy and petrology; 91.67.Ty—in Geochemistry; 92.10.Wa and 92.20.Vn—in oceanography; 91.80.Wx—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Modeling; general theory
  - **Atmosphere and environment topics are kinds of study of air, climate, land and water, whose scales are set by particle number or by classical constants.**
    _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from topics tied to no fundamental constant, to particle-number-scaled, to gravity-scaled, to Earth-system scale._
    - **Land, water and human-impact topic areas describe terrestrial and aquatic systems and are not tied to one fundamental constant.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from land surface to water storage to water management and human impact._
      - **Freshwater and cryosphere topics cover the chemistry, supply, deficit and frozen storage of water on land.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the general field to water chemistry, precipitation and drought._
        - Hydrology and glaciology; cryosphere (see also 92.70.Ha—in Global change)
        - Chemistry of fresh water
        - Precipitation (see also 92.60.jf—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Drought
      - **Subsurface and soil water topics describe how water is stored in, and exchanged with, the ground and living plants.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from soil and groundwater to plant-water exchange._
        - Groundwater
        - Soil moisture and temperature
        - Evapotranspiration (see also 92.60.jc Evaporation—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Eco-hydrology; plant ecology
      - **Surface water and water management topics cover water bodies and human control of them, together with the budgets that account for water.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from natural water bodies to engineered works and overall budgets._
        - Surface water, water resources
        - Wetlands
        - Irrigation; dams
        - Hydrologic cycles and budgets
    - **Land, water and human-impact topic areas describe terrestrial and aquatic systems and are not tied to one fundamental constant.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **Land-surface processes and hazards reshape the ground through the movement of earth, ice and landforms.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from mass movements to landform and ice processes._
        - Debris flow and landslides
        - Geomorphology
        - Glaciology (see also 92.30.Mc—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Desertification
      - **Human-impact topics cover the pollution and other anthropogenic changes that people cause in marine and freshwater systems.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from marine pollution to wider anthropogenic effects._
        - Marine pollution
        - Anthropogenic effects (see also 92.30.De—in Geophysics Appendix)
    - **Topics whose scale is set mainly by the number of molecules or particles, N, covering water, clouds, surface exchange, pollution and composition.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by how directly molecular number alone sets the scale, with water and cloud processes first, then transport and reaction topics, then composition._
      - **Water and cloud processes are scaled by molecular number and phase-change thermodynamics, so N sets their scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Set by N with little role for ħ or c._
        - Water in the atmosphere
        - Cloud physics and chemistry
        - Ion chemistry of the atmosphere
        - Atmospheric electricity, lightning
        - Electromagnetic wave propagation
      - **Surface exchange, particulate, pollution and volcanic topics are scaled by the number of particles and molecules transported and reacting, so N sets their scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Set by N and gravity-driven transport, slightly more chemical than the cloud group._
        - Land/atmosphere interactions
        - Particles and aerosols (see also 92.20.Bk—in oceanography; 91.67.gp and 92.30.Ef—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Air quality and air pollution (see also 07.88.+y Instruments for environmental pollution measurements)
        - Volcanic effects
      - **Atmospheric composition topics concern the chemical makeup of the air and the sources, sinks and transport of its constituents.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Arranged by structure, from layer-specific chemistry to the sources and sinks that feed it._
        - Stratospheric composition and chemistry
        - Tropospheric composition and chemistry, constituent transport and chemistry
        - Constituent sources and sinks
    - **Atmospheric topics concerned with global-scale circulation, modeling and storms, whose scale is set by gravity and bulk air together with classical fluid constants.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the largest global scale, set by G and N, down to local structure and classical fluid dynamics._
      - **Large-scale weather and climate phenomena are scaled mainly by gravity and the huge number of molecules in bulk air, so G and N set their scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Most dominated by G and N, with no quantum or relativistic role._
        - Paleoclimatology (see also 92.70.Gt Climate dynamics—in Global change)
        - Tropical meteorology
        - Climatology, climate change and variability (see also 92.70.Gt and 92.70.Kb—in Global change; 92.30.Bc—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Polar meteorology
        - Weather analysis and prediction
      - **Atmosphere-scale and coupling topics treat the global air system and its links to water and the ocean.**
