        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from hydroclimate and general atmosphere to circulation and ocean coupling._
        - Hydrometeorology, hydroclimatology
        - Properties and dynamics of the atmosphere; meteorology (see also 92.40.Zg Hydrometeorology, hydroclimatology)
        - General circulation
        - Ocean/atmosphere interactions, air/sea constituent fluxes (see also 92.10.Kp—in oceanography)
      - **Atmospheric modeling and local structure topics deal with how the air is represented, layered, moved and composed.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from modeling to boundary layer, winds and composition._
        - Modeling and model calibration (see also 92.70.Np Global climate modeling)
        - Boundary layer structure and processes
        - Winds and their effects
        - Atmospheric composition, structure, and properties
      - **Stratosphere-troposphere interactions and storms are atmospheric phenomena whose scale is set by classical fluid and thermodynamic constants rather than by c, ħ, or G.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Stratosphere/troposphere interactions
        - Storms
      - **Radiative and upper-atmosphere composition topics are scaled by photon energy and quantum transitions, so ħ and c set their scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Most dependent on ħ and c through photons and atomic or molecular quantum processes._
        - Radiative processes, solar radiation
        - Exospheric composition and chemistry
        - Thermospheric composition and chemistry, energy deposition
        - Mesospheric composition, energy deposition, constituent transport and chemistry
    - **Earth-system and climate topics describe the air, the climate, the cycling of matter and the global change of the Earth system, all at classical environmental scales.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from atmosphere and climate to cycling and global change._
      - **Atmospheric dynamics topics concern the physical motion and state of the air, including waves, turbulent mixing and its pressure, density and temperature.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Arranged by structure, from wave motion to mixing to bulk thermodynamic state._
        - Acoustic gravity waves, tides, and compressional waves
        - Convection, turbulence, and diffusion (see also 92.30.Ef—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Pressure, density, and temperature
        - Airglow and aurorae (see also 94.20.Ac Auroral ionosphere; 94.30.Aa Auroral phenomena in magnetosphere)
        - Other upper atmospheric phenomena: red sprites; blue jets; atmospheric gamma ray and intense VHF emissions
      - **Climate change topics concern long-term or abrupt alterations of the climate system and its atmospheric, cryospheric, oceanic and regional components.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Arranged by structure, from global change to its abrupt, regional, atmospheric, cryospheric and oceanic aspects._
        - Global change
        - Abrupt/rapid climate change
        - Atmosphere
        - Climate dynamics (see also 92.60.Ry—in meteorology; 92.30.Bc—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Cryospheric change
      - **Earth-surface and cycling topics concern exchanges between land, life, ocean and air, including biogeochemical and water cycles and weathering.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Arranged by structure, from land-atmosphere exchange to biogeochemical cycles, weathering, sea level and the water cycle._
        - Geochemical cycles (see also 91.67.Nc—in Geochemistry; 92.20.Sg—in oceanography; 92.30.Gh—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Geomorphology and weathering (see also 92.40.Gc Erosion and sedimentation; sediment transport; 92.40.Pb—in hydrology; 92.40.P- in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Oceans, sea level change (see also 92.10.hp—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Regional climate change (see also 92.60.Ry—in meteorology; 92.30.Bc—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Water cycles
      - **Biogeochemical processes and land/atmosphere interactions are Earth-system topics whose claims are governed by classical environmental scales rather than by the fundamental constants c, ħ, G or N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Biogeochemical processes
        - Land/atmosphere interactions
      - **Global change and climate topics are the impacts, models and drivers that describe how the Earth system and its climate change.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from impacts, through models, to specific drivers of change._
        - Impacts of global change; global warming (see also 92.30.Np—in Geophysics Appendix)
        - Global climate modeling
        - Earth system modeling
        - Solar variability impact
        - Land cover change
  - **Geophysics topics are kinds of subject headings covering geographic organization, exploration methods and applied or space geophysics.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from regional headings to methods to applications._
    - **Geographic regions and observation headings are the landmasses, oceans and zones, and the categories, under which geophysical information is organized.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from headings to continents, islands, oceans and zones._
      - **Observation headings are the general category and the regional information subcategory under which geophysical observations are organized.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Parent heading first, then its regional information subheading._
        - Geophysical observations, instrumentation, and techniques
        - Information related to geographical regions
      - **Continental regions are the large landmasses of the Earth, each treated as a geographic region for geophysical information.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Listed in the source order from Africa through South America._
        - Africa
        - Antarctica
        - Asia
        - Australia
        - Europe
      - **Americas and large islands are the western landmasses, including North America, South America and large islands such as Greenland.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Listed in the source order from North America to large islands._
        - North America
        - South America
        - Large islands (e.g., Greenland)
      - **Ocean regions are the major bodies of salt water that cover most of the Earth's surface.**
        _basis: parts, order: structure. Listed in the source order from the Arctic Ocean to the Southern Ocean._
        - Arctic Ocean
        - Atlantic Ocean
        - Indian Ocean
        - Pacific Ocean
        - Southern Ocean
      - **Regional seas and the polar, temperate and tropical zones are kinds of geographic region of the Earth.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: regional seas first, then climate zones from poles to tropics._
        - Regional seas
        - Polar regions
        - Temperate regions
        - Tropical regions
    - **Exploration geophysics methods and settings are the physical-property measurements and the places where the subsurface is probed.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from measurement methods to survey settings._
      - **Physical measurement methods in exploration geophysics each probe the subsurface through a different physical property such as gravity, magnetism, radioactivity or seismic waves.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the PACS listing of methods._
        - Gravity methods
        - Magnetic and electrical methods
        - Radioactivity methods
        - Seismic methods
      - **Exploration geophysics is carried out in distinct settings, namely continental structures, oceanic structures and boreholes.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: land, ocean, then downhole._
        - Exploration of continental structures
        - Exploration of oceanic structures
        - Downhole methods
    - **Applied and space geophysics covers supporting techniques and applications of exploration geophysics together with plasma physics of the near-Earth space environment.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from applied techniques to space plasma._
      - **General exploration geophysics and its supporting techniques cover computation, remote sensing and oil prospecting applications.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: the general heading first, then supporting techniques and applications._
        - Instruments and techniques for geophysical research: Exploration geophysics (see also 91.50.Ga Bathymetry, seafloor topology; 91.50.Yf Submergence instruments, ROV, AUV, submersibles, and ocean observatories—in marine geology; 92.10.Yb Hydrography—in oceanography)
        - Computational methods and data processing, data acquisition and storage
        - Remote sensing in exploration geophysics (see also 91.40.Yt—in Volcanology; 91.55.Uv—in Structural geology)
        - Oil prospecting, pipelines, and conduits (see also 91.50.Sn Ocean drilling)
      - **Space plasma physics of the ionosphere and magnetosphere covers plasma phenomena such as dust interactions, radiation processes and solitary waves.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: the field heading first, then its topics._
        - Physics of the ionosphere and magnetosphere
        - Space plasma physics (see also 96.50.-e Interplanetary physics)
        - Plasma interactions with dust and aerosols
        - Radiation processes
        - Solitons and solitary waves
- **Near-Earth and planetary space covers charged-particle regions, space phenomena, observation and planets.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From near-Earth regions outward to planets._
  - **The ionosphere and magnetosphere are charged-particle regions of near-Earth space, and the planetary outer environment extends the same field-and-particle subjects to other planets.**
    _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from the ionospheric topic areas to their subdivisions, then the magnetospheric areas and the planetary outer environment._
    - **Ionospheric topic areas are the structural, electrodynamic, spacecraft-interaction and observational subjects that together make up the study of the ionosphere.**
      _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from the ionosphere's structure through its electrodynamics and interactions to the methods used to observe it._
      - **Ionospheric structure topics describe the altitude-defined layers and the composition, temperature and density of the ionospheric plasma.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from broad structure to specific layers, with scales set largely by plasma density and temperature._
        - Physics of the ionosphere (for ionospheres of the planets, see 96.12.ji and 96.15.hk; for radiowave propagation, see 41.20.Jb—in electromagnetism)
        - Ionospheric structure, composition
        - Plasma temperature and density
        - D region
        - E region
      - **Ionospheric electrodynamics topics cover the electric fields, currents, particle precipitation and disturbances that drive and perturb the ionosphere.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from steady current systems to transient disturbances._
        - Auroral ionosphere (see also 92.60.hw Airglow and aurorae—in meteorology; 94.30.Aa Auroral phenomena in magnetosphere)
        - Particle precipitation (see also 94.30.Ny—in Physics of the magnetosphere)
        - Electric fields; current system
        - Ionospheric disturbances, irregularities, and storms
        - Ionospheric dynamics and interactions
      - **Spacecraft interaction topics address how spacecraft and meteors exchange charge, energy and matter with the surrounding atmosphere and plasma.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from charging and wakes to broader atmospheric interaction._
        - Spacecraft/atmosphere interactions
        - Spacecraft sheaths, wakes, and charging
        - Meteor-trail physics
      - **Observation and forecasting topics cover the experimental, sounding, modeling and prediction methods used to study and anticipate ionospheric and space conditions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from laboratory and active techniques to applied forecasting, which are the least tied to fundamental constants._
        - Experimental techniques and laboratory studies (see also 52.72.+v—in physics of plasmas)
        - Space weather
        - Ionospheric modeling and forecasting
        - Ionospheric soundings; active experiments
    - **Ionospheric subdivisions are kinds of ionospheric topic distinguished by altitude region, internal process, or external coupling.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from regions to internal processes to external interactions._
      - **Ionospheric regions are kinds of ionosphere distinguished by altitude layer, from the F region up to the topside.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by altitude layer from the F region to the topside._
        - F region
        - Topside region
        - Polar cap ionosphere
        - Mid-latitude ionosphere
        - Equatorial ionosphere
      - **Ionospheric plasma processes are kinds of internal dynamics and chemistry that govern how ionospheric plasma is produced, moves and radiates waves.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from ionization chemistry to motion, waves and wave-particle effects._
        - Ion chemistry and composition; ionization mechanisms
        - Plasma motion; plasma convection; particle acceleration
        - Plasma waves and instabilities
        - Wave/particle interactions
      - **Ionospheric interactions are kinds of coupling between the ionosphere and the atmosphere, the magnetosphere, dust, solar radiation and radio waves.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from coupling with neighbouring regions to external forcing and wave propagation._
        - Ionosphere/atmospheric interactions
        - Ionosphere/magnetosphere interactions
        - Plasma interactions with dust and aerosols
        - Solar radiation and cosmic ray effects
        - Electromagnetic wave propagation
    - **Magnetospheric subject areas are kinds of magnetospheric topic covering its general subjects, large-scale disturbances and near-Earth trapped-particle zones.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general subjects to global disturbances to inner regions._
      - **Magnetospheric topics are kinds of subject within magnetospheric physics, covering its overall physics, aurora, modeling, configuration, trapped particles and current systems.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general physics and phenomena to configuration, particles and currents._
        - Physics of the magnetosphere
        - Magnetospheric modeling and forecasting
        - Magnetospheric configuration and dynamics
        - Electric fields, field-aligned currents and current systems, and ring currents
      - **Global disturbances are large-scale time-varying magnetospheric events driven by solar wind energy input and field reconfiguration.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from global storms to pulsations and reconnection._
        - Magnetic storms, substorms
        - Magnetic pulsations
        - Magnetic reconnection
      - **Inner and trapped-particle regions are the magnetospheric zones close to Earth where particles are confined by the geomagnetic field.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the radiation belts outward to the plasmasphere._
        - Radiation belts
        - Inner magnetosphere
        - Plasmasphere
    - **The outer environment of a planet consists of its gaseous atmosphere and the magnetic fields, rings, dust and charged particles around it.**
      _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered outward from the atmosphere to the magnetic and particle environment beyond it._
      - **Atmosphere topics concern the gaseous envelopes surrounding planets.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the solid-planet atmosphere to the fluid-planet atmosphere._
        - Atmospheres
        - Atmospheres
        - Polar regions
      - **Magnetic and particle-environment topics concern the fields, rings, dust and charged-particle interactions around a planet.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the planet's magnetic field to the dust and particles it interacts with._
        - Magnetic field and magnetism
        - Magnetic field and magnetism
        - Interactions with particles and fields
        - Rings and dust
        - Planetology of fluid planets (see also 96.12.-a Planetology of solid surface planets; 96.30.Bc Comparative planetology)
  - **Plasma, field and orbital phenomena of space are kinds of topics whose scale is set by c, by G, or by no constant at all.**
    _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from phenomena scaled by c or no constant, to those scaled by gravity G, to interplanetary plasma structures._
    - **Plasma wave and particle phenomena are kinds of classical electromagnetic processes in space plasmas whose scale is set by c or by none of the constants c, ħ, G and N.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from wave processes to particle phenomena to general wave and particle exchange._
      - **Wave and plasma process topics concern how waves propagate, interact with particles and become turbulent in space plasmas, with c setting the electromagnetic scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from most directly c-dependent wave propagation to turbulent interactions._
        - Wave propagation (see also 94.30.Tz—in Physics of the magnetosphere)
        - Wave/wave, wave/particle interactions
        - Turbulence
      - **Magnetospheric particle phenomena, energetic trapped particles and auroral activity, are classical plasma and electromagnetic processes whose scale is set by none of the quantum, gravitational or particle-number constants c, ħ, G and N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Energetic trapped particles
        - Auroral phenomena in magnetosphere (see also 94.20.Ac Auroral ionosphere)
      - **Wave and particle processes are the ways electromagnetic waves, instabilities, and energetic or convecting plasma move and exchange energy in the magnetosphere.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from precipitation and waves to plasma motion._
        - Energetic particle precipitation (see also 94.20.Qq—in Physics of the ionosphere)
        - Electromagnetic wave propagation (see also 94.20.Bb—in Physics of the ionosphere)
        - MHD waves, plasma waves, and instabilities
        - Plasma motion; plasma convection
    - **Spatial and gravitational topics are scaled by Newtonian or relativistic gravity through G, alone or together with c, and cover orbits, regions of space and their boundaries.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from regional headings to gravity-scaled topics._
      - **Outer and boundary regions are the distant magnetospheric domains and the interfaces where the magnetosphere meets the solar wind.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from outer regions to boundary layers, tail and polar cap._
        - Magnetosphere interactions
        - Outer magnetosphere
        - Magnetospheric cusp
        - Magnetopause
        - Magnetosheath
      - **Tail and polar structures plus general astronomy cover the nightside plasma sheet, the polar cap, and the foundational astronomy headings.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the magnetotail to polar regions and astronomy._
        - Magnetotail
        - Plasma sheet
        - Polar cap phenomena
        - Fundamental astronomy and astrophysics; instrumentation, techniques, and astronomical observations
        - Fundamental astronomy
      - **Orbital and positional topics are scaled by Newtonian gravity through G, with no role for c or ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: dynamics first, then measurement and prediction of positions._
        - Orbit determination and improvement
        - Eclipses, transits, and occultations
        - Astrometry and reference systems
        - Ephemerides, almanacs, and calendars
      - **Relativity and gravitation is the topic whose scale is set jointly by G and the speed of light c.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: a single topic._
        - Relativity and gravitation (see also section 04 General relativity and gravitation; 98.80.Jk Mathematical and relativistic aspects of cosmology)
        - Fundamental aspects of astrophysics (see also section 26 Nuclear astrophysics)
        - Celestial mechanics (including n -body problems) (see also 45.50.Pk—in Classical mechanics of discrete systems)
        - Chaotic dynamics (see also 05.45.-a Nonlinear dynamics and chaos)
    - **Interplanetary plasma topics are the fields, plasma, dust and gas between planets together with the shocks, streams, waves and ejecta that structure them.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **Interplanetary physics covers the fields, plasma, dust and gas that fill the space between planets out to the heliopause.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the general topic to specific components._
        - Interplanetary physics (see also 94.05.-a Space plasma physics)
        - Interplanetary magnetic fields
        - Solar wind plasma; sources of solar wind
        - Interplanetary dust and gas
        - Heliopause and solar wind termination
      - **Shocks, discontinuities and streams are sharp or recurring plasma structures that form in the solar wind and around planets.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the sharpest boundary (shock) to the broadest recurring flow structure (stream)._
        - Planetary bow shocks; interplanetary shocks
        - Discontinuities
        - Corotating streams
      - **Waves, ejecta and variations are time-dependent disturbances that propagate through or modulate the interplanetary plasma.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by timescale of variation, from fast waves to the slow solar cycle._
        - MHD waves; plasma waves, turbulence
        - Ejecta, driver gases, and magnetic clouds
        - Interplanetary propagation and effects
        - Solar cycle variations
  - **Observational methods and bands are kinds of measurement whose scale is fixed by ħ with c, by c alone, or by the number N of particles or samples.**
    _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from quantum-scaled (ħ and c), through wave-optical (c), to number-scaled (N) topics and the observing bands._
    - **Quantum-scaled topics and measurements are set by Planck's constant ħ together with c, because photon and energy quanta fix what is observed.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from atomic topics to measurements to extreme-regime instruments._
      - **Particle, atomic, molecular, and spectral topics are scaled by Planck's constant ħ together with c, which fix quantum energy levels and transition rates.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: from particles to atoms to molecules to spectral data._
        - Elementary particle processes
        - Atomic processes and interactions
        - Molecular and chemical processes and interactions
        - Atomic and molecular data, spectra, and spectral parameters (opacities, rotation constants, line identification, oscillator strengths, gf values, transition probabilities, etc.)
      - **Spectral, photometric and polarimetric measurements are set by ħ and c, since photon energy quanta determine what is detected.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from broadband photometry to finer spectroscopic and polarization resolution of photon properties._
        - Photoconductors and bolometers
        - Photography and photometry (including microlensing techniques)
        - Spectroscopy and spectrophotometry
        - Polarimetry
        - Photometric, polarimetric, and spectroscopic instrumentation
      - **High-energy and particle detectors, gravitational-wave detectors and timekeeping instruments are set by ħ, c and G in extreme quantum or gravitational regimes.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from photon-counting high-energy detection to particle detection and then gravitational and precision-timing regimes._
        - X- and γ-ray telescopes and instrumentation
        - Neutrino, muon, pion, and other elementary particle detectors; cosmic ray detectors (see also 29.40.-n Radiation detectors—in Nuclear physics)
        - Gravitational radiation detectors; mass spectrometers; and other instrumentation and techniques (see also 04.80.Nn Gravitational wave detectors and experiments in—General relativity and gravitation)
        - Auxiliary and recording instruments; clocks and frequency standards
    - **Wave-optical imaging and interferometric methods have scales fixed by the speed of light c through wavelength, diffraction, phase and baseline.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from imaging to interferometry._
      - **Imaging and wavefront-based telescopes and techniques have resolution scales set by the speed of light and wavelength, with diffraction as the limit.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from single-aperture telescopes to interferometric and wavefront-corrected systems approaching the diffraction limit._
        - Ground-based ultraviolet, optical and infrared telescopes
        - Space-based ultraviolet, optical, and infrared telescopes
        - Solar instruments
        - Radio telescopes and instrumentation; heterodyne receivers
        - Adaptive and segmented optics (see also 42.68.Wt Remote sensing; LIDAR and adaptive systems—in atmospheric optics)
      - **Interferometric and astrometric methods have scales fixed by c through phase and baseline relationships between wavefronts.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from general astrometric instruments to pure interferometry in increasing reliance on phase coherence._
        - Astrometric and interferometric instruments
        - Interferometry
    - **Bulk, radiation-fluid and data-handling topics are scaled by the particle or sample number N rather than by a single fundamental constant.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from radiation and bulk matter to statistics and general methods._
      - **Radiation and fluid-like topics are scaled by c and ħ in the light and by the particle number N in the bulk medium.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: radiation first, then bulk media._
        - Radiation mechanisms; polarization
        - Radiative transfer; scattering
        - Hydrodynamics
        - Magnetohydrodynamics and plasmas (see also 52.30.Cv and 52.72.+v—in physics of plasmas)
      - **Bulk-matter and detector topics are scaled by the particle number N of many-body matter, with thermal and solid-state properties.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: bulk matter first, then instruments._
        - Thermodynamic processes, conduction, convection, equations of state
        - Dust processes (condensation, evaporation, sputtering, mantle growth, etc.)
        - Astronomical and space-research instrumentation (see also 94.80.+g Instrumentation for space plasma physics, ionosphere, and magnetosphere)
        - Charge-coupled devices, image detectors, and IR detector arrays (see also 85.60.Gz Photodetectors)
      - **Probes and data-processing methods have scales governed by counting statistics and numbers of samples, N, rather than a single physical constant.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from deep-space probe platforms to data-reduction and computational methods that depend on sample number._
        - Lunar, planetary, and deep-space probes
        - Observation and data reduction techniques; computer modeling and simulation
        - Image processing (including source extraction)
        - Mathematical procedures and computer techniques
      - **Observational techniques are general methods for acquiring and processing astronomical data regardless of the spectral band used.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from acquisition to digitization to analysis._
        - Remote observing techniques
        - Digitization techniques
        - Time series analysis, time variability
    - **Low-frequency, mid-range and high-energy observing bands each detect radiation in a different range of photon energy.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by increasing photon energy: low-frequency, then mid-range, then high-energy and non-electromagnetic._
      - **Low-frequency observing bands detect long-wavelength radiation, from radio and microwave down to the far infrared, where the photon energy ħc/λ is small.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from longest to shorter wavelength._
        - Radio, microwave (>1 mm)
        - Submillimeter (300 μm-1 mm)
        - Far infrared (10-300 μm)
      - **Mid-range observing bands cover mid-infrared through near-ultraviolet light, where photon energies are set by atomic and molecular transitions.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from longer to shorter wavelength._
        - Infrared (3-10 μm)
        - Near infrared (0.75-3 μm)
        - Visible (390-750 nm)
        - Near ultraviolet (300-390 nm)
      - **High-energy and non-electromagnetic observations detect ultraviolet and shorter-wavelength photons, particles and gravitational radiation, where ħ, c and G set the scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from highest-energy photons to other messengers._
        - Ultraviolet (10-300 nm)
        - X-ray
        - γ-ray
        - Neutrino, muon, pion, and other elementary particles; cosmic rays
        - Gravitational radiation, magnetic fields, and other observations
  - **Planetary science topics are kinds of subjects classified by how strongly gravity G sets their scale, from major planets to small bodies.**
    _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by the strength of gravitational scaling, from solid-planet scope and major planets down to small bodies and shaping processes._
    - **The scope of solar-system study is laid out as a heading together with the gravity, interior and surface topics of a solid planet.**
      _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from the overall scope heading to the planet's gravity field, then its interior, then its surface._
      - **The classification headings and the planetology topics of solid planets define the scope of astronomical observation and of solar-system study, with gravity G setting orbital and rotational scales.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the top-level headings to the specific topics._
        - Astronomical observations (additional primary heading(s) must be chosen with these entries to represent the astronomical objects and/or properties studied)
        - Solar system; planetology
        - Planetology of solid surface planets (see also 96.15.-g Planetology of fluid planets; 96.30.Bc Comparative planetology)
        - Origin and evolution
        - Orbital and rotational dynamics
      - **Gravity and orbital-dynamics topics concern the mass-driven fields and motions that set a planet's gravitational scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the planet's own field to its orbital and rotational motion._
        - Gravitational fields
        - Gravitational fields
        - Orbital and rotational dynamics
        - Origin and evolution
      - **Interior and deep-body topics concern the internal structure, thermal state and geological activity of a planet beneath its surface.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the deep interior up to the crustal activity it drives._
        - Interiors
        - Interiors
        - Heat flow
        - Tectonics, volcanism
      - **Surface and composition topics concern the material makeup and outer boundary layers of a planet.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from bulk composition to the surface and polar regions._
        - Composition
        - Composition
        - Surfaces
        - Surfaces
    - **Topics in planetary science are classified by how strongly the gravitational constant G sets their scale.**
      _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the strongest dependence on G (orbital dynamics) to the weakest (electromagnetic and particle interactions)._
      - **Gravitational and orbital-dynamics topics have their scale set almost entirely by G through the gravitational field and orbital motion of a body.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Most strongly G-dominated: scale fixed directly by G and mass._
        - Tidal forces
        - Orbital and rotational dynamics
        - Gravitational fields
        - Gravitational field, selenodesy, and magnetic fields
      - **Origin and evolution topics are scaled by G through gravitational accretion and collapse over long timescales.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Strongly G-dominated, but also depends on material and thermal processes._
        - Origin and evolution
        - Origin and evolution
        - Rings and dust
        - Tori and exospheres
      - **Impact and cratering topics are scaled by G through surface gravity together with the strength and density of the materials involved.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Moderately G-dependent: gravity competes with material strength._
        - Impact phenomena
        - Impacts, cratering
        - Features, landmarks, mineralogy, and petrology
        - Polar regions
      - **Electromagnetic and particle-interaction topics are scaled mainly by c and plasma processes, with G entering only weakly.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Weakly G-dependent: scale set by c and field physics._
        - Interactions with particles and fields
        - Ionospheres
        - Magnetic fields and magnetism
        - Moon
    - **Major planets and their principal satellite systems are bodies whose scale is set mainly by gravity G, ordered from the inner solar system outward.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **Inner planets and Mars's moons are the rocky bodies closest to the Sun together with the satellites of Mars.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the classification, by distance from the Sun._
        - Mercury
        - Venus
        - Mars
        - Martian satellites
      - **Jupiter and its satellites form the Jovian system, together with the generic study of satellites.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the classification, from the planet to its satellites._
        - Satellites
        - Jupiter
        - Jovian satellites
      - **Saturn, its satellites and Uranus are the outer giant planets and the moon system of Saturn.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the classification, by distance from the Sun._
        - Saturn
        - Saturnian satellites
        - Uranus
    - **Major planets and their principal satellite systems are bodies whose scale is set mainly by gravity G, ordered from the inner solar system outward.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **Material, atmospheric and compositional topics are scaled by N and ħ through atomic and molecular properties, with G playing the smallest role.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Least G-dominated: scale set by N and quantum constants via atomic and molecular physics._
        - Physical properties of materials
        - Atmospheres
        - Composition
        - Planetology of comets and small bodies
      - **Primary planetary bodies in the outer solar system are the distant planet Neptune and the dwarf planet Pluto.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally by primary body._
        - Neptune
        - Pluto
    - **Small bodies, outer satellite and ring systems, individual large moons and the processes that shape them are objects and phenomena studied comparatively across the solar system.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **Physical processes are the phenomena that act on and shape small bodies and planetary surfaces and environments.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the classification, from impacts to volcanism._
        - Impact phenomena
        - Interactions with solar wind plasma and fields
        - Plasma and MHD instabilities
        - Radiation and spectra
        - Volcanism
      - **Small bodies and comparative studies cover general solar system objects, comets, dwarf planets and their satellites.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the classification, from general to specific bodies._
        - Solar system objects
        - Comparative planetology (see also 96.12.-a Planetology of solid surface planets; 96.15.-g Planetology of fluid planets)
        - Comets (see also 96.25.-f Planetology of comets and small bodies)
        - Dwarf Planets
        - Dwarf planet satellites
      - **Satellite and ring systems of the outer planets are the moons and rings orbiting Uranus, Neptune and Pluto.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally by host body._
        - Uranian satellites
        - Neptunian satellites
        - Plutonian satellites
        - Planetary rings
      - **Small solid bodies and debris are objects such as dust, trans-Neptunian objects, asteroids, meteoroids and meteorites.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from dust to larger bodies._
        - Dust, extraterrestrial materials
        - Kuiper belt, trans-Neptunian objects
        - Asteroids, meteoroids
        - Meteors, meteorites and tektites (see also 91.65.Sn Meteorite mineralogy and petrology; 94.20.Xa Meteor-trail physics; 91.67.gn—in Geophysics Appendix)
      - **The large moons of Jupiter and Saturn are individual satellites studied as worlds in their own right.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally by host planet and distance._
        - Io
        - Europa
        - Ganymede
        - Callisto
        - Titan
- **Astrophysics studies the Sun, stars, stellar systems, galaxies and the Universe at increasing scales.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From the Sun to single stars to systems to the Universe._
  - **Particle, solar and radiation claims concern energetic particles, the Sun, and radiative or observational processes whose scales are set mainly by electromagnetic constants c and ħ or by gravity and particle number in the Sun.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure_
    - **Energetic particle claims concern fast charged particles and neutrals, their acceleration, spectra and interactions with matter, with scales set by electromagnetic and relativistic constants.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from particle acceleration to particle interactions with matter._
      - **Energetic particle physics covers high-energy charged particles and the processes that accelerate them and set their spectra.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the general population of cosmic rays to specific acceleration and spectral properties._
        - Cosmic rays (see also 94.20.wq Solar radiation and cosmic ray effects)
        - Energetic particles
        - Particle acceleration
        - Composition, energy spectra and interactions
      - **Particle interactions with matter include cascades in air and the pickup and neutral particles that enter from the interstellar medium.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from large cascades of many particles to individual neutral and pickup particles._
        - Extensive air showers
        - Interactions with terrestrial matter
        - Pickup ions
        - Neutral particles
    - **Solar and heliospheric claims describe the Sun's layers, coronal structures and magnetic eruptive activity, treated classically with scales set largely by gravity and particle number.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from the largest scales inward through layers, structures and activity._
      - **The Sun and the outer heliosphere set the largest scales, from solar bulk properties to the boundary with interstellar space.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the Sun itself outward to the Oort cloud at the greatest distance._
        - Solar physics
        - Diameter, rotation, and mass
        - Composition
        - Heliosphere/interstellar medium interactions
        - Oort cloud
      - **The solar layers are the successive radial regions of the Sun, from the interior through the visible surface up into the outer atmosphere.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the solar interior outward to the corona._
        - Solar interior
        - Helioseismology, pulsations, and shock waves
        - Photosphere
        - Chromosphere
      - **Coronal structures are the distinct magnetically shaped formations in the solar corona, such as open-field holes and closed loops or streamers.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from open-field regions to closed-field formations._
        - Coronal holes
        - Coronal loops, streamers
        - Solar electromagnetic emission
        - Solar irradiance
        - Particle emission, solar wind (see also 94.30.vf—in Geophysics Appendix; 26.65.+t Solar neutrinos in nuclear astrophysics)
      - **Magnetic and eruptive activity covers solar magnetism and the transient events and cycles driven by it, including spots, flares, prominence eruptions and mass ejections.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the magnetic field and reconnection to the events it drives._
        - Electric and magnetic fields, solar magnetism
        - Magnetic reconnection
        - Solar activity (see also 92.70.Qr—in Global change)
        - Sun spots, solar cycles
        - Flares
      - **Coronal mass ejections, prominence eruptions, the corona and the transition region are the outer-atmosphere layers and eruptive events of the Sun, treated classically with scales set by gravity (G) and particle number (N) rather than by c or ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Coronal mass ejection
        - Prominence eruptions
        - Corona
        - Transition region
    - **Radiation and measurement claims have scales set by c and ħ through radiative and electromagnetic processes, or are largely empirical with c entering only through light travel.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from radiative physics to empirical population statistics._
      - **Claims about radiation, outflows and fields at stellar surfaces have scales set by c and ħ through radiative transfer, opacity and electromagnetic processes.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Mixed dependence: c and ħ dominate, with G entering only indirectly._
        - Stellar atmospheres (photospheres, chromospheres, coronae, magnetospheres); radiative transfer; opacity and line formation
        - Mass loss and stellar winds
        - Magnetic and electric fields; polarization of starlight
        - Stellar activity
      - **Claims about distances, motions and population statistics have scales that are largely empirical, with c entering only through light travel and measurement.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Least constant-bound: set by observation and statistics rather than by fundamental constants._
        - Distances, parallaxes
        - Proper motions and radial velocities (line-of-sight velocities); space motions (see also 95.10.Jk Astrometry and reference systems)
        - Luminosity and mass functions
        - Star counts, distribution, and statistics
    - **Observational probes, radiation sources, instruments and notices are the means of detecting and recording astronomical claims, with scales set by c and G or by no constants at all.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from probes and instruments to radiation sources and then to constant-free notices._
      - **Observational probes of galaxies use lensing, positions and statistical correlations to measure mass and luminosity properties.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from lensing to astrometry to statistical studies._
        - Gravitational lenses and luminous arcs (see also 95.30.Sf Relativity and gravitation—in fundamental aspects of astrophysics and section 04 General relativity and gravitation)
        - Astrometry; identification
        - Statistical and correlative studies of properties (luminosity and mass functions; mass-to-light ratio; Tully-Fisher relation, etc.)
      - **Radiation sources and backgrounds are objects and fields identified by the wavelength or particle type they emit, from radio to cosmic rays.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general heading through the electromagnetic bands to cosmic rays and backgrounds._
        - Unidentified sources of radiation outside the Solar System
        - Radio sources
        - IR sources (for IR sources in interstellar medium, see 98.38.Jw and/or 98.58.Jg)
        - X-ray sources; X-ray bursts (see also 97.30.Qt Novae, dwarf novae, recurrent novae, and other cataclysmic (eruptive) variables; 97.80.Jp X-ray binaries)
        - γ-ray sources; γ-ray bursts
      - **Instruments and broad fields of observation are scaled by c and G, since they detect light and gravitational waves from distant sources.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the general field of astronomy to the instruments used within it._
        - Gravitational wave detectors
        - Astronomy
        - Telescopes
        - Editorial note
        - Addenda
      - **Bibliographic corrections and notices carry no physical constants, so none of c, ħ, G or N sets the scale of their claims.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure of the publication notice, with the general category first and its specific notice types after._
        - Errata and other corrections
        - Errata
        - Publisher's note
        - Corrected article
        - Retraction
  - **Stellar claims concern individual stars, from their interiors and equilibrium stages to their pulsation and activity, with scales fixed by G, N, ħ and c through the star's structure.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure_
    - **Stellar structure claims concern stars in general, their interiors, collapse and oscillations, with scales that follow from G, N and ħ through the star's structure.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from stellar context to interiors, collapse and oscillations._
      - **Stellar context places the Sun among stars by covering stars in general and their characteristic properties.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the general class of stars to their specific properties._
        - Stars (for relativistic stars, see 04.40.Dg in general relativity and gravitation)
        - Stellar characteristics and properties (see also section 26 Nuclear astrophysics)
      - **Claims about stellar interiors and bulk properties have scales set jointly by G, ħ and N, as in the Chandrasekhar mass and the stellar mass-radius relation.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Most constant-bound: gravity, quantum degeneracy and particle number together fix the scale._
        - Stellar structure, interiors, evolution, nucleosynthesis, ages
        - Masses
        - Radii
        - Luminosities; magnitudes; effective temperatures, colors, and spectral classification
      - **Claims about gravitational collapse and infall have scales set mainly by G acting on the particle number N, with c limiting the speeds and ħ entering only through microphysics.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Strongly G-dominated dynamics, with c and ħ as secondary controls._
        - Star formation
        - Accretion and accretion disks
        - Circumstellar shells, clouds, and expanding envelopes; circumstellar masers (for interstellar masers, see 98.38.Er or 98.58.Ec)
        - Stellar rotation
      - **Claims about stellar oscillations, surface structure and composition have scales that follow from G and N through the star's structure, with little direct dependence on c or ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Weakly constant-bound: the scale is inherited from the structure fixed by G and N._
        - Pulsations, oscillations, and stellar seismology
        - Surface features (including starspots)
        - Abundances, chemical composition
        - Hertzsprung-Russell, color-magnitude, and color-color diagrams
    - **Single stars and stellar remnants are classed by the stage of equilibrium that fixes their scale, from nucleon number N in fusing stars to quantum degeneracy ħ and relativity c in compact objects.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from main-sequence stars through evolved and degenerate objects to compact remnants._
      - **Hydrogen-burning main-sequence stars are supported by core fusion, with gravity balanced against gas and radiation pressure and a scale set by the number of nucleons N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general class to hot, intermediate and cool main-sequence stars._
        - Normal stars (by class): general or individual
        - Main-sequence: early-type stars (O and B)
        - Main-sequence: intermediate-type stars (A and F)
        - Main-sequence: late-type stars (G, K, and M)
      - **Evolved luminous stars are stars that have left the main sequence and developed extended envelopes.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from giants to supergiants to carbon-rich types._
        - Giant and subgiant stars
        - Supergiant stars
        - Carbon stars, S stars, and related types (C, S, R, and N)
      - **Degenerate and sub-stellar objects are held up by quantum degeneracy pressure or lie at the edge of stellar formation, so that ħ enters their scale.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from white dwarfs to low-mass objects to the earliest stars._
        - Faint blue stars (including blue stragglers), white dwarfs, degenerate stars, nuclei of planetary nebulae (for planetary nebulae, see 98.38.Ly or 98.58.Li)
        - Low luminosity stars, subdwarfs, and brown dwarfs
        - Population III stars
      - **Compact remnants of stellar death are objects whose scale is set by gravity, relativity and quantum degeneracy, that is by G, c and ħ together.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the general late-stage category to specific remnant types._
        - Late stages of stellar evolution (including black holes)
        - Supernovae (see also 26.30.-k Nucleosynthesis in novae, supernovae, and other explosive stars; for nuclear physics aspects of supernovae evolution, see 26.50.+x)
        - Pulsars
        - Neutron stars (see also 26.60.-c Nuclear matter aspects of neutron stars in—Nuclear physics)
        - Black holes (see also 04.70.-s Physics of black holes in—General relativity and gravitation; for galactic black holes, see 98.35.Jk and 98.62.Js)
    - **Variable and active stars are stars whose brightness changes or surface activity arise from pulsation tied to G and density or from mass loss, magnetic fields and outbursts.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from regular pulsation to irregular eruptive activity._
      - **Pulsating variable stars change in brightness through radial or non-radial oscillation whose period follows the free-fall time set by G and density.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from low-amplitude blue variables to Cepheids, Miras and RR Lyrae stars, with Population II stars last._
        - Population II stars (horizontal branch, metal poor, etc.)
        - Low-amplitude blue variables (alpha Cygni, beta Cephei, delta Scuti, delta Delphini, delta Canis Majoris, SX Phoenicius, etc.)
        - Cepheids (delta Cephei, W Virginis)
        - Long-period variables (Miras) and semiregulars
        - RR Lyrae stars; RV Tauri and PV Telescopii variables
      - **Eruptive, emission and peculiar stars show activity or anomalies driven by mass loss, magnetic fields or surface outbursts.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from emission-line and peculiar stars to flare stars and novae._
        - Variable and peculiar stars (including novae)
        - Emission-line stars (Of, Be, Luminous Blue Variables, Wolf-Rayet, etc.)
        - Chemically peculiar stars (Ap, Am, etc.)
        - Flare stars (UV Ceti, RS Canum Venaticorum, FU Orionis, R Coronae Borealis variables, etc.)
        - Novae, dwarf novae, recurrent novae, and other cataclysmic (eruptive) variables (see also 97.80.Gm, Jp Cataclysmic binaries and X-ray binaries)
  - **Stellar system claims concern bound groupings of stars, from binary and planetary systems to stellar assemblies and the Milky Way's content, with scales set by G, c and the number N of stars.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure_
    - **Binary, interacting and planetary systems are small gravitationally bound systems whose scale is set by G with masses and by light at speed c.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from simple binaries through interacting systems to planetary systems._
      - **Binary stars classified by observational method are systems whose orbital scale is set by Newtonian gravity G and the stellar masses.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the general binary category to specific detection methods._
        - Binary and multiple stars
        - Astrometric and interferometric binaries
        - Visual binaries
        - Spectroscopic binaries; close binaries
        - Eclipsing binaries
      - **Interacting, accreting and multiple stellar systems are those where gravity G together with radiation at speed c governs mass transfer and dynamics.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from peculiar variables to interacting binaries and multiples._
        - Unusual and peculiar variables
        - Cataclysmic binaries (novae, dwarf novae, recurrent novae, and nova-like objects); symbiotic stars (see also 97.30.Qt Novae)
        - X-ray binaries (see also 98.70.Qy X-ray sources and 97.60.Gb Pulsars)
        - Multiple stars
      - **Extrasolar planetary systems are small bodies and disks whose scale is set by gravity G and by detection through light at speed c.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the general category to detection, planets and disks._
        - Extrasolar planetary systems
        - Photometric and spectroscopic detection; coronographic detection; interferometric detection
        - Substellar companions; planets
        - Infrared excess; debris disks; protoplanetary disks; exo-zodiacal dust
    - **Stellar assemblies are large groups of stars whose scale is set by gravity G acting on a number N of stars.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the largest systems down to loose associations, open clusters and the Galaxy._
      - **Large stellar assemblies and the Universe at large are systems whose scale is set by gravity G acting on a huge number N of stars.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the whole Universe down to clusters._
        - Stellar systems; interstellar medium; galactic and extragalactic objects and systems; the Universe
        - Stellar clusters and associations
      - **Stellar associations are loose, young groups of stars whose gravitational binding is weak, so their scale is set by G and the number N of stars.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the Milky Way to external galaxies._
        - Associations of stars (OB, T, R) in the Milky Way
        - Associations of stars (OB, T, R) in external galaxies
      - **Open clusters are moderately bound groups of young stars whose size is set by G and the number N of member stars.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the Milky Way to external galaxies._
        - Open clusters in the Milky Way
        - Open clusters in external galaxies
        - Globular clusters in the Milky Way
        - Globular clusters in external galaxies
      - **Galactic mass, motion and structure entries describe the Milky Way's gravitational dynamics, whose scale is set by G and the total stellar number N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from global properties to dynamics and large-scale components._
        - Characteristics and properties of the Milky Way galaxy
        - Mass and mass distribution
        - Kinematics, dynamics, and rotation
        - Galactic halo
        - Spiral arms and galactic disk
    - **Galactic content topics for the Milky Way are framed by how G, N, ħ and c together set the scale of its stars, gas and fields.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the overall Milky Way framing to its further topics._
      - **Galactic content and processes cover the stars, gas, fields and flows of the Milky Way, where ħ and c enter through atomic physics, radiation and magnetism as well as G.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from the center outward to the surrounding medium._
        - Electric and magnetic fields
        - Galactic center, bar, circumnuclear matter, and bulge (including black hole and distance measurements)
        - Stellar content and populations; morphology and overall structure
        - Infall and accretion
        - Galactic winds and fountains
      - **Further topics in Milky Way astrophysics, from the solar neighborhood and interstellar medium to galactic formation and chemical evolution, whose scales are set by gravity and large particle numbers (G and N) rather than by quantum or relativistic constants.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Solar neighborhood
        - Interstellar medium (ISM) and nebulae in Milky Way
        - Origin, formation, evolution, age, and star formation
        - Chemical composition and chemical evolution
      - **Claims about irregular and dwarf galaxies and about quasars and active nuclei are scaled by G and c, where relativistic gravity near compact masses sets the energetics.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by degree of relativistic compactness, from irregular and dwarf systems to active nuclei._
        - Irregular and morphologically peculiar galaxies
        - Dwarf galaxies (elliptical, irregular, and spheroidal)
        - Quasars; active or peculiar galaxies, objects, and systems
      - **Powerful active nuclei and the galaxies that host them form a class of galaxies whose output is dominated by accretion onto a central black hole.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the most luminous and distant nuclear sources (quasars) to the more modest active galaxies and radio galaxies._
        - Quasars (for quasar absorption and emission-line systems; Lyman forest, see 98.62.Ra)
        - Active and peculiar galaxies and related systems (including BL Lacertae objects, blazars, Seyfert galaxies, Markarian galaxies, and active galactic nuclei)
        - Radio galaxies
        - Protogalaxies; primordial galaxies
        - Starburst galaxies and infrared excess galaxies
    - **Normal galaxies are classified by morphology and by gravity acting on enormous numbers of stars, with little role for ħ.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general classification to the nearest galactic system._
      - **Claims about the classification and morphology of normal galaxies are scaled by gravity (G) acting on enormous numbers N of stars, with little role for ħ.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked by degree of organized rotational structure, from classification schemes through ellipticals and lenticulars to spirals._
        - Normal galaxies; extragalactic objects and systems (by type)
        - Classification and classification systems
        - Elliptical galaxies
        - Lenticular (S0) galaxies
        - Spiral galaxies
      - **Normal galaxies of the Local Group are classified by morphology, as elliptical, spiral, irregular or dwarf systems.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the largest, most massive galaxy types to small dwarf and irregular systems._
        - Elliptical galaxies
        - Spiral galaxies (M31 and M33)
        - Magellanic Clouds and other irregular galaxies
        - Dwarf galaxies (elliptical, irregular, and spheroidal)
      - **The Local Group and its Magellanic neighbours are the nearest galactic system, including the gas stream torn from the Clouds.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from the whole Local Group down to the Magellanic stream trailing the Clouds._
        - Local group; Magellanic Clouds
        - Magellanic stream
    - **Galaxy formation, matter flows, evolution and dynamical modelling are processes that unfold over time and are scaled by G and N, with c, ħ entering in dynamics.**
      _basis: cause_effect, order: time. Ordered in time from formation and inflow through evolution to modelled dynamics._
      - **Galaxy-wide formation and dynamical properties describe how whole galaxies originate, evolve, gain mass, move and carry fields.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from origin and composition to mass, motion and fields._
        - Characteristics and properties of external galaxies and extragalactic objects (for the Milky Way, see 98.35.-a)
        - Masses and mass distribution
        - Kinematics, dynamics, and rotation
        - Electric and magnetic fields
      - **Matter flows into and out of galaxies through accretion, infall, jets, bursts and galactic winds.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from inflow to outflow._
        - Infall, accretion, and accretion disks
        - Jets and bursts; galactic winds and fountains
        - Distances, redshifts, radial velocities; spatial distribution of galaxies (for observational cosmology, see 98.80.Es)
        - Magnitudes and colors; luminosities
        - Intergalactic matter; quasar absorption and emission-line systems; Lyman forest (for quasars, see 98.54.Aj; for intracluster matter, see 98.65.Hb)
      - **Claims about how galaxies and their stars originate, form and evolve, including how their chemical makeup changes over cosmic time, whose scale is set by gravity (G) and the number of particles involved (N) rather than by quantum or relativistic constants.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Origin, formation, evolution, age, and star formation
        - Chemical composition and chemical evolution
      - **Dynamic astrophysical processes and their modelling are scaled by G and c, with ħ and N entering through nuclear and many-body physics.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from explosive events to formation processes and the simulations that model them._
        - Transient & explosive astronomical phenomena
        - Planets & planetary systems
        - Formation & evolution of stars & galaxies
        - Numerical simulations in gravitation & astrophysics
  - **Galactic and cosmological claims concern interstellar gas, galaxies, their structures and evolution, and the whole Universe, with scales set chiefly by G and the number N of constituents.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure_
    - **Interstellar gas claims are grouped by the phase of the gas and by the atomic, radiative and gravitational constants that scale each phase.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from general gas properties to cold and then hot gas._
      - **Claims about the general physical state of the interstellar gas, its microphysics and its grains, are set by atomic constants such as ħ together with c in radiation and magnetic processes.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from bulk gas properties to atomic processes, grains and infrared emission._
        - Physical properties (abundances, electron density, magnetic fields, scintillation, scattering, kinematics, dynamics, turbulence, etc.)
        - Atomic, molecular, chemical, and grain processes
        - Interstellar dust grains; diffuse emission; infrared cirrus
        - Infrared emission
      - **Claims about cold, dense molecular and neutral gas and its masers and outflows are scaled by quantum line transitions (ħ) and by gravity (G) acting on large particle numbers N.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from diffuse neutral gas to dense clouds, masers and jets._
        - Molecular clouds, H 2 clouds, dense clouds, and dark clouds
        - Interstellar masers (for circumstellar masers, see 97.10.Fy)
        - Jets, outflows, and bipolar flows (for pre-main sequence objects, see 97.21.+a)
        - H I regions and 21-cm lines; diffuse, translucent, and high-velocity clouds
      - **Claims about ionized, hot and shocked gas are scaled by radiation and plasma physics, where c and ħ set photoionization and emission and N sets the particle count.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from ionized nebulae to hot gas and stellar-death remnants._
        - H II regions; emission and reflection nebulae
        - Intercloud medium (ICM); hot and highly ionized gas; bubbles
        - Planetary nebulae (for nuclei of planetary nebulae, see also 97.20.Rp)
        - Supernova remnants
      - **Interstellar emission regions and remnants are gas structures and radiation sources within galaxies, such as nebulae, hot bubbles and supernova remnants.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from radiation to gas phases, nebulae, remnants and tidal features._
        - Infrared emission
        - Intercloud medium (ICM); hot and highly ionized gas; bubbles
        - Planetary nebulae (for nuclei of planetary nebulae, see also 97.20.Rp)
        - Supernova remnants
        - Tidal tails; H I shells
    - **This group gathers claims about the interstellar medium, from the kinds of gas, dust, clouds and flows in external galaxies to phenomena such as masers and outflows, whose scale is set by classical constants like G and c rather than by quantum scales.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure_
      - **The interstellar medium of external galaxies is classified by the kind of gas, dust, cloud or flow, ranging from general properties to specific nebulae and clouds.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Ranked from general physical and chemical properties to specific dust, cloud, maser, jet, H I and H II components._
        - Interstellar medium (ISM) and nebulae in external galaxies
        - Atomic, molecular, chemical, and grain processes
        - Molecular clouds, H 2 clouds, dense clouds, and dark clouds
        - H I regions and 21-cm lines; diffuse, translucent, and high-velocity clouds
        - H II regions; emission and reflection nebulae
      - **Interstellar-medium phenomena whose scale is set by classical astrophysical constants such as G and c rather than quantum scales, covering gas properties, dust, masers and outflows.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure_
        - Physical properties (abundances, electron density, magnetic fields, scintillation, scattering, kinematics, dynamics, turbulence, etc.)
        - Interstellar dust grains; diffuse emission; infrared cirrus
        - Interstellar masers (for circumstellar masers, see 97.10.Fy)
        - Jets, outflows and bipolar flows (for pre-main sequence objects, see 97.21.+a)
    - **Galactic components, small galaxy systems, large-scale structures and bound stellar systems are the structures whose scale is set by G and the number N of their constituents.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the parts of a single galaxy to groups, clusters and large bound systems._
      - **Galactic components and populations are the structural parts of a galaxy, such as its halo, disk, nucleus and stellar content.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from outer halo to disk, nucleus and stellar content._
        - Galactic halos
        - Spiral arms and bars; galactic disks
        - Galactic nuclei (including black holes), circumnuclear matter, and bulges
        - Stellar content and populations; radii; morphology and overall structure
      - **Small galaxy systems are interacting, merging or compact groupings of a few galaxies bound by mutual gravity.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from pairs and triples to compact groups and mergers._
        - Interacting galaxies; galaxy pairs, and triples
        - Small and compact galaxy groups
        - Galaxy mergers, collisions, and tidal interactions
      - **Large-scale galaxy structures are rich clusters, their intracluster gas, and superclusters spanning the largest observed scales.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general heading to clusters, intracluster matter and superclusters._
        - Galaxy groups, clusters, and superclusters; large scale structure of the Universe
        - Galaxy clusters
        - Intracluster matter; cooling flows
        - Superclusters; large-scale structure of the Universe (including voids, pancakes, great wall, etc.)
      - **Large gravitationally bound systems of stars and galaxies are scaled by G together with the very large number N of their constituents.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from galaxies down to the stars they contain._
        - Galaxies
        - Stars
    - **Cosmology topics concern the whole Universe and its relics, with scales set by G and c, N for element abundances, and ħ for quantum cosmology.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered structurally from the general cosmology topic to the constants that set its claims._
      - **Cosmology topics concern the Universe as a whole, including its origin and early-Universe particle and field models, plus cosmic rays and background radiations as relics.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure from the general cosmology heading to origin and early-Universe models._
        - Cosmic rays (including sources, origin, acceleration, and interactions) (see also 26.40.+r Cosmic ray nucleosynthesis—in Nuclear astrophysics)
        - Background radiations
        - Cosmology (see also section 04 General relativity and gravitation; for origin and evolution of galaxies, see 98.62.Ai; for elementary particle and nuclear processes, see 95.30.Cq; for dark matter, see 95.35.+d; for dark energy, see 95.36.+x; for superclusters and large-scale structure of the Universe, see 98.65.Dx)
        - Origin and formation of the Universe
        - Particle-theory and field-theory models of the early Universe (including cosmic pancakes, cosmic strings, chaotic phenomena, inflationary universe, etc.)
      - **Cosmological claims about the whole Universe are set by G and c through gravitation and light travel, with N entering as the abundance of the elements, and with ħ added only for quantum cosmology.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from G and c scales of expansion and relativity to the ħ scale of quantum cosmology._
        - Observational cosmology (including Hubble constant, distance scale, cosmological constant, early Universe, etc)
        - Origin, formation, and abundances of the elements (see also 26.35.+c Big Bang nucleosynthesis—in Nuclear astrophysics)
        - Mathematical and relativistic aspects of cosmology
        - Quantum cosmology (see also 04.60.-m Quantum gravity—in General relativity and gravitation)

### 4. Matter in equilibrium is the regime where particle number N and thermal energy set the scale of settled phases, covering condensed matter, surfaces, soft matter and energy materials.

- **States and structure of matter cover liquids, solids, phase transitions, many-body and quantum-fluid systems.**
  _basis: kinds, order: structure. From liquids and solids to transitions and many-body states._
  - **Liquid and liquid-crystal topics are kinds of study of fluid and partially ordered matter, from general theory of liquid structure to specific liquid types and liquid crystals.**
    _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from general liquid theory to specific liquids to liquid crystals._
    - **Theoretical and general treatments of liquid structure are methods and properties that apply across liquid types and depend on scattering theory and particle number.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed from formal theory to general methods and properties._
      - **Theoretical treatments and liquid-structure descriptions depend on the formal scattering theory and on the particle number N of the many-body system.**
        _basis: kinds, order: degree. Dependence on N becomes dominant, as the claim concerns statistical many-body structure rather than a single probe._
        - Theories of x-ray diffraction and scattering
        - Theories of neutron diffraction and scattering
        - Theories of electron diffraction and scattering
        - Structure of liquids
        - Theory and models of liquid structure
      - **General methods and properties for studying liquid structure cover simulation, relaxation dynamics, and the structure of simple and associated liquids.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: general approaches before specific liquids._
        - Computer simulation of liquid structure
        - Time-dependent properties; relaxation (for glass transitions, see 64.70.P-)
        - Structure of simple liquids
        - Structure of associated liquids: electrolytes, molten salts, etc
    - **Specific liquid types are studied individually, ranging from simple atomic and molecular liquids to polymeric liquid systems.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Listed from simple liquids to polymeric liquids._
      - **Simple atomic and molecular liquids are specific liquid types, such as noble gases, molecules, metals and alloys, whose structures are studied individually.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: umbrella heading for specific liquid structures first, then individual liquid types._
        - Studies of specific liquid structures
        - Liquid noble gases
        - Molecular liquids
        - Liquid metals and alloys
      - **Polymeric liquid systems include macromolecular solutions, polymer melts and blends, and swelling and cross-linked polymer networks.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: umbrella heading first, then its subtypes._
        - Macromolecular and polymers solutions; polymer melts
        - Polymer solutions
        - Polymer melts and blends
        - Polymer swelling, cross linking
    - **Liquid crystal topics are the parts of the study of partially ordered soft phases: theory and measurement, order and defects, and confined or polymeric systems.**
      _basis: parts, order: structure. Ordered from general models, to ordering and defects, to specific system types._
      - **Theories and measurements of liquid crystal structure comprise molecular and continuum models together with experimental determinations of the phases.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: umbrella heading, then models, then experiments._
        - Liquid crystals (for phase transitions in liquid crystals, see 64.70.M-; for liquid crystals as dielectric materials, see 77.84.Nh; for liquid crystals as optical materials, see 42.70.Df; for liquid crystal devices, see 42.79.Kr)
        - Molecular and microscopic models and theories of liquid crystal structure
        - Continuum models and theories of liquid crystal structure
        - Experimental determinations of smectic, nematic, cholesteric, and other structures
      - **Order, surface effects, and defects in liquid crystals concern orientational ordering, alignment at surfaces, defects, and defect-based blue phases.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: ordering first, then surfaces, then defects._
        - Orientational order of liquid crystals; electric and magnetic field effects on order
        - Surface phenomena: alignment, anchoring, anchoring transitions, surface-induced layering, surface-induced ordering, wetting, prewetting transitions, and wetting transitions
        - Defects in liquid crystals
        - Blue phases and other defect-phases
      - **Liquid-crystal systems are partially ordered soft phases whose structure depends on confinement, solvent, or polymer character.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure, from confined to solvent-based to polymeric liquid crystals._
        - Microconfined liquid crystals: droplets, cylinders, randomly confined liquid crystals, polymer dispersed liquid crystals, and porous systems
        - Lyotropic phases
        - Polymer liquid crystals
        - Nanocrystals
        - Nanotubes
    - **Crystal structure knowledge has two parts: general principles common to all crystals and the specific solids classified by chemical makeup.**
      _basis: parts, order: structure. General principles come first, then specific materials._
      - **General principles of bulk crystal structure cover the symmetry, binding, stoichiometry and transformation behaviour common to all crystals.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: from the general field to theory, transformations, binding and composition._
        - Structure of bulk crystals
        - Theory of crystal structure, crystal symmetry; calculations and modeling
        - Crystallographic aspects of phase transformations; pressure effects (see also 81.30.Hd in materials science)
        - Crystal binding; cohesive energy
        - Crystal stoichiometry
      - **Specific crystalline solids are kinds of materials classified by their chemical makeup, from pure elements to organic compounds.**
        _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered by structure: general heading, then elements, alloys, inorganic and organic compounds._
        - Structure of specific crystalline solids (for surface structure, see 68.35.B-)
        - Elemental solids
        - Alloys
        - Inorganic compounds
        - Organic compounds
    - **Crystal defect subject matter is divided into kinds by defect size, foreign atoms and host material.**
      _basis: kinds, order: structure. Ordered from point defects and extended defects to impurities and host materials._
